# Changelog

All notable changes to this project are documented in this file.

## [Unreleased]

### Added — `s6-policy-residuals-112-124` (issues 112, 124): a policy layer can declare its baseline posture, and the printer test bounds rejection messages

CRITICAL tier (sensitive area: policy delivery/config surface). Issue #107 is recorded as a ratified residual (no code); Issues #112 and #124 are addressed.

- **#112, `defaultOutcome` in the policy format.** A rule set may carry an optional top-level `defaultOutcome` (allow or deny). A bad value is rejected with the field's own enum error. It resolves through the existing trust rank (`src/policy/rule/precedence.ts`): a central-declared posture cannot be relaxed by shipped-defaults or project; a lower-trust layer may only tighten toward deny; equal-or-higher trust overrides; a layer voided by a mandatory-id collision contributes nothing. The loader (`src/policy/config/loader.ts`) exposes the result as `LoadSuccess.defaultOutcome` (`outcome` plus `source`), falling back to the bootstrap allow when no layer declares one. Result field only: printer stdout, the kernel-gate hook, the bootstrap ruleset, `kernel.ts` and the pin are byte-unchanged; `rule-types.ts` gained one type-only optional field.
- **#124, test-only.** `printer.test.ts`'s rejection helper takes a required per-site bound instead of a wildcard, so a "helpful diagnostics" change that dumps the offending policy file into a fail-closed message is caught again. Zero production diff.
- **Tests.** Written first by test-writer and confirmed red at HEAD (32 red, 112 pre-existing green) before the build; green after it. Seven mutants (relax allowed, tighten disabled, voided layers counted, fallback flipped, key unregistered, layer posture dropped, no enum check) each turn a non-empty set of those tests red and are restored.
- **Post-build fix, Issue #291.** The first #124 bound only caught a whole-file echo; a partial dump (the first bytes of the offending file) passed. test-writer replaced it with an exact-tail equality check on the rejection message, proven by the new `ISSUE-123(c)` test (commit 9fc9f55). `printer.test.ts` now has 14 tests, including `ISSUE-123(b)` and `ISSUE-123(c)`.
- **Reviews.** `red-team` go (two MED); `app-security-reviewer` and `cross-domain-reviewer` APPROVE-WITH-CONDITIONS. red-team's trust matrix ran 243 combinations through the real loader with zero relax violations. Both MED findings are handled: #291 is fixed, and #292 (no operator surface for the posture) is ruled a bound deferral, with Issue #288 now `severity:med`, milestone S6, and carrying a before-the-hook-goes-live precondition.
- **Rulings.** Implicit lock by trust rank rather than an explicit mandatory flag (a deviation from red-team's proof-test wording, recorded in `docs/decisions.md`). Accepted, disclosed properties: peers can relax each other's posture when central is absent, and a lower-trust layer may tighten toward deny. Enforcement of the posture is tracked by Issues #288 and #93 (the hook is unwired). Issue #107 stays open as a ratified residual.
### Fixed — `s1-oss01-residuals-270-271` (issues 270, 271): the high-byte sweep derives its scope and position, and the scan-timeout grant no longer depends on which pattern was slow

CRITICAL tier (secret-scanning sensitive area; ratified by the Manager). Two non-blocking MEDs `red-team` filed in round 2 of `s1-oss01-detection-residuals` (`docs/reviews/s1-oss01-detection-residuals-red-team-round2-2026-09-22.md`).

- **#270, test-only.** The 0xA0 sweep (`oss01-no-value-class-however-written-hides-a-high-byte`) hand-kept two things: which patterns it swept and the one offset where it put the byte. Now a registry in `patterns.test.ts` must classify every id in `SECRET_PATTERNS` (free-form with an exemplar, or ascii-only with a reason), checked in both directions, and the byte replaces each character of the value in turn. Named controls show a first-character-only narrowing, a last-character-only narrowing and an unclassified new pattern each fail; the old single-offset probe passes the first two, asserted inside the control. The free-form or ascii-only classification stays a declared judgment, stated in the test header. Counts come from the run (`t.diagnostic`).
- **#271, `history-scan.ts` and `allowlist-tool.ts`.** The `oss01-scan-timeout` grant hash is now a function of the scanned text alone (`scanTimeoutHash`), not of which pattern was slow, and `allowlist-tool hash` computes it directly with no scan, so a developer's local run prints the hash CI needs on any machine, even where nothing times out. New tests: `oss01-a-scan-timeout-grant-does-not-depend-on-which-pattern-was-slow`, `oss01-scan-timeout-unlock-hash-does-not-depend-on-this-machines-speed`. One existing expectation was replaced, not deleted: `sb2-hash-lines-computes-the-scan-timeout-pattern-id-without-throwing` no longer expects an empty result for ordinary text.
- **Ratified residual, not fixed.** Whether a blob near the 500ms bound times out at all is still a wall-clock race (and a small blob can time out under CPU contention), so the gate can occasionally block falsely, never pass falsely. THOTH-ADR-0002's register row now says so. A deterministic matcher is the only complete remedy; deferred to Issue 287.
- **Stage-3 fix-now, #290 (test-only).** An `ascii-only` declaration in the sweep registry was an unchecked opt-out: a mis-declared new pattern hid every high byte with the suite green. `oss01-an-ascii-only-declaration-is-mechanically-consistent-with-its-pattern` now rebuilds each bracketed class of an `ascii-only` pattern and rejects one that accepts a byte in 0x80-0xFF, or an unbracketed wildcard. It passes on today's catalog and goes red on a widened class, on a mis-declared new pattern and on a free-form pattern re-declared `ascii-only` (real `patterns.ts` mutated and reverted). The test header states what stays a declared judgment: the reason text, a free-form exemplar's value span, the whitespace escape used as a separator, and lookarounds.
- **Stage-3 fix-now, #289 (accepted widening, boundary pinned).** With the pattern id out of the hash, one scan-timeout grant covers every pattern's timeout on that blob, and a timed-out pattern's matches are not reported, so a granted blob passes even where a slow pattern skipped it. The Manager accepted this; THOTH-ADR-0002's register row now says it plainly, and `oss01-a-scan-timeout-grant-covers-every-patterns-timeout-on-that-blob-and-nothing-more` pins the boundary against the real gate: the same text at another path blocks, a different text at the same path blocks, a real pattern's match is never exempted, a real pattern id carrying the timeout hash exempts nothing, and a passing run still prints one `ALLOWLISTED` line per skipped pattern. Removing the path or the pattern id from the join, or restoring the pattern id to the hash, turns it red.
- **Untouched.** `SCAN_TIMEOUT_MS`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, the allowlist file and loader, `patterns.ts`. No allowlist entry uses the reserved id, so no grant changes.
- **Checks.** `npm run typecheck` and `npm run lint` clean; `npm test` 1098 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (after the Stage-3 fix-now round); `npm run oss:secret-scan` 0 blocking; `qa:reference-resolver` and `qa:completeness-claims` pass. Mutating the real password pattern (first-only, last-only, positive-class narrowing) or adding an unclassified pattern turns the named sweep tests red; the two new `#271` tests and the one edited test were red before the code change.

### Fixed — `qa14-ci-red-citation-wording` (PR 281): three comment wording defects that kept master's QA-14 gate red

CRITICAL tier (sensitive area: the two SUR-03 hook files, comment-only diff). Master's QA-14 (`reference-resolver.ts`) gate had been failing since this session's four earlier merges landed — ten citations it could not resolve. Three were plain wording defects in live hook comments, not review-report content, so fixable without touching an immutable report (PRINCIPLES.md rule 11):

- `hooks/sessionstart-tool-enum.mjs` named an upstream anthropics/claude-code feature-request issue in a shape the resolver's cross-repo-issue detector always treats as blocking; reworded to name the same issue without that shape.
- The same file quoted the gitignored per-user Claude settings path inside backticks, which the resolver's path-citation detector then tried, and failed, to resolve against this repository's own tree; the backticks around that one fragment were dropped.
- `hooks/userpromptsubmit-halt-relay.mjs` quoted a slash-joined shorthand for the two Node stream-write methods inside backticks, tripping the same path-citation detector; split into two separately backtick-quoted, individually valid method names — a documentation-precision improvement in its own right, independently confirmed by every reviewer.

Zero behavior change — comment text only, independently verified byte-for-byte identical outside the three edited lines. The remaining seven citations that kept (and keep) the gate red live entirely inside this session's own already-committed, immutable dated review reports and a `docs/REVIEW_LOG.md` row; they cannot be edited and are tracked as a dedicated follow-up issue rather than patched here.

- **Reviews.** CRITICAL tier: `red-team` go (two MED findings, neither gating — a maintainability note about undocumented style choices in these same comments, and a caution that any future close-out writing about this exact fix must avoid reproducing the three flagged shapes verbatim, or it re-reds the gate it just fixed), `app-security-reviewer` APPROVE (zero findings — confirmed the SUR-03 hardening landed in the prior story is byte-for-byte untouched), `cross-domain-reviewer` APPROVE-WITH-CONDITIONS (one HIGH finding entirely outside this diff's own files, surfaced while verifying it: the repository's default branch currently has no branch-protection rule, so a required CI check failing at merge time has not actually blocked any of this session's four prior merges — filed as its own issue and routed to the human as a repository-settings decision, explicitly not a condition on this change).
- **Checks.** `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`, `npm test` (1090 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped) and `npm run oss:secret-scan` (0 blocking) all pass.
- **Post-review CI history on this PR, for the record.** First push: real, unrelated CI failure (rerun came back green on the identical commit) — dispatched `debugger`, which reproduced the actual mechanism against real production code: GitHub's 2-vCPU runners can starve the main thread long enough that an OSS-01 scan's intentional wall-clock timeout ceiling fires on trivial regex work, silently swapping a genuine match for a timeout finding in one pre-existing, unrelated test. No code fix — touching that ceiling to quiet a rare, self-healing race would be disproportionate; a dated report explains the mechanism for next time. That report itself quoted the test's own synthetic fixture literal as evidence, triggering the same dogfood pattern this project has hit before — one allowlist entry added, then its own required companion entry in `history-scan.test.ts`'s `REVIEWED_BASELINE` map (the mechanism that stops a credential grant from silently covering a *different*, unreviewed occurrence later).

### Fixed — `s1-238-unlock-command-cquote` (issue 238): the `hash` unlock command now resolves a path git C-quotes

STANDARD tier (proposed by the implementer, ratified by the Manager; scope `s1-238-unlock-command-cquote`). Secret-scanning sensitive area, but a print-formatting/CLI-input fix with no change to match or detection logic. No `test-writer` dispatch: an internal CLI hint string, no UI flow or API surface.

- **Before.** `git ls-tree` C-quotes a tracked path holding a non-ASCII byte or a literal quote/backslash (e.g. `café.txt` prints as `"caf\303\251.txt"`), and `src/lib/git.ts`'s `lsTree()` keys its Map by that exact raw spelling. `allowlist-tool.ts hash <commit> <path> <patternId>` matched ONLY that raw key, so typing the plain filename a maintainer actually sees exited non-zero (`<path> is not in <commit>`), forcing a hand-computed hash.
- **After.** A new `decodeGitQuotedPath` (`src/lib/git.ts`) undoes git's own C-quoting; `hash`'s lookup now falls back to matching a tree entry by its decoded spelling when the direct key misses, so either the plain filename or git's own raw quoted form resolves to the same blob. `lsTree()`'s own keys, the allowlist's `path` field semantics (THOTH-ADR-0002) and everything `unlockDetails` prints are unchanged — verified: the raw C-quoted form always contains a backslash, which `history-scan.ts`'s `SHELL_SAFE` set already excludes, so no C-quoted path has ever produced (or now produces) a runnable `HASH-COMMAND` line; the #239 shell-safety gate and its full test suite (`history-scan.test.ts`, 77 tests) pass unmodified.
- **Tests, real git.** `src/lib/git.test.ts` adds unit coverage for `decodeGitQuotedPath` (plain passthrough, octal-byte escapes, named C-escapes, "never guess" on an unrecognized escape) plus a round-trip test against a real `git ls-tree` line for a `café.txt` fixture. `src/secret-scan/allowlist-tool.test.ts` adds an end-to-end regression against a real git-plumbed non-ASCII fixture, with a positive control proving the raw spelling really is C-quoted on this platform, and asserting the plain and raw spellings both resolve and print the identical hash line.
- **Checks.** `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`, `npm test` (1049 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped) and `npm run oss:secret-scan` (0 blocking, 2031 allowlisted) all pass.
### Fixed — `s5-halt-mechanism-hardening` (issues 96, 206, 274, 276, 277, 278): the SUR-03 halt mechanism's env-var fallback, cross-session guard, and message-forgery defenses

CRITICAL tier (proposed by the implementer, ratified by the Manager; scope `s5-halt-mechanism-hardening`). All issues touch CLAUDE.md's "Policy enforcement / session gates" sensitive area (`hooks/sessionstart-tool-enum.mjs`, `hooks/userpromptsubmit-halt-relay.mjs`), bundled because they land in the same two files. No `test-writer` dispatch: hook-internals hardening, no new/changed UI flow or API surface — covered by the implementer's own regression tests, consistent with this story's established precedent (`-fixnow`/`-friendly-labels` sibling test files).

**Round 1 (this entry's original text, corrected below) claimed Issue #96 and #206's still-open half were both closed. A CRITICAL-tier round-1 review (`red-team` no-go, `app-security-reviewer` REWORK, `cross-domain-reviewer` APPROVE-WITH-CONDITIONS) found the #96 fix was a complete no-op and #206's fix had four demonstrated bypasses plus an unsanitized second render path. Round 2 fixed all of it, but round-2 review (`red-team` go with a named MED, `app-security-reviewer` REWORK, `cross-domain-reviewer` APPROVE) found round 2's own "structural" unlock-token fix was still a text-matching defense, one level up from round 1's — a zero-width character or homoglyph spliced into "unlock:" defeats it, and the round-2 regression oracle was byte-identical to the defense's own regex (a tautology). Round 3 (this entry, current text) replaces the whole detection-based approach with a position-based one — the paragraphs below replace, not append to, the earlier narration, per this repo's own "an entry's factual content stays true" convention (the wrong/incomplete claims are corrected here rather than left standing under a "later found wrong" footnote, since no Issue was ever closed on a wrong claim — Issues #96 and #206 stayed open throughout until genuinely fixed).**

- **Issue #96 (fixed — spike genuinely resolved this time).** When `hooks/sessionstart-tool-enum.mjs`'s stdin failed to parse, `session_id` fell back to the literal `"unknown-session"`, and the resulting halt-state write landed in `unknown-session.json` — a file `hooks/userpromptsubmit-halt-relay.mjs` never reads for a real session, so the session proceeded unblocked, a fail-open gap in a mechanism whose whole point is fail-closed. **Round 1's fix read `process.env.CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` and cited code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks as confirming it exists — both the name and the citation were wrong.** `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` does not exist as a real environment variable on this runtime; it is only ever a text-template placeholder Claude Code substitutes into prompt/skill text, never a real `process.env` key on a hook subprocess. Round 1's fix was therefore a complete no-op, demonstrated end-to-end by `red-team` and `app-security-reviewer` independently. **The correct variable, `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, is confirmed by measurement, not documentation prose:** a fresh `claude -p` session, launched in a throwaway scratch directory, with a diagnostic SessionStart hook that dumped its own `process.env` to a file, run once, output read directly — `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` was present, set to that session's own distinct id. This is the spike PRINCIPLES rule 18 requires before any fact a design leans on ships, and it was skipped in round 1 (a WebSearch was substituted for it). Both scripts now consult the correct variable before falling back to the shared literal, gated through a new shared shape check (`isValidSessionId`, `^[A-Za-z0-9._-]{1,128}$`, GitHub Issue #276 / red-team F5, below), and stay in sync by construction (`resolveFallbackSessionId()` in each file). A dedicated new regression test proves the OLD, wrong variable name is never consulted even when it is set and the correct one is absent — so this fix cannot silently regress back to the wrong name. The happy path (stdin parses, a validly-shaped `session_id` present) is untouched.
- **Issue #274 / red-team F2 (fixed — the cross-session guard, generalized).** Fixing #96 by correcting the variable name alone would have reactivated the round-2 escalation it was built to prevent: `reconcileReason`'s anti-collision guard special-cased only the literal `"unknown-session"` bucket, reasoning that any OTHER resolved session id is, "by construction," unique to this invocation. That reasoning breaks the moment the env fallback can return a real-shaped id: an environment value can legitimately name a *different*, currently-active session (a nested Claude Code session, an inherited shell export, a reused CI/tmux environment) — red-team demonstrated this end-to-end, a victim session's genuinely-active halt going from blocked (exit 2) to silently unblocked (exit 0). **Fix:** `sessionstart-tool-enum.mjs`'s `main()` now threads a `sessionIdFromStdin` boolean into `reconcileReason`, true only when this run's `sessionId` was read directly from THIS invocation's own stdin `session_id` field — the one case the host itself vouches the id belongs to this invocation. The additive-only guard (never reconcile to `set:false`) now fires whenever that boolean is false, which subsumes the literal-bucket case as one instance rather than the only one. Reasoning stated plainly: an env-resolved session id is trusted enough to *write* a fail-closed `set:true` disclosure (the safe direction), never trusted enough to *clear* another reason's active halt, because nothing on this runtime guarantees an env-resolved id is unique to one invocation. `hooks/userpromptsubmit-halt-relay.mjs` is read-only and never calls `reconcileReason`, so it needs no equivalent boolean — only the shared `isValidSessionId` shape gate, below.
- **Issue #276 / red-team F5 (fixed, defense-in-depth).** A resolved session id (from either stdin or the env fallback) now has to match `^[A-Za-z0-9._-]{1,128}$` (`isValidSessionId`, one function, duplicated across both hook files) before it is trusted for a halt-state path (`haltStatePath`'s `join()` call, closing a latent path-traversal surface red-team flagged as unproven-but-cheap-to-close) or for the `reconcileReason` trust boolean above. This also closes red-team's own noted "same-shape sibling": a stdin `session_id` of `""` is a string but not a *usable* one, and previously slipped past the anti-collision guard the same way an env-resolved id could.
- **Issue #206 / #277 (fixed — round 2's `neutralizeUnlockToken` detection layer replaced with a structural, position-based redesign).** `app-security-reviewer`'s finding 5 demonstrated that `describeActiveReasons` concatenated a trusted label, the sanitized `detail` string, and the trusted `(unlock: ...)` suffix with no boundary of its own, so a crafted `detail` could forge a fake `(unlock: ...)` parenthetical ahead of the real one. Round 1 fixed this by backslash-escaping literal `(`/`)`; red-team demonstrated four bypasses the same day (fullwidth parens, small-form parens, square brackets, a no-bracket shape). **Round 2 fixed those four** by neutralizing the literal `unlock:` token itself (after Unicode NFKC normalization) instead of just its surrounding punctuation. **Round-2 review (`red-team` go/MED, `app-security-reviewer` REWORK, GitHub Issue #277) found this was still a text-matching defense, one level up:** a zero-width character (U+200B, U+00AD, U+2060) spliced into the middle of "unlock", or a homoglyph substitution (Cyrillic/Greek о, a colon lookalike such as U+A789/U+2236), survives NFKC normalization and the ASCII regex untouched, while rendering as plain "unlock:" to a human — demonstrated end-to-end through the real production path (a hostile claude.ai connector display name in `~/.claude.json` → the real SessionStart writer → the real relay). Round 2's own regression oracle (`countUnlockTokenOccurrences`) was byte-identical to the defense's own regex, so it was structurally blind to every one of these shapes and reported "1 = clean" for all eight shapes red-team demonstrated. **Round 3 (this fix) stops trying to detect forged text and makes forgery impossible by position instead.** The trusted, real unlock instructions for every active reason are now rendered entirely from this file's own code-controlled strings (`FRIENDLY_LABELS`/`UNLOCK_HINTS`, or a fully generic, non-interpolating fallback for an unmapped key) and placed on the message's first physical line, with nothing third-party-influenced ever interpolated into that line. Third-party-controlled text (`detail`, and — defense in depth — the reason KEY) is rendered separately, on later lines, behind a fixed banner labeled untrusted/informational-only. The separation is forgery-proof because the one and only physical newline character is, and always has been, stripped from third-party text by the existing control-character strip (`diagnosticSanitize`, `sanitizeDetail`'s successor) *before* this file ever renders it — so a line boundary this file itself inserts can never have been produced by untrusted content, by construction, with nothing left to enumerate, fold, or match. `neutralizeUnlockToken` and `escapeParens` (rounds 2-1's token/punctuation matchers) are deleted; the `systemMessage` JSON field (not limited to one line, per this file's own doc citation) still carries the whole message, so no disclosure is lost — only the trust boundary moved from a detectable substring to a real line boundary.
- **GitHub Issue #276 / red-team F4 (fixed — the reason KEY itself, not only `detail`; redesigned again in round 3).** `describeActiveReasons` originally rendered the reason KEY raw at two sites — the friendly-label prefix, and (for an unmapped key) inside the generic unlock-hint fallback. Round 2 routed the unmapped-key fallback through the same token/punctuation sanitizer as `detail`. Round 3 goes further: the trusted first line now uses a purely positional label (`Reason N`) and a generic hint that points at a diagnostic line (`DETAILS[N]`) instead of ever interpolating the raw key into trusted text at all — closing the same class of gap for the reason key that the round-3 redesign closes for `detail`, and removing the last third-party-influenced interpolation from the trusted first line.
- **GitHub Issue #276 / red-team round-2 R6 (fixed, folded into the same redesign).** `main()`'s top-level exception handler interpolated the caught error's own `.message` directly into the one-and-only message line — the one render path earlier rounds' sanitization never covered. V8's own `JSON.parse` error message echoes a snippet of malformed input verbatim, newlines included, when the input is invalid from its very first byte (demonstrated: `JSON.parse("abc\ndef")` throws a message containing a literal embedded newline) — splitting the documented single-stderr-line contract. The catch handler is now built the same structural way as the active-reasons path: a fully code-controlled trusted first line, with the sanitized exception message relegated to a diagnostic line behind the same banner.
- **Interaction between the fixes.** All touch the same two files, at largely disjoint call sites — session-id resolution near the top of each `main()` (#96/#274/#276-F5) vs. message composition (#206/#277/#276-F4/R6) — except for one deliberate seam: `blockWithMessage` interpolates `sessionId` raw into the rendered message's trusted first line, and after #96's fix that value can originate from the env fallback; `isValidSessionId`'s shape gate (closing #276-F5) makes that interpolation safe by construction rather than adding a third, separate sanitization pass.
- **Issue #278 (fixed — round-3 review, `red-team`, widened the control-strip past ASCII).** Round 3's structural redesign (above) rests on one load-bearing sentence: `diagnosticSanitize` strips the one physical newline character (0x0A) from third-party text before this file ever renders it, so a `\n` the code itself inserts is a boundary untrusted content can never produce. `red-team`'s round-3 re-attack found this sentence was true only for literal `\n` — the control-strip was ASCII-only (`[\x00-\x1F\x7F]`), so Unicode `U+2028` (LINE SEPARATOR), `U+2029` (PARAGRAPH SEPARATOR) and `U+0085` (NEL, a C1 control) survived it and NFKC normalization untouched, and all three DO render as a line break in a real terminal — demonstrated end-to-end through the full production path. Critically, the PRIMARY guarantee (no untrusted character can ever reach the trusted first line) held under every attack red-team tried; what broke was the absolute claim, the containment of untrusted text to its own single `DETAILS[N]` diagnostic line, and the regression suite's ability to see any of it (every test splits on `0x0A` only). Red-team verified the fix itself before reporting: widening the strip to `[\p{Cc}\p{Zl}\p{Zp}]` under the `/u` flag (Cc subsumes the existing ASCII range plus every other Unicode control/format character including U+0085; Zl/Zp are U+2028/U+2029 by definition) closes all three demonstrated shapes with the suite still green. The Manager applied this exact verified fix directly (no further implementer round — the fix was already written and tested by the reviewer that found the gap), plus a small wording fix to `DIAGNOSTIC_BANNER` red-team separately flagged (LOW): it disclaimed a forged *copy of the banner itself* but not a forged *"end of untrusted section"* marker, which is far cheaper for an attacker to fit inside the 200-character detail cap than a full banner copy. A new regression test (`hooks/userpromptsubmit-halt-relay-issue206-unlock-forgery.test.ts`) reproduces all three separator shapes end-to-end and asserts the character is stripped everywhere in the rendered message, not just absent from the code's own line count.
- **Not fixed this round, explicitly deferred.** Red-team's F6 (SUSPICION, `writeHaltReason` is a non-atomic read-modify-write with no lock — a concurrent-writer race could lose a `set:true` reason) is UNPROVEN (no concurrent-writer drill run) and disclosed as a residual, not attempted here. Red-team's round-3 SUSPICION (LOW) that `composeTrustedSummary` has no length/count cap where `diagnosticSanitize` has `MAX_DETAIL_LENGTH` (a session with thousands of active reasons could produce an arbitrarily long first line) is also left as a disclosed residual — no live trigger found (the only production writer owns 3 keys), and reaching it already requires a strictly stronger capability (direct halt-state file writes) than this mechanism defends against, per red-team's own round-2 R8/Issue #93 note.
- **Tests.** `hooks/sessionstart-tool-enum-session-id-fallback.test.ts` (6 tests, unchanged since round 2) and `hooks/userpromptsubmit-halt-relay-issue206-unlock-forgery.test.ts` (15 tests: the legacy round-2 PoCs re-run against the round-3 redesign, the 7 specific zero-width/homoglyph/colon-lookalike shapes red-team and app-security demonstrated in round 2, a full production-path test through the real `sessionstart-tool-enum.mjs` writer, the defense-in-depth unmapped-key case, a benign control, the hostile-reason-key case, the R6 exception-message-newline case, and the new Issue #278 Unicode-line-separator case) — the oracle is exact string equality of the message's literal first physical line against a hand-computed, fully-trusted expected string, never a regex over the payload, so it shares no matching logic with the (now-deleted) token/punctuation defenses it replaces — non-circular by construction, not merely by care. `hooks/userpromptsubmit-halt-relay-friendly-labels.test.ts` and `hooks/userpromptsubmit-halt-relay-fixnow.test.ts` (11 tests each, same count, updated expected strings for the new message shape). Non-vacuous by mutation: collapsing the trusted/diagnostic line join from `"\n"` to `" "` (reproducing the old single-line design) turned 15 of the 25 tests in the two most relevant files red immediately (red-team's own round-3 re-run of this same mutation found 25 red out of the full 79-test hook suite); the file was restored via `cp` from a pre-mutation backup and the suite re-confirmed green afterward. Full suite: `npm test` 1064 tests, 1064 pass, 0 skipped, 0 fail. `npm run typecheck` and `npm run lint` both clean.

### Fixed — `s1-242-qa15-timeout-headroom` (issue 242): QA-15's per-instrument timeout no longer sits close enough to a real instrument's runtime to false-red under load

STANDARD tier (proposed by the implementer, ratified by the Manager; scope `s1-242-qa15-timeout-headroom`). Touches a required CI gate's pass/fail logic (`src/qa/completeness-claim-checker.ts`), not one of CLAUDE.md's named sensitive areas — a timeout-value tuning fix whose failure mode is the gate becoming MORE lenient about false negatives, never less strict about a real defect. No `test-writer` dispatch: an internal gate with no UI flow or API surface.

- **Before.** `verifyMarkerClaim` hardcoded `timeoutMs: 60_000` for every instrument in `KNOWN_INSTRUMENTS`, one shared cap, no per-instrument override. `qa-mutation-shell` ran close enough to it (39s quiet / 56.5s under load, per two reviewers' round-2 reports, `docs/reviews/s1-136-value-scoped-allowlist-*-round2-2026-09-19.md`) that a loaded CI runner reported a false red ("produced no parseable number") and passed clean on a quiet re-run.
- **Measured first, not guessed (PRINCIPLES.md rule 18).** Timed every registered instrument for real on this machine: `qa-fixture-coverage`/`qa-diff-fixture`/`qa-fixture-isolation`/`qa-recurring-findings`/`adr-cache-ensure` all sub-second; `qa14-marker-corpus-probe-*`/`qa14-continuation-residual-probe-*` under 1s; `qa-mutation-shell` ~23s; `qa-reference-resolver` ~47s; `oss-history-scan` ~69s — already OVER the old 60s cap on a quiet run, though not currently wired to a live blocking marker. `oss-history-scan`, not `qa-mutation-shell`, is this repo's own slowest known instrument.
- **After.** The cap is a named, documented constant, `INSTRUMENT_TIMEOUT_MS = 180_000`, raised globally rather than given a per-instrument override — no instrument here needs a short timeout to fail fast on a real hang, so a per-instrument budget mechanism would add complexity with no matching benefit. 180s is ~2.6x `oss-history-scan`'s own quiet 69s, ~3.2x `qa-mutation-shell`'s own reported LOADED 56.5s, and ~3.8x `qa-reference-resolver`'s quiet 47s — real headroom over every measured slow instrument, not just the one that was reported.
- **Tests.** Two new tests in `completeness-claim-checker.test.ts`: one pins `INSTRUMENT_TIMEOUT_MS` at least 2x both the slowest measured quiet instrument and `qa-mutation-shell`'s own reported loaded runtime; one proves (via a spy runner) `verifyMarkerClaim` actually passes the real configured constant through to the runner, not a stale hardcoded value. `npm test`: 1045 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped. `node src/qa/completeness-claim-checker.ts` (QA-15 itself): PASS, 2 files checked, all claims verified — `qa-mutation-shell` re-ran KILLED 53/53 at ~23s, comfortably inside the new 180s cap.
### Fixed — `s1-oss01-detection-residuals` (issues 246, 247, 249, 250): OSS-01 now decodes UTF-16, bounds a hostile blob's scan time, catches every spelling of the 0xA0 defect, and no longer lets blob dedupe defeat allowlist path scoping

CRITICAL tier (proposed by the implementer, ratified by the Manager; scope `s1-oss01-detection-residuals`). Secret-scanning sensitive area: the scanner (`src/secret-scan/history-scan.ts`, `src/secret-scan/patterns.ts`) and the CI gate (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`). No `test-writer` dispatch: an internal gate with no UI flow or API surface. All four issues originate from the same red-team report, `docs/reviews/s1-237-nul-byte-scan-red-team-2026-09-20.md` (attacks 1, 2, 3), and are bundled into one story because they are small, individually well-specified and share the same file family.

- **Issue 246 (UTF-16 decode).** A blob starting with a UTF-16 byte-order-mark (LE `FF FE` or BE `FE FF`) is now ALSO decoded to real text and matched, on top of the always-on latin1 byte-for-byte reading — additive, never a replacement: a byte-order-mark can open a non-text binary blob for reasons that have nothing to do with UTF-16 (this module's own `high-bytes.bin` test fixture starts `FF FE 80 81 ...`), so replacing the latin1 reading would have reopened Issue 237's own "no blob is skipped whatever its bytes" guarantee. BOM presence is the sole detection signal — deliberately not a heuristic guess at BOM-less UTF-16, so a UTF-16 file with no BOM stays a disclosed, narrower residual. Every pattern in the catalog matches ASCII-only text, so a decoded match hashes identically (THOTH-ADR-0002's existing `hashMatchedBytes`, unchanged) to the same literal written as plain ASCII, which is what lets one allowlist entry cover both forms (Manager ruling on the open design question). `allowlist-tool.ts hash` decodes the same way, so the unlock command it prints matches what the gate computed.
- **Issue 247 (scan-time bound).** `internal-hostname`, `email-address` and `private-key-block` all backtrack super-linearly on an adversarial shape with no closing anchor (measured: a 200 KB hyphen-run took ~33s / ~27s unbounded per pattern; a 560 KB repeated-BEGIN-marker blob, several seconds and climbing quadratically), invisible in a PR diff when the blob looks binary. Every pattern's match against every blob now runs under a hard wall-clock ceiling (`SCAN_TIMEOUT_MS = 500`, `matchAllBounded`, a reused `vm.Script` context — measured directly: `vm.Script#runInContext`'s own `timeout` option does interrupt an in-progress catastrophically-backtracking `RegExp` match in this Node version). A pattern that cannot finish in time is reported as a blocking finding under the reserved `oss01-scan-timeout` pattern id, never silently skipped — fail closed, reviewable and grantable through the same value-scoped allowlist (no new exemption shape). `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s `ci` job gains `timeout-minutes: 30` as an independent backstop. 500ms is roughly 100x this repo's own largest real blob's worst per-pattern time (~5ms, CHANGELOG.md, ~330 KB, measured).
- **Issue 249 (behaviour-based 0xA0 guard).** The old `oss01-no-negated-whitespace-class-hides-a-high-byte` guard only recognised the two-character `\s`/`\S` tokens, so a value class spelled with `\xa0`, the U+00A0 escape, `\p{White_Space}` or a literal 0xA0 byte reinstated the identical defect while staying green against most of those spellings, per the red-team report's own measured table (`docs/reviews/s1-237-nul-byte-scan-red-team-2026-09-20.md`, attack 2). Replaced with a behaviour probe, `oss01-no-negated-class-rejects-a-high-byte`: negated character classes in the catalog are extracted, rebuilt standalone, and run against high bytes 0x80 through 0xFF — the probe never looks at how an exclusion was written, only at what it actually excludes, so a future spelling this list did not anticipate is still caught. The old test stays as the friendlier diagnostic. A positive control reproduces the red-team's own spelling table.
- **Issue 250 (blob dedupe vs. path scoping).** `scanUnseenBlob` skipped any blob sha already seen, so a byte-identical blob at two paths was reported at the first path only and an allowlist grant there silently exempted the identical blob everywhere else — contradicting THOTH-ADR-0002's "path, patternId and value hash must all agree" rule. Fixed by keying the dedupe on `(path, sha)`, not `sha` alone: a blob's own scan cost is still paid once per distinct sha (cached), but its result is now replayed under every distinct PATH that sha is found at, not just the first one. Deliberately NOT keyed on `(commit, path)` — a first cut of this fix tried that and inflated this repo's own real-history match count over 12x (2031 to 25021 allowlisted matches), because a file that never changes still gets a fresh tree entry at every commit that includes it; reporting it again at every one of those commits is not what the ADR's path-scoped rule needs. Caught by re-measuring the real scan before shipping, not assumed correct from the diff alone.
- **A second regression, also caught by measurement, not assumed.** An early version of the Issue 246 fix deduplicated a blob's own matches across its latin1/UTF-16 variants by `(patternId, valueSha256)`, which silently collapsed every REPEATED occurrence of the same value within one blob (this repo's own CHANGELOG.md repeats several hostnames across many entries) into a single finding — changing what "a match" means for every blob in real history, not just a UTF-16 one, and dropping the real-history allowlisted count from 2031 to 923. Fixed by not deduplicating at all: every variant's own occurrences, including genuine repeats, flow through unchanged. Verified: the real full-history scan now reports exactly 2031 allowlisted matches, 0 blocking, unchanged from the pre-story baseline.
- **Named proof-tests, all in `src/secret-scan/history-scan.test.ts` / `patterns.test.ts` / `pre-commit-scan.test.ts` unless noted:** `oss01-utf16-text-file-does-not-hide-a-secret` (both `scanHistory` and the pre-commit CLI; UTF-16LE and UTF-16BE, each with a BOM), `oss01-utf16-without-a-bom-stays-a-disclosed-residual`, `oss01-utf16-bom-sniff-does-not-hide-a-latin1-secret-in-a-non-utf16-blob` (the `high-bytes.bin` interaction, isolated), `oss01-scan-time-is-bounded-on-a-hostile-blob` (hyphen-run cell, repeated-BEGIN-marker cell for `private-key-block`, a clean-content control, and the real CI entry point), `oss01-no-negated-class-rejects-a-high-byte` plus its positive control, `oss01-a-grant-at-one-path-does-not-exempt-the-same-blob-at-another` (plus a control where both paths are granted).
- **Interactions checked, not just the individual fixes.** #246's decode runs before #247's per-pattern timeout wrap (decode is O(n), never itself a slow path). #250's per-(path,sha) cache is content-addressed, so it stays correct with #246's variant decoding (which depends only on the blob's own bytes). #247's 500ms budget was chosen with ~100x headroom over this repo's own measured worst-case real-content pattern time, specifically so #246's newly-reachable UTF-16 matching does not itself trip the timeout on legitimate content.
- **Checks.** `npm run typecheck` and `npm run lint` clean. `npm test`: 1055 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full run, this story's own branch). Code-correctness comparison, same base history, old code vs. new code (`git stash` toggling the diff on and off at the same commit, so only the scanner logic differs): PASS, 0 blocking, 2031 allowlisted both before and after — the fix adds capability without changing what this repo's real, pre-existing history matches. After committing this story's own change (one more commit, touching files that already carry pre-existing self-referential test fixtures — every edited file's own new blob version re-matches those same already-allowlisted values again, the same mechanics any commit touching those files has always had): PASS, 0 blocking, 2067 allowlisted, in ~74s.
- **Out of scope, unchanged.** The LOW/SUSPICION findings from the same red-team report (`oss01-widening-a-value-class-can-swallow-an-adjacent-match`, the binary-asset chance-match suspicion) were not filed as Issues and are not addressed here.

### Fix-now round (2026-09-22): six findings from round-1 CRITICAL review (`red-team` no-go, `app-security-reviewer` REWORK) fixed together

Round-1 review (`docs/reviews/s1-oss01-detection-residuals-{red-team,app-security,cross-domain}-2026-09-22.md`) found the story above shippable in its bulk but broken at its own edges: the new `oss01-scan-timeout` finding kind was grantable through the value-scoped allowlist without being content-addressed, its own unlock command never ran, the #249 behaviour probe was still syntax-scoped, and a UTF-16 decode/hash interaction could collide two distinct values. All security-category findings, all fixed now, none deferred (PRINCIPLES rule 21's exposure cap does not apply to security findings).

- **Issue 264 (HIGH, red-team + app-security-reviewer).** `oss01-scan-timeout`'s `valueSha256` was `sha256("oss01-scan-timeout:<patternId>:<text.length>")` — a function of length only, never the blob's actual bytes. Two different 200,000-byte blobs (a legitimate oversized asset and an attacker's blob of the same length) hashed identically, so granting the first silently exempted the second forever. Fixed: the hash is now computed over the variant's own scanned text (`hashMatchedBytes(...:${text})`), so it is content-addressed like every other finding — two different blobs never share a hash, whatever their length. Named test: `oss01-a-scan-timeout-grant-does-not-exempt-a-different-blob-at-the-same-path` (`history-scan.test.ts`).
- **Issue 267 (MED, red-team), landed together with 264, never alone.** The `HASH-COMMAND` the gate prints for every `oss01-scan-timeout` block looked `oss01-scan-timeout` up in `SECRET_PATTERNS` and threw `unknown pattern id` before reading a byte — exit 2, always, for every path and commit, so the unlock for the finding above was unreachable. Fixed: `allowlist-tool.ts`'s `hashLines` special-cases the reserved id by re-running every real pattern against the text and keeping only the resulting `oss01-scan-timeout` findings — the same computation the gate itself performs, so it reproduces the identical hash(es). Named tests: `oss01-every-blocking-pattern-id-has-a-runnable-unlock-command` (`history-scan.test.ts`, real CLI end to end) and `sb2-hash-lines-computes-the-scan-timeout-pattern-id-without-throwing` (`allowlist-tool.test.ts`, direct unit level).
- **Issue 268 (MED, red-team).** The #249 behaviour probe (`oss01-no-negated-class-rejects-a-high-byte`) only extracted NEGATED classes (`[^...]`) before probing them; its own premise ("a non-negated class can only match more, never hide data") is false — a positive class, a negative lookahead or a unicode-property escape narrows a value's matching identically well, and four such spellings passed the old guard green against the real shipped `generic-password-assignment` pattern. Superseded, not merely patched: a new whole-pattern behaviour sweep, `oss01-no-value-class-however-written-hides-a-high-byte` (`patterns.test.ts`), splices every byte 0x80-0xFF into each of the catalog's two free-form-value patterns' (`generic-password-assignment`, `private-key-block`) own exemplars and runs the real, shipped regex end to end — construct-independent by design. A positive control proves it catches two non-negated spellings the old, syntax-scoped guard is provably blind to (`negatedClasses()` finds nothing to probe in either mutation).
- **Issue 266 (MED, app-security-reviewer).** `hashMatchedBytes` reinterpreted every match as latin1, which truncates a JS string code point above 0xFF to its low byte. For the two patterns with an open or negated value class, a UTF-16-decoded match built from high-code-point characters (visually nothing like the reviewed literal) truncated to the same low-byte sequence as an already-reviewed plain-ASCII value, colliding on `valueSha256` — demonstrated with Armenian-range glyphs colliding with `"SuperSecretPW1"`'s hash. Fixed: a match containing any code point above 0xFF is now hashed over its own UTF-16LE code units instead of a lossy latin1 reinterpretation (lossless, so two distinct matched strings never collide); every match that stays inside 0x00-0xFF — every latin1-variant match, and every UTF-16-variant match of genuinely ASCII/latin1-range text, the common and intended case — keeps the unchanged latin1 hash, so the "one entry covers both forms" equivalence still holds for the case it was designed for. Chosen over rejecting the match outright: a reject-on-ambiguity design would have stopped reporting a genuine secret that merely contains a non-latin1 character, the worse failure mode for a security gate. Named test: `oss01-utf16-disguised-match-does-not-collide-with-a-reviewed-ascii-value` (`history-scan.test.ts`), reproducing app-security-reviewer's own PoC shape.
- **Issue 265 (HIGH, app-security-reviewer) — investigated, not reproduced.** app-security-reviewer observed `oss01-a-grant-at-one-path-does-not-exempt-the-same-blob-at-another`'s real-CLI subtest intermittently return `cli.code === 0` (1 failure in 4 runs) when the three touched test files were run at CI's own `node --test` concurrency shape. Investigated by: (a) 700+ direct trials of the exact production path this test proves (`scanHistory`, `partitionAllowlisted`, and the real CLI, both in-process and via fresh subprocess spawn) at 20-way and 60-way concurrency, including 200 trials under 40 artificial CPU-stress workers deliberately oversubscribing this machine's 22 cores — zero reproductions; (b) 8 clean sequential runs of the exact 3-file `node --test` command CI uses (one run's failure was this fix-now round's own unrelated, already-fixed test-authoring bug in a different, newly-added test, not this one); (c) 20/20 clean runs of the named test alone in the same 3-file shape; (d) 5 full-suite `npm test` runs matching CI's exact invocation — 4 clean, 1 failure under EXTREME self-inflicted contention (the artificial stress workers and probe above running simultaneously with the full suite) in a different, unrelated test (`pre-commit-scan.test.ts`'s Issue #194 regression, a subprocess-stdout assertion), not this one — consistent with generic subprocess-spawn sensitivity under contention far beyond CI's own conditions, not a logic defect in the #250 fix; (e) a concrete candidate hypothesis (an inherited `NODE_CHANNEL_FD`/IPC environment leak into the spawned CLI subprocess, the same failure class `src/lib/exec.ts`'s own header comment documents for a different nesting case) was checked directly by dumping the child process's own `process.env` under `node --test` and ruled out — no such variable is ever present, and the two that are (`NODE_TEST_CONTEXT`, `NODE_TEST_WORKER_ID`) are already stripped by `cleanSubprocessEnv()`. No isolation defect was found in the test's own harness (each fixture repo gets its own unique `mkdtemp` directory; no shared state). Not fixed, because no reproducible defect — in the test or the production code — could be found to fix. Disclosed here as an unreproduced residual for the Manager, not silently dropped.
- **Issue 269 (MED, red-team, code-traced).** THOTH-ADR-0002's residual register had two rows this diff's own predecessor story falsified: the UTF-16 row still read "what remains open is UTF-16 text" without distinguishing that the BOM'd half is now closed (issue 246) and only the BOM-less half remains; the blob-dedupe row still read "not changed by this ADR" when issue 250 changed exactly that. Both reworded to describe the current, narrower state without overstating what's fixed — BOM-less UTF-16 stays a disclosed, open gap.
- **Checks.** `npm run typecheck` and `npm run lint` clean. `npm test` (clean run, no artificial load): 1061 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped, 142s. `node src/secret-scan/history-scan.ts`: PASS, 0 blocking, 2076 allowlisted (up from 2067: this round's own edited files re-match their own already-reviewed fixture literals, the same mechanics any commit touching those files has always had).

### Fixed — `s1-229-qa14-red` (issue 229): QA-14 diff mode no longer goes red on example references that already sit in historical records

CRITICAL tier (proposed by the implementer, ratified by the Manager; scope `s1-229-qa14-red`). QA-14 is a P0 CI gate and a scoping error fails open. No `test-writer` dispatch: an internal gate with no UI flow or API surface. Plan: `docs/plans/s1-229-qa14-red-phase1-2026-09-21.md`. Build report with the measurements: `docs/plans/s1-229-qa14-red-build-report-2026-09-21.md`. The rulings are in `docs/decisions.md`, human ratification pending.

- **Before.** The diff-mode run CI performs scanned every changed file whole, so a routine append to the decision log, the review log, the changelog or a state doc re-flagged the example references in old rows. Replaying the last merge on master (the range ending at commit 22b7141): 26 failing citations among 1412 scanned, spread over six files.
- **After.** Which text of a file is checked is decided in the new `src/qa/reference-scope.ts`; `src/qa/reference-resolver.ts` gains one call, a base-existence helper, a stderr note when the diff cannot be read, and a file name on each failure line. Replaying the same range with only this mechanism: 6 failing (five in `docs/STATE.md`, one in `src/secret-scan/patterns.ts`); after the `docs/STATE.md` and `docs/backlog.md` rewording below: 1 (`src/secret-scan/patterns.ts`).
- **Append-only records are checked on the text a diff adds** (`docs/decisions.md`, `docs/decisions-archive.md`, `docs/REVIEW_LOG.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, every file under `docs/reviews/`), but only for a path that already exists at the diff base. A path absent at the base (a new file, or the destination of a rename, with or without an edit) has no old text to skip and is checked whole. A citation a diff adds to any record is checked, including a new line that repeats a token failing on an old line. Nothing is keyed on a token or a path.
- **Moved lines are not new text, by a multiset rule.** An added line identical to a line removed from an append-only record the same run scans (the archive sweep, a move between records) is not checked; each removed line licenses at most one added line, so a bad line added three times and removed once still has two checked copies. A removal from anything else licenses nothing: a living doc (state, backlog, `CLAUDE.md`, a plan, source), a test file, the `adrCatalog` mirror, a file the diff deletes, or the old path of a renamed file. A failing line therefore cannot be walked out of a whole-file-scanned file into a record diff mode never re-reads.
- **The `adrCatalog` mirror in `docs/.maat-state.json` is not checked** (root and the `priorScope` chain only). It is a generated cache of ADR text. Every other field of the file is scanned. The cut is made only on a file that equals the two-space JSON serialization of its own parse (CRLF and one trailing newline aside); any other form, including a duplicate key, an escaped key spelling or a comment, is scanned whole.
- **Fails closed to a whole-file scan:** a full-tree run, a diff that cannot be read (now announced on stderr), a failing base-existence check, an append-only path absent at the base (rename with an edit included), and any file the diff parser cannot attribute (a quoted path, a pure rename, a mode change, a binary diff, a non-default git prefix setting).
- **Living docs stay whole-file.** `docs/STATE.md` and `docs/backlog.md` are read whole, so their standing example references were reworded in place to describe the example instead of quoting it; every number in those sentences is unchanged. Nothing under `docs/reviews/` was modified.
- **What QA-14 no longer checks, stated for QA-16:** a citation on a line that already existed in an append-only record and that the diff does not touch; an added line identical to a line the same diff removes from an append-only record (a move); a citation inside the `adrCatalog` mirror. Every other line a diff adds is checked.
- **Requirement text not amended here.** The QA-14 row in `REQUIREMENTS.md` still reads "every reference in a changed file"; the amendment is tracked in Issue #252 and in plan section 6 for the human. The file carries stale citations of its own that would turn this change red if it were edited in the same diff.
- **Residuals, disclosed.** A new review report that quotes example references is new text and still fails. An edited historical row is checked whole. A change that edits `REQUIREMENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, an older plan, a hook or `src/secret-scan/patterns.ts` turns red on that file's own standing citations. A full-tree run stays whole-file by design. A word-form issue citation whose number starts the next line (an added line that begins with a bare number, after an unchanged line ending in the word issue) is unclassified in added-text scope, where a whole-file scan would flag it. A line that already sits unchecked in one record can be moved into another as a move.
- **Review round 1 fixes, red first (Issue #253, Issue #254).** A rename with an edit into an append-only path no longer exempts the moved body; a removal from a living doc no longer licenses a move. The hand-written JSON tokenizer was replaced by the parse-and-compare cut above. Assertions were added for the three surviving mutants. The mutations run against the shipped module and the named test each turned red are listed in the build report, sections 5 and "Fix-now round 1"; two mutants there are equivalent and turn nothing red.
- **Tests, red first.** The file-name tests and the scoping tests were each run against the unchanged behavior before their implementation. The scoping tests include the real archive-sweep script, real git diffs (rename with and without an edit, delete, binary, mode change), and laundering cases. One mutant survived a fixture that was too weak, which led to a stronger fixture with the same assertions.

### Fixed — `s1-237-nul-byte-scan` (issue 237): OSS-01 no longer skips a file with a NUL byte, and a UTF-8 character ending in 0xA0 no longer hides a password

CRITICAL tier (proposed by the implementer, ratified by the Manager; scope `s1-237-nul-byte-scan`). Secret-scanning sensitive area: the scanner and its pattern catalog. No `test-writer` dispatch: an internal gate with no UI flow or API surface. Plan: `docs/plans/s1-237-nul-byte-scan-phase1-2026-09-20.md`. The rulings behind it are in `docs/decisions.md`, human ratification pending.

- **Before.** The scanner skipped a whole blob when a NUL byte sat in its first 8000 bytes, in the pre-commit hook and in CI, so a key literal in such a file was never matched (demonstrated in a scratch clone: the same staged file exited 0 at the base commit and exited 1 with the fix, at both entry points). Separately, the generic-password value class used the whitespace escape, which matches latin1 0xA0: a UTF-8 character whose second byte is 0xA0 (a-grave) ended the value early and hid the password.
- **After.** Every blob is decoded as latin1 and matched like any other; a NUL byte is an ordinary byte. `looksBinary` and its call are deleted and nothing replaces them. The decode, the value hash and the allowlist are unchanged. A legitimate binary that produces a match is handled by the existing value-scoped entry (THOTH-ADR-0002): no new exemption shape, no new "unscannable" state. The password value class now lists ASCII whitespace by hand (`[^'" \t\n\v\f\r]`). The separator tokens and the any-character idiom keep the whitespace escape on purpose: there it hides no data, and narrowing it would stop matching a file whose separator is a lone 0xA0. The widening is not strictly additive: under leftmost non-overlapping matching, a widened value can run past a 0xA0 and close on a later quote, consuming an adjacent assignment that the old class matched by itself (the consuming match still blocks). Measured here by the spike below: no history blob is affected (1097 scanned) and the old and new (path, pattern, hash) triple sets are equal.
- **Not covered, filed as issue 246.** Issue 237's title leads with UTF-16 text (a byte order mark and interleaved NUL bytes), which still matches nothing under latin1 decoding. Other encodings that interleave or transform the bytes are the same residual (UTF-32 and base64 also yield 0 matches), tracked under issue 246, not a new Issue. This change closes issue 237 for the NUL skip and the 0xA0 miss only, and makes no claim about UTF-16 or any other encoding.
- **Spike, run first.** `docs/spikes/s1-237-nul-blob-counts-2026-09-20.mjs` prints counts only. From HEAD, the old rule skipped one blob (an earlier version of the S-B2 spike file); it matches nothing, so the fix adds no match and no blocking triple on this repository, and the widened class moves no (path, pattern, hash) triple. Neither STOP condition of the plan fired.
- **Allowlist unchanged.** `docs/qa/secret-scan-allowlist.json` is byte-identical to the base. The generator's `verify` mode passes with 0 newly allowlisted and 0 newly blocking, and a fresh `generate` is byte-identical to the tracked file (`cmp`).
- **Tests, red first.** Commit C1 holds the new tests and was run against the unchanged scanner: a NUL byte at offset 0, 1, 7000 or 7999 hid the literal from `scanHistory`, from the history-scan CLI and from the pre-commit hook; an allowlisted NUL-bearing blob was never evaluated; 0xA0 hid a password; one whitespace escape sat inside a negated class of the catalog (found by a tokenizer over the pattern sources, not by hand). Controls are green by design: no NUL, a NUL at byte 8000 and 9000, an e-acute password, ASCII whitespace still ending a value, a lone 0xA0 separator still matching, a NUL-bearing file with no key still passing.
- **Existing tests changed (SE ADR-0005 and ADR-0010; the human confirms at the pull request).** None deleted to make CI pass. `sb2-binary-helper-window-matches-the-scanner` (asserted the skip at byte 7999) became `oss01-no-8000-byte-window-nul-at-7999-and-8000-are-both-scanned`, same fixtures and offsets, assertion inverted. `sb2-no-tracked-text-file-is-skipped-as-binary` (an assurance that no tracked file held a NUL byte) was replaced by `oss01-no-blob-is-skipped-whatever-its-bytes`, a new test on generated bytes (leading, interior and trailing NUL, NUL padding, high bytes, an image-header prefix, a run of every byte value), not an inversion of the old one. NUL offsets 0, 1, 7000, 7999, 8000 and 9000 are covered by `oss01-nul-byte-does-not-hide-a-secret`. Removed as dead code with the rule they measured: the helper `trackedFilesSkippedAsBinary`, its `KNOWN_BINARIES` list, and the sibling test `sb2-no-tracked-text-file-is-skipped-as-binary-tolerates-an-unstaged-deletion`, which tested that helper alone (its no-working-tree-file state is asserted inside the new test). `git grep` found no other user. With the source change in and the old tests untouched, the window test was the only failure.
- **Cost.** Pre-commit scan, same tree, old and new code, median of three: 15.3 s before and 14.9 s after (sequential), 16.6 s and 17.5 s (interleaved). The run-to-run spread on this machine exceeds the difference. `npm test`: 980 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped.
- **Residuals filed, not fixed here.** Issue 246 (the residual above) and issue 247 (quadratic time in the hostname and email patterns on a long hyphen-joined run; pre-existing and independent of NUL bytes).

### Changed — `s1-136-value-scoped-allowlist` (issues 136 and 203): an OSS-01 allowlist entry now exempts only the values it names

CRITICAL tier (proposed by the implementer, ratified by the Manager; scope `s1-136-value-scoped-allowlist`). Secret-scanning sensitive area: the suppression list and the code that reads it. No `test-writer` dispatch: an internal gate with no UI flow or API surface. Plan: `docs/plans/s1-136-value-scoped-allowlist-phase1-2026-09-19.md` (with the design-challenger dispositions appended). Draft `docs/adr/thoth-0002-value-scoped-secret-scan-allowlist.md` is **Proposed**: the human accepts or declines it at the pull request. `src/secret-scan/patterns.ts`, `src/secret-scan/pre-commit-scan.ts`, `src/secret-scan/simulated-commit.ts`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `.githooks/pre-commit` and `src/lib/git.ts` are untouched (empty diffstat).

- **Before.** An entry matched on `path` and `patternId` only, so an identical synthetic credential passed in a file that had an entry and failed in a file that had none: the entry blinded the gate to that pattern in that file for every future edit (issue 136, demonstrated with a positive control).
- **After.** An entry is `{path, patternId, valueSha256: [...], reason}`. A match is allowlisted only when its path, its pattern id and the sha256 of the matched bytes all agree; otherwise it blocks. The scanner hashes each match when it finds it (latin1 round trip of the decoded text, equal to the file's own bytes) and stores no raw text. One implementation serves the CI history scan, the pre-commit hook and the dogfood test. There is no legacy shape: an entry without a valid lowercase 64-hex list, empty, or with any malformed element is rejected and its matches block.
- **Block output.** A blocked run now names its unlock: the rule, a per-pair command (`src/secret-scan/allowlist-tool.ts hash <commit> "<path>" <patternId>`, which runs in the Windows shell) that prints each match's hash beside its redacted form, printed only for a path of `[A-Za-z0-9._/-]` (any other path gets a `NO-COMMAND-PRINTED` line that is not a command and shows the path percent-encoded, Issue 239), and the fact that the hashed value is the regex match text (for `generic-password-assignment` and `aws-secret-access-key` that includes the key name, operator and quotes). Rejected entries and file-level rejections (unreadable, not JSON, not an array) are named with index, path, pattern id and a reason class, never a value or a hash. The gate never prints the hash of a blocked match, and the generated history-scan report keeps a hash only for allowlisted matches.
- **Migration.** `docs/qa/secret-scan-allowlist.json` was migrated in one commit by the retained generator (`src/secret-scan/allowlist-tool.ts`, `generate` and `verify`): each existing entry keeps its path, pattern id and reason verbatim and gains the sorted, distinct hashes of its own matches over history at the base commit. `verify` enforces the subset structurally (every migrated entry sits on a legacy pair with its reason verbatim, every listed hash is carried by a real match, an already value-scoped entry is unchanged) and also reports zero newly allowlisted and zero newly blocking occurrences on that scope, as a second, direct measurement; an independent instrument (red-team) reached the same zero. Its output is counts only, classified per pattern id, under the dated-reports directory and credential-shaped, so a reviewer approving opaque hashes has value-level evidence without a raw value. An already value-scoped entry is never widened by a re-run. Rollback: revert the whole story as one unit (the merge commit with `git revert -m 1`, or the squash commit; on an unmerged branch every commit after `7b62344`, the parent of `fefce23`, with `git revert --no-commit 7b62344..<branch tip>`; the range has no caret because cmd treats a caret as an escape character). Reverting only the migration commit restores loader and file together and keeps the gate safe, but leaves the suite red (measured by cross-domain review at commit 46e8d88; at a later commit that revert also conflicts), because the red-first tests and the generator's test still expect the new behavior. Facts that stay true: an old loader reads the new file, and with the new loader and a damaged file, repair on disk by re-running `generate` on the legacy file at the base commit (drilled in a scratch clone: corrupted file fails closed, repaired file commits without `--no-verify`).
- **Issue 203 (report grants).** The `docs/reviews/` exclusion in the reviewed-baseline guard is removed and the credential-shaped report grants are pinned with per-grant reasons (hashes from the guard's own derivation). A legacy-shaped report grant blocks at the gate. A report grant carrying the literal's own correct hash still passes the gate (no oracle); the baseline guard catches an omitted pin, not a deliberate self-hashed one, and report immutability is not enforced mechanically (issue 233). Stated, not closed.
- **Named exception for the human.** One pinned value is the truncated prefix of what its report records as a real credential (issue 89, still open, human-only). The ADR rule now reads "usable credential (a value that authenticates)" and names this single exception; the human accepts it at the pull request or rotates and removes it.
- **Tests.** Named tests written red first, then the code. Existing tests changed in fixture shape (inline entries and match literals gain the new field; the dogfood test loads through the real loader), import lines and comments were added or edited, the reviewed-baseline guard's derive helper lost its report-directory exclusion (issue 203, with its comment block rewritten), and the issue 203 change flips one assertion; no test or assertion is removed or weakened. Fixtures are built at runtime, so no new fixture literal needed an allowlist entry; the spike's distinct-triple count was checked before and after every commit and did not move. The story's own spike had raw NUL bytes (binary to git, skipped by the scanner); fixed, and a test now asserts no tracked text file is skipped as binary.
- **Cost.** Same-tree, alternating old and new code, median of five: 14.18 s before, 14.43 s after the pre-commit scan (about two percent). Hash cost measured by the spike is tens of milliseconds per scan. `npm test`: 944 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped after the fix-now round and its three later batches below (929 before them); typecheck and lint clean.
- **Out of scope, filed:** issue 237 (a NUL byte in the first bytes hides a text file from the scanner; pre-existing), issue 233 (report immutability), issue 234 (stale sensitive-area globs), issue 235 (entries that match nothing), issue 236 (the pre-commit hook reads the allowlist from the working tree).
- **Post-build review round (fix-now).** Reviews: `docs/reviews/s1-136-value-scoped-allowlist-{app-security,red-team,code,cross-domain}-2026-09-19.md`. Fixed: issue 239 (HIGH, found independently by app-security and red-team): the printed unlock command put a contributor-chosen file path in double quotes, so a name holding a command substitution, backticks, an ampersand, a semicolon, a pipe or quote parity ran when a maintainer pasted it (demonstrated in sh, PowerShell and cmd). A runnable command is now printed only for a path of `[A-Za-z0-9._/-]`; any other path gets a line that is not a command, with the path percent-encoded, plus one how-to line that names no path: the value hash is the sha256 of the matched text (the whole regex match, no trailing newline, computed with a local sha256 tool over the literal in your own file), a note that the allowlist entry's path field is the percent-decoded form of the printed path, or rename the path to a shell-safe one, with a shell-safe channel tracked in issue 241. An earlier version told the maintainer to hand-quote the path; a Manager ruling removed it because red-team measured every quoting strategy executing a payload for some hostile name in some shell. Red-first tests `sb2-unlock-command-never-embeds-a-shell-metacharacter-path` and `oss01-unlock-command-never-interpolates-shell-metacharacters-from-a-path` cover a table of hostile names through the real CLI output, paste every unlock line into the platform shell, and include a positive control that the old command shape does execute a payload. This also covers the symptom of issue 238 (the human closes it). Pinned: issue 240 (generate and verify read history and the legacy file at the base ref; a two-commit fixture test, red under three mutants), the ten-pair cap of the unlock lines, and the `hash` subcommand printing every match of a blob. The binary-skip assurance now reads blobs from the object database (an unstaged deletion no longer fails it with ENOENT). Red-team F2: verify's newly-allowlisted count is reachable but never the only failure (found by enumeration), so it stays as a second measurement and two tests pin that. The ADR frontmatter now serves both rules the catalog had dropped, the ADR residual table gained the rows the reviewers named, and the rollback and subset wording was corrected to what is true. Final batch: three code-reviewer pins (the encoder's hex zero-padding and code-point iteration, the per-pattern dedupe key of the unlock lines, and the binary-skip helper's window checked against the scanner's own behavior), and the ADR's moved-base rule (the regeneration pull request states the count delta) is now a served Rules-for-agents constraint. Issue 241 stays open as an enhancement. Round three: the rejected-entry line now shows the allowlist entry's path and pattern id percent-encoded and clipped (issue 243), the how-to sentence is one constant pinned verbatim by a test and a second test classifies every printed line (issue 244), and the rollback range is written without a caret. Round four: the problem and classification lines of `allowlist-tool verify` show entry paths and pattern ids the same way (a follow-up to issue 243, found by reading the tool's own output sites), and unparseable input gets a fixed message instead of the JSON parser's, which quotes a piece of the file. Closing issues 136, 203, 238, 239 and 240 is the human's call at the pull request.

STANDARD tier (ratified by the Manager; scope `s1-135-pat-regression-test`). Test-only. No `test-writer` dispatch: no UI flow or API surface. Plan: `docs/plans/s1-135-pat-regression-test-phase1-2026-09-19.md`.

Its title carries both names the reviewers proposed: the Issue #135 regression wording and the bracketed `github-fine-grained-pat-word-boundary-test`.

The new positive fixtures therefore need no allowlist entry and add no member to Issue #136's value-hash migration set. - **Disclosed, not designed for.** A camelCase shape (the prefix, twenty or more alphanumerics, then an underscore) still matches. It is unmeasured beyond a scan of the tracked tree, which found no unexplained match. A demonstrated one becomes its own Issue.

### Fixed — `s1-226-duplicate-field` (Issue #226): a duplicated `--field=` to either QA-14 probe now fails loud instead of resolving first-wins

STANDARD tier (ratified by the Manager; scope `s1-226-duplicate-field`). No `test-writer` dispatch: an internal CLI's argv, stderr and exit-code contract, no UI flow or HTTP API. `src/lib/git.ts`, `src/qa/completeness-claim-checker.ts`, `docs/qa/secret-scan-allowlist.json` and the CI workflow are untouched.

- **Before.** `parseMarkerCorpusField` and `parseContinuationResidualField` picked the first `--field=` token with `Array.find`, so a second token was ignored: the probe answered for the first field and exited 0.
- **After.** Each parser filters the `--field=` tokens and, when more than one is present, throws an `Error` that quotes every token (`JSON.stringify`, comma-joined). The check runs before value validation, so a duplicate that also carries a bad value reports the duplicate. The process exits non-zero with nothing on stdout, as the stray-argument gate does (Issues #173 and #179).
- **Identical repeats are rejected too** (Manager ruling, human-preapproved): a repeated `--field=total` is an error, not a harmless no-op.
- **Placement.** Inside the two parsers, where the ambiguity is resolved, not in `assertKnownArgs`: `--field=` is a recognized flag, so that gate's contract and message are unchanged. Both probes' `main()` already call the parser before any file collection, so the rejection precedes every git call. No shared helper; valid invocations and the existing error messages are unchanged.
- **Tests** (one block per test file; no existing test edited, and the only existing line touched is the marker test's `node:path` import, widened to add `dirname`): a unit test for two or more distinct tokens in either order, a unit test for an identical repeat, and a real-subprocess test run from a non-git directory (`GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES` set) that asserts non-zero exit, both tokens on stderr, empty stdout and no `not a git repository` in stderr. The marker test file gained a mirrored `withNonGitDir` helper and a `dirname` import.
- **Mutation proofs, run by hand on each probe and restored byte-identical.** Deleting the duplicate check turns all three new tests red; relaxing it to reject only differing values turns the identical-repeat unit test and the subprocess test red; moving the parser call after file collection turns the subprocess test red (stderr becomes git's error and the tokens are absent).

### Fixed — `s1-227-cleanup-race` (Issue #227): `pre-commit-scan.test.ts` hardened against the temp-dir cleanup race (`ENOTEMPTY`)

STANDARD tier (ratified by the Manager; scope `s1-227-cleanup-race`). Test-only change. No `test-writer` dispatch: no UI flow or API surface. The code change is `src/secret-scan/pre-commit-scan.test.ts` only (the branch also carries state, run-log, plan and review files); `pre-commit-scan.ts`, the allowlist, `ci.yml` and `src/lib/git.ts` are untouched.

- **Cause (inferred, not captured on the runner).** Git spawns a detached `git maintenance run --auto` after a successful `git commit`, and it can still be writing under `.git/objects/info/` when the test's `finally` deletes the clone. A single recursive delete then fails with `ENOTEMPTY`. Only the test that makes a successful commit in a clone of the real repo does this; a commit refused by the hook never reaches that step.
- **Retry.** A local `removeTree(dir)` helper in the test file calls the recursive delete with `maxRetries: 5, retryDelay: 100`. It has no catch: a failure that outlasts the retries still throws and still fails the test.
- **Cause removal.** The `noprepare` test clones with `-c gc.auto=0 -c maintenance.auto=false`, so no maintenance process is spawned in that clone. `git config --local` in the clone returns `0` and `false`. The isolated-repo tests in the file also commit, but they are covered by the retry, not by the `-c` arguments; only the `noprepare` test's clone gets the arguments.
- **Scope of the routing.** Every temp-directory cleanup in `pre-commit-scan.test.ts` goes through `removeTree`. Generated by grep, not typed: `grep -nE '\b(rm|rmSync|rmdir|rmdirSync)\s*\(' src/secret-scan/pre-commit-scan.test.ts` reports one match, the call inside `removeTree` itself.
- **Assertions untouched.** `git diff -U0` on the file shows no added or removed `assert` or `test(` line; the file still runs 13 tests, 0 failed, 0 skipped.

Measured on Windows, Node 24, git 2.54 (the runner is Linux, Node 22, git 2.55; the race itself is not reproduced here): a concurrent writer recreating files during the delete makes the bare recursive delete fail with `ENOTEMPTY` in the large majority of trials and the retrying form fail in none (40 trials per configuration; raw counts in the S2 row of `docs/plans/S1-227-cleanup-race-phase1-2026-09-19.md` and in the dated review reports in `docs/reviews/`). A trace of the successful commit shows the maintenance spawn in a default clone and none in the clone made with the two `-c` arguments. No permanent test pins either finding; the evidence is in that plan and those reports.

**Disclosure.** Node's recursive delete applies `maxRetries` at each directory level, so a persistent (non-transient) failure surfaces much later than 1.5 s. `code-reviewer` measured 1.5 s, 10.8 s and 66 s at 1, 2 and 3 nested levels; deeper paths were not run, and no figure beyond those is claimed. It still rejects with the original error, so the test still fails. Whether to switch to a flat retry loop is tracked in Issue #231.

Not part of this change: other test and source files in the repository still call the recursive delete directly. Tracked as Issue #231. QA-14's standing red is Issue #229.

### Fixed — `s1-qa-probe-hardening` (Issues #175, #179, #182): the two QA-14 probes now agree on their file list, reject stray arguments, and disclose untracked files in the count

STANDARD tier (ratified by the Manager; scope `s1-qa-probe-hardening`). No `test-writer` dispatch: an internal CLI's argv and stderr contract, no UI flow or HTTP API. `src/lib/git.ts` and `docs/qa/secret-scan-allowlist.json` are untouched.

- **#179, `continuation-residual-probe.ts` fails loud on a stray argument.** A token that is not `--field=...` (a leftover ref, `HEAD~5`, `--bogus-flag`) used to print a count and exit 0. It now exits non-zero, names the token on stderr (with a Node stack trace, as the twin probe's gate does), prints nothing on stdout, and is rejected before any file collection. Valid invocations are unchanged. The new `assertKnownArgs` mirrors the twin probe's and is deliberately not shared.
- **#175, `continuation-residual-probe.ts` counts untracked scannable files.** Its file list came from `git ls-tree HEAD` while content came from the working tree, so a new untracked file was invisible. The list now comes from `git.lsFilesWorkingTree()`, the source `marker-corpus-probe.ts` has used since Issue #172. Submodule gitlinks and untracked nested repositories stay excluded.
- **#182, both probes warn on stderr when untracked files are inside the count.** New shared `src/qa/untracked-scan-warning.ts` names the number of untracked scannable files and lists up to 10 paths, one call per probe's `main()`. Human ruling, 2026-09-19: the number, stdout and exit code are unchanged, so `[[completeness: ...]]` markers and `KNOWN_INSTRUMENTS` keep reading the same single integer. This supersedes the "disclosed, not fixed" comment recorded for #182 on 2026-09-13.

> **Behavior change.** `--field=continuation-residual` now sends citations found in untracked files to `gh`, so a large scratch file can push the run past the distinct-issue cap (`QA14_MAX_ISSUES`, default 300) and fail loud with exit 1. The stderr warning names the untracked files behind the change.

Tests: T179-1..4, T175-1..2, TW-1..8 (`untracked-scan-warning.test.ts`, fake `Runner`), TWR-1..3 (real subprocess in isolated `mkdtemp` repos). The earlier test that pinned the silent-accept behavior as a known gap is replaced by T179-3, the ordered successor its own comment named. Mutations applied by hand and shown red: removed and reordered gate, relaxed gate, list source reverted, git arguments changed, each filter and the path cap dropped, warning moved to stdout, warning call removed, warning changing exit code or count.

Not part of this change: #226 (a duplicated `--field=` resolves first-wins) stays its own Issue.
