### Added — Issue #63: AST-based hardening for `kernel-purity-check.ts`'s forbidden-globals scan

STANDARD tier (ratified by the Manager, `docs/run-log.jsonl`'s tier-ratified event, scope `kernel-purity-ast-hardening`). No `test-writer` dispatch: a QA/CI instrument's own internal detection logic, no new/changed UI flow or API surface — covered by `story-implementer`'s own unit tests per this project's test-first carve-out.

Closes the disclosed residual from the prior Issue #63 fix-now (regex-only widening): a literal-text regex cannot see a forbidden global reached through an alias, a destructure, or a computed/bracket property access.

- `src/qa/kernel-purity-check.ts`: new `scanForbiddenGlobalsAst(source)` — a syntax-only TypeScript AST scan (`ts.createSourceFile`, no type-checker/`Program`) that runs ADDITIVELY alongside the existing regex layer (`scanForbiddenGlobals`); both feed the same `"forbidden-global"` violation kind, wired together in `scanFileContent`. Resolves identifier ALIASES and reassignments — including through `as`/`satisfies`/non-null (`!`) and legacy `<Type>expr` casts, this codebase's own idiom for indexing `globalThis`/`global` under strict TS — back to nine tracked forbidden roots: `globalThis`, `global`, `Reflect`, `eval`, `Function`, plus (Manager Q1 ruling, extending ADR-0021 POL-11's own named forbidden categories: network, timer, process-spawning) `fetch`, `setTimeout`, `setInterval`, `require`. Flags:
  - a destructure bound directly off a forbidden root (`const { get } = Reflect`) — the destructure itself is the violation;
  - a property or computed/bracket element access on a forbidden root or any tracked alias of one, transitively (`const g = globalThis; g["eval"]`) — a bracket key is constant-folded (a string literal, or a `+`-chain of string literals only) to describe it in the violation detail; a non-literal/dynamic key is still flagged, since the base object reached is what's forbidden.
- Disclosed residual (documented in the file's own header comment, matching `stripComments`'s existing "heuristic-only" disclosure convention): a fully dynamic/runtime-computed identifier or property name is inherently unresolvable by static analysis; a bare call reached directly through a tracked alias with no further property/element access on it (e.g. `const f = fetch; f(url)`) is out of this layer's current scope. Neither is a defect — both are inherent-limit/scope disclosures, same footing as the regex layer's own literal-text limits.
- AC5 (renamed import, e.g. `import { spawn as s } from "node:child_process"`) needed no new production code — already covered by the existing `classifyImport` non-relative-import check, which keys on the import specifier, never the local binding name; confirmed by a regression fixture + test only.
- New fixtures: `src/qa/selftest-fixture/kernel-purity/violating/{aliased-globalthis,destructured-reflect,computed-bracket-access,renamed-import,aliased-network-timer}.ts`; `clean/{safe-lookalike-alias,safe-destructure,safe-bracket-access}.ts`.
- 19 new tests in `src/qa/kernel-purity-check.test.ts` (43 total, up from 24) — direct unit tests on `scanForbiddenGlobalsAst` (proving the AST layer blocks independently of the regex layer), plus `checkKernelPurity` integration tests against the new fixtures. Zero edits to existing test bodies/behavior; the pre-existing 24 tests pass unmodified.
- No `package.json` change: `typescript` was already a `^5.7.3` devDependency (used for `tsc --noEmit`); this story is its first runtime `import` of the package, resolved the same way in CI (`npm ci` installs devDependencies).

Full `npm test`: 867/869 pass — the 2 failures (`AC1-b` connector-fixture pin, `QA-15` STATE.md bare-claim regression) are pre-existing on `master` (confirmed identical, 848/850, via a direct master checkout in this same working tree) and unrelated to this change; `QA-15` is already being fixed on a separate branch (`fix/state-md-bare-claim-regression`), out of this story's scope.

**Fix-now round-1, this session — closes GitHub Issues #211 and #210, both filed independently by `app-security-reviewer` and concurred by `cross-domain-reviewer`, plus the tier-ratification/state-transition gap `cross-domain-reviewer` found code-traced:**
- **Issue #211 [HIGH] fixed:** `detectUsages` only walked `PropertyAccessExpression`/`ElementAccessExpression` — a forbidden root aliased then called directly with no further property/element access (`const f = fetch; f(url)`) passed both layers with zero detections, for exactly the 4 roots (`fetch`/`setTimeout`/`setInterval`/`require`) the Q1 ruling added to close ADR-0021 POL-11's network/timer/process-spawning categories — the dominant real invocation shape for those 4 roots, nullifying the ruling's own stated purpose. Fixed: new `detectDirectCallViolation` handles `ts.isCallExpression`, flagging a call whose callee (cast-unwrapped) is an identifier resolving via the alias map to any forbidden root — same shape as the existing property/element-access branches. `aliased-network-timer.ts`'s fixture gained 4 new direct-call functions (one per root); `kernel-purity-check.test.ts` gained regression tests covering each root, both bare and aliased.
- **Issue #210 [MED] fixed:** the classic `({}).constructor.constructor("return this")()` sandbox-escape idiom reached `globalThis`/arbitrary code with zero detections from either layer — no tracked root identifier appears anywhere, so alias/root tracking structurally can't catch it. Fixed: new `detectConstructorPivotViolation`, a narrow, root-independent check — any adjacent `.constructor.constructor(...)` property-access-then-call chain is itself the violation, regardless of the base expression. Verified zero false positives against this repo's real `src/policy/kernel/**` production code and every `clean/` self-test fixture before committing to it (no `.constructor` usage exists anywhere in either). New violating fixture `constructor-pivot.ts` (2 functions) + clean false-positive-guard fixture `safe-single-constructor-access.ts` (single-level `.constructor` access/call is NOT flagged). Header comment's disclosed-residual paragraph updated to name the real, narrower remaining residual honestly: splitting the chain across two variable declarations (`const step1 = ({}).constructor; const step2 = step1.constructor; step2(...)`) is NOT caught, since the check is syntactic/adjacent-token-only and does not track aliases of `.constructor` itself — confirmed by a dedicated disclosed-residual test, not assumed either way.
- **Mutation-verified, not just passing:** both fixes' new tests (5 unit tests + 2 integration tests) were confirmed to fail against the pre-fix code — `git stash` isolated `kernel-purity-check.ts` back to its pre-fix-now state, re-ran the full test file: exactly the 5 new Issue #211/#210 unit tests and 2 new integration tests failed (45/50 pass), every other test (including all pre-existing ones) stayed green — then restored the fix and reran: 50/50 pass.

**Fix-now round-2, this session — closes the bracket-notation gap in Issue #210's re-confirm:**
- **Generalized the constructor-pivot detector, not patched with a second parallel branch:** `asConstructorPropertyAccess` replaced with `asConstructorAccess`, which resolves EITHER notation at each hop of the two-deep chain — a `.constructor` property access, OR an element access whose key constant-folds (via the existing `foldStringConcat` helper, the same one used for `globalThis["proc"+"ess"]` detection) to the literal string `"constructor"`. `obj.constructor.constructor(...)`, `obj["constructor"]["constructor"](...)`, and any mixed dot/bracket chain now all funnel through the same check.
- New violating-fixture functions `pivotViaBracketNotation`/`pivotViaMixedNotation` in `constructor-pivot.ts`; new clean false-positive-guard functions `describeCtorNameBracket`/`callSingleConstructorBracket` in `safe-single-constructor-access.ts` (a single-level bracket-notation `.constructor` access/call is still NOT flagged).
- 3 new tests in `kernel-purity-check.test.ts` (53 total, up from 50): a unit test covering the three bracket/mixed variants, a bracket-notation false-positive-guard unit test, and an integration test asserting the VIOLATING fixture now reports exactly 4 constructor-pivot findings (2 dot-notation from round-1 + 2 bracket/mixed from this round) while the CLEAN fixture stays clean.
- Header comment's disclosed-residual paragraph corrected again, honestly: the bracket-notation gap is now closed; the narrower variable-split residual (splitting either notation's chain across two variable declarations) is unchanged and still not caught, since the check remains syntactic/adjacent-token-only and does not track aliases of `.constructor` itself.

### Changed — `friendly-halt-messages`: human-readable labels on SUR-03 halt messages

CRITICAL tier (ratified unchanged: `hooks/userpromptsubmit-halt-relay.mjs`/`hooks/sessionstart-tool-enum.mjs` are CLAUDE.md's named "Policy enforcement / session gates" sensitive area, regardless of edit size; `docs/run-log.jsonl`'s 2026-09-17T23:56:01 tier-ratified event, scope `friendly-halt-messages`). No `test-writer` dispatch: no new UI flow or API surface, only the wording of an existing operator-facing hook message.

- **`hooks/userpromptsubmit-halt-relay.mjs`:** new `FRIENDLY_LABELS` frozen map + `friendlyLabelFor(reasonKey)`, used as `describeActiveReasons()`'s message-line prefix in place of the raw, hyphenated reason-key string. Covers the SUR-03-owned reason keys below:
  - `SUR-03-unclassified-tool` → "Unrecognized tool"
  - `SUR-03-unclassified-connector` → "Unrecognized connector"
  - `SUR-03-enumeration-failed` → "Tool/connector check failed"
  - `SUR-03-central-fixture-expired` → "Allowlist exemption expired"

  An unmapped reason key falls back to showing its own raw key as the label (unchanged fallback shape, mirroring `unlockHintFor`'s own existing generic fallback). `UNLOCK_HINTS`/`unlockHintFor`/`sanitizeDetail`/`blockWithMessage`/`inspectHaltState` untouched.
- **`hooks/sessionstart-tool-enum.mjs`:** new `quoteNames(names)` helper, individually double-quoting each unclassified tool/connector name (`"foo", "bar"`), used at both `reconcileReason(...)` call sites for `SUR-03-unclassified-tool`/`SUR-03-unclassified-connector`. The previous redundant `unclassified:`/`connector identity present:` detail-text prefixes are dropped — the relay's new friendly label already carries that meaning. `ENUMERATION_FAILED_REASON_KEY`/`FIXTURE_EXPIRED_REASON_KEY` detail construction untouched.

**Two edge cases shipped unhandled, by explicit ratification (no escaping/truncation-boundary logic):** a literal `"` inside a tool/connector name breaks the visual quote boundary; `sanitizeDetail`'s own 200-char cap may truncate a long multi-name list mid-quote.

**Verification, real:** `npm run typecheck`/`npm run lint` clean; `npm test` **842/842 pass, 0 fail, 0 skipped** (834 carried + 8 new, `hooks/userpromptsubmit-halt-relay-friendly-labels.test.ts` 5, `hooks/sessionstart-tool-enum-friendly-labels.test.ts` 3); both new files RED-CONFIRMED before implementation (7/8 failing on the unimplemented behavior, the 8th — the unmapped-key fallback — passing by construction since it required no change). ADR cache `HIT` (35 ADRs, fingerprint `83b2e3e`, unchanged — no ADR-relevant surface touched). test-writer's own `hooks/userpromptsubmit-halt-relay.test.ts`, `hooks/userpromptsubmit-halt-relay-fixnow.test.ts`, `hooks/sessionstart-tool-enum.test.ts`, `hooks/sessionstart-tool-enum-fixnow.test.ts` left completely unmodified and confirmed still passing.

### Added — Path B: a standing, blocking git pre-commit secret-scan check on the simulated-commit tree
CRITICAL tier (ratified unchanged: `src/secret-scan/*` is CLAUDE.md's named "Secret scanning / CI gates" sensitive area, same file family as `cifix`'s own CRITICAL-tier precedent every round; `docs/run-log.jsonl`'s 2026-09-14T17:06:26 tier-ratified event, scope `path-b-precommit-secret-scan`). Reviewers: `red-team` + `app-security-reviewer` + `cross-domain-reviewer`. No `test-writer` dispatch: a git pre-commit hook / CLI instrument, same class as this project's existing QA instruments — no UI flow, no API surface.

Closes the "green in the working tree, red in the commit" gap OSS-01's full-history scan cannot catch pre-commit by construction, demonstrated twice (GitHub Issues #131, #180; `docs/backlog.md`'s cifix-council Path B item).

- **`src/secret-scan/simulated-commit.ts`** (new): builds the git commit that WOULD be created by `git commit` right now — staged changes applied on top of `HEAD` — entirely via git plumbing (`GIT_INDEX_FILE`-scoped `read-tree HEAD` → per-path `update-index --cacheinfo`/`--force-remove` → `write-tree` → `commit-tree -p HEAD`), never touching the real `.git/index` or `HEAD`. Content for every staged path is resolved from the **real index's own staged blob** (`git ls-files -s`, read-only) — **human-ruled 2026-09-14 (`docs/decisions.md`) over the plan's own literal `git add`-the-staged-paths sketch**, which re-reads the working tree and would silently diverge from what `git commit` actually commits whenever a staged file is further edited before commit (a genuine false-negative/false-positive risk on a security gate, self-flagged during Phase 1 planning). Two regression tests pin this directly, in both directions (a clean-staged/dirty-working-tree file scans clean; a secret-staged/cleaned-working-tree file still scans as a match). Renames/copies/deletions handled correctly via `git diff --cached --name-status -z` parsing.
- **`src/secret-scan/pre-commit-scan.ts`** (new): scans the simulated commit's own tree (not its ancestry — `scanHistory`'s `revList` overridden to return exactly the one simulated commit sha) with OSS-01's existing, **completely unmodified** `scanHistory`/`loadAllowlist`/`summarizeMatches`/`SECRET_PATTERNS` (`history-scan.ts`) — zero new exemption-grant surface, `docs/qa/secret-scan-allowlist.json` stays the sole mechanism, no in-file marker, no second grant path. Single-tree scope keeps this proportional to tree size (not full history length), fast enough for every real commit.
- **`.githooks/pre-commit` + `package.json`'s `"prepare": "git config core.hooksPath .githooks"`**: a tracked `core.hooksPath` directory, not a copied `.git/hooks/pre-commit` shim — chosen so the hook never goes stale relative to its own committed source (no re-copy step to forget), and needs zero new dependencies (rules out `husky`). Runs automatically on `npm install`/`npm ci`, confirmed by a real, live `npm ci` run against a fresh local clone this session (not assumed): the `prepare` script fired, `core.hooksPath` was set, and — self-demonstrating the exact mechanism this story built — the resulting real `git commit` correctly refused both a planted canary secret AND this story's own then-unallowlisted test fixtures (see the `qa` entry below).
- `package.json`: `oss:pre-commit-scan` script alias (manual-run convenience, matches `oss:secret-scan`'s naming).
- `src/lib/exec.ts`: `Runner` gains an optional per-call `env` override, merged over the existing cleaned `process.env` copy — needed to scope `GIT_INDEX_FILE` to individual git-plumbing calls without ever mutating the parent process's own `process.env` (SE ADR-0003: inject I/O-performing dependencies, don't reach for global mutable state). Two new regression tests confirm the override reaches the subprocess and the parent's own env is untouched.
- ~~23 new tests (`simulated-commit.test.ts` 14, `pre-commit-scan.test.ts` 9) plus 2 in `exec.test.ts`~~ **CORRECTED (round-1 fix-now review, red-team editorial finding): `simulated-commit.test.ts` had 13 `test(` calls at that commit, not 14 — the round-1 section below gives the current, freshly-counted total instead of repeating a frozen figure.** All real-subprocess against isolated `mkdtemp` git repo fixtures (`R4`'s two fresh-clone tests use a real local `git clone --local` of this project itself, ~11s, confirmed to actually install and actually block).

**Ratified acceptance criteria R1-R8, explicitly named (red-team/cross-domain round-1 finding #186/finding-2: R2, R3, R7 were previously covered only implicitly in this entry's prose):**
- **R1** (simulated tree via git plumbing, real HEAD/index untouched, `scanHistory` reused unmodified) — `simulated-commit.ts`/`pre-commit-scan.ts` above.
- **R2** (zero new exemption-grant surface) — `pre-commit-scan.ts` imports `loadAllowlist`/`summarizeMatches`/`scanHistory` from `history-scan.ts` unmodified; `docs/qa/secret-scan-allowlist.json` stays the sole exemption mechanism, no in-file marker, no second grant path.
- **R3** (real, blocking `git commit` refusal) — the installed `.githooks/pre-commit` hook; `pre-commit-scan.test.ts`'s R3 tests exercise a real `git commit` against a real installed hook.
- **R4** (repeatable install) — `core.hooksPath` + `package.json`'s `prepare` script; live `npm ci` fresh-clone verification below.
- **R5** (`.github/workflows/ci.yml` untouched) — confirmed via `git diff --stat`, empty, every round.
- **R6** (the 3 Issue #136 `patterns.test.ts` allowlist entries untouched) — confirmed via `git diff`, every round.
- **R7** (sensitive-area review ceremony) — CRITICAL tier ratified; `red-team` + `app-security-reviewer` + `cross-domain-reviewer` dispatched, reports in `docs/reviews/path-b-precommit-secret-scan-{red-team,cross-domain,app-security}-2026-09-14.md`.
- **R8** (close the Issue #181 residual loop) — `docs/qa/recurring-findings-registry.md`'s row updated.

**Deliberately out of scope, per the ratified plan:** R5/R6 above.

**qa (self-demonstrating dogfood, this story's own live run):** the manual `npm ci` fresh-clone verification above caught this story's own new test fixtures (`simulated-commit.test.ts`, `pre-commit-scan.test.ts`) — synthetic AWS-key-shaped/reserved-test-domain-email literals — as real, correctly-detected, then-unallowlisted matches, exactly the class Issues #131/#180 already named. 4 new `docs/qa/secret-scan-allowlist.json` entries added (same convention as every prior story's own test fixtures) before the diff was clean.

**Verification, real:** `npm run typecheck`/`npm run lint` clean; `npm test` **810/810 pass, 0 fail, 0 skipped** (785 carried + 25 new); `node src/qa/completeness-claim-checker.ts` PASS (2 files); `node src/qa/recurring-findings-registry.ts` PASS (2 classes logged); `node src/qa/broken-instrument-gate.ts` VACUOUS-PASS (0 registered, disclosed); ADR cache `HIT` (35 ADRs, fingerprint `83b2e3e`, unchanged — no ADR-relevant surface touched). `history-scan.test.ts`'s own real-repo dogfood test (full-history OSS-01 scan against the real, current allowlist) stays a clean pass.

**Known, disclosed limitation (red-team finding 6, round-1): `git commit --no-verify` bypasses this hook entirely** (skips any git hook — not specific to this one), as does a commit made by a client with hooks disabled, or one made before `npm install`/`npm ci` has ever run. No local git hook can prevent this; it is a defense-in-depth, pre-commit-only backstop, not an unbypassable control. `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s OSS-01 full-history scan (unaffected by any local bypass, and the only check a server-side merge ever runs) is the real backstop. Now stated explicitly in `simulated-commit.ts`'s and `.githooks/pre-commit`'s own comments too, not just here.

**Stage-3 round-1 result: `red-team` no-go (2 HIGH + 4 MED), `cross-domain-reviewer` APPROVE-WITH-CONDITIONS (2 MED), `app-security-reviewer` APPROVE (clean).** Reports: `docs/reviews/path-b-precommit-secret-scan-{red-team,cross-domain,app-security}-2026-09-14.md`; `docs/decisions.md`'s 2026-09-14 round-1 row. Human preapproved autonomous fix-now continuation on every finding (unambiguous defects, no ruling needed).

**Fix-now round-1, this session:**
- **Issue #187 [HIGH] fixed:** `.githooks/pre-commit` was committed mode `100644` — git silently ignores a non-executable hook (`githooks(5)`), so the gate was structurally inert on every POSIX/CI clone while passing every test on this Windows dev box (git-for-windows ignores the exec bit entirely, the opposite failure direction). Fixed via `git update-index --chmod=+x .githooks/pre-commit` (now `100755` in the committed tree). New regression test asserts the TRACKED mode directly (`git ls-files -s`), since no test that exercises the hook can detect this on this platform.
- **Issue #188 [HIGH] fixed:** `resolveStagedBlob`'s per-path `git ls-files -s -- <path>` call had `<path>` interpreted as a git **pathspec**, not a literal (`--` disables option parsing, not pathspec magic) — a staged filename containing `[`, `]`, `*`, or `?` (e.g. `k[0-9].js`) could match a lexicographically-earlier sibling, and `.find()` silently took the first (wrong) blob. Red-team demonstrated a live secret leak end-to-end through the real installed hook: `k[0-9].js` (secret) staged alongside `k5.js` (clean) scanned as PASS and the secret landed in real history. Fixed per red-team's own suggested shape: replaced every per-path call with ONE unfiltered `git ls-files -s -z`, parsed into a `Map<path, {mode, sha}>`, each staged path then looked up by an exact key — removes the glob-interpretation class entirely and cuts subprocess spawns from O(staged paths) to 1. New regression test pins the exact attack shape; independently re-run live against a fresh local clone with the real installed hook — the secret is now correctly caught (`FAIL`, non-zero exit, nothing landed) rather than silently passing.
- **Issue #189 [MED] fixed:** applying `git diff --cached --name-status` entries in reported (path-sorted) order could apply an `A <file>` before a `D <dir>/<child>` for the same path segment, hitting `update-index fatal: <path> appears as both a file and as a directory` on a directory→file (or file→directory) refactor a real `git commit` accepts without complaint — a permanent, no-unlock block. Fixed via two-pass application: every deletion first, then every add/modify (the same restructuring that made #188's fix trivial to extend, per red-team's own note). Two new regression tests cover both directions (directory→file, file→directory).
- **Issue #190 [MED] fixed:** `pre-commit-scan.ts`'s `main()` had no `try/catch` — every internal failure (empty repo, a git plumbing error) dumped a raw Node stack trace instead of a named, one-line message (PRINCIPLES rule 2). Exit code was already correct (fails closed); this is presentation only. Wrapped `main()`: prints `[Path B pre-commit-scan] BLOCKED: ...` naming what failed, keeps the non-zero exit. New regression test asserts a clean one-line message with no stack-trace markers.
- **Issue #191 [MED] fixed:** `"prepare": "git config core.hooksPath .githooks"` failed `npm ci`/`npm install` hard (exit 128, "not in a git directory") in any install context without a `.git` directory (a future Docker `COPY`-only layer, a tarball install). Extracted into a real, tested module, `src/lib/git-hooks-install.ts` (`installGitHooks`, SE ADR-0003 dependency injection) — a missing `.git` directory is now a quiet no-op (exit 0), not a hard failure. `package.json`'s `prepare` now calls this module. New regression tests cover both the no-`.git` case (via a direct CLI invocation, not a full `npm ci` — real, but cheaper) and the real-repo install case.
- **Issue #187 finding 8 [LOW] fixed (folded in, cheap):** the same `installGitHooks` module now detects and warns (not blocks) when it overwrites a pre-existing, different `core.hooksPath` (e.g. husky, a manually installed Gitleaks hook per devops ADR-0008) — previously silent. Reports the prior value and how to restore it. New regression test pins this.
- **Red-team suspicion #7 [MED->settled] addressed:** `git commit -a`/`-- <path>` were already correct, but only because of an undocumented, unpinned `GIT_INDEX_FILE` env-passthrough in `src/lib/exec.ts`'s `cleanSubprocessEnv()` — a future hardening to strip `GIT_*` keys would have silently reverted `git commit -am` to a false negative on the single most common commit form. Now documented as load-bearing at its exact point of mechanism (`exec.ts`) and at its point of reliance (`simulated-commit.ts`'s header), and pinned by a new real end-to-end `git commit -am` regression test.
- **Red-team suspicion #9 [LOW, disclosed, not fixed]:** temp-index cleanup is a `finally` block (covers throws, not a SIGINT mid-run) — disclosed in `simulated-commit.ts`'s header (contents are an index file, never secret bytes; reclaimed by ordinary temp-dir cleanup). Not independently testable without a flaky signal-timing drill; the cost/benefit didn't justify it this round.
- **`docs/.maat-state.json` scope transition (Issue #185) and round-counter bump:** already committed by the Manager before this round began (`1a63416`, `1aabb2c`) — not touched again here.
- **Second dogfood catch, this round (same mechanism, same class as Issues #131/#180/round-0's own catch):** the Manager's own committed Stage-3 round-1 review reports (`docs/reviews/path-b-precommit-secret-scan-{red-team,app-security}-2026-09-14.md`) quote this story's synthetic secret/email fixtures as required verbatim evidence (PRINCIPLES rule 19) — 4 more `docs/qa/secret-scan-allowlist.json` entries added the same way, before the real full-history scan (`node src/secret-scan/history-scan.ts`) went clean again.
- **Verification, real (round-1):** `npm run typecheck`/`npm run lint` clean; full suite `npm test` **822/822 pass, 0 fail, 0 skipped** (810 measured after the original build, +12 net this round — freshly counted, not derived: `simulated-commit.test.ts` 17, `pre-commit-scan.test.ts` 12, `exec.test.ts` 5, `git-hooks-install.test.ts` 6 (new file) are this round's own current per-file totals); `node src/secret-scan/history-scan.ts` PASS, 0 blocking (663 allowlisted); `node src/qa/completeness-claim-checker.ts` PASS; ADR cache `HIT` (35 ADRs, fp `83b2e3e`, unchanged — no ADR-relevant surface touched by this round). `#187`/`#188`'s fixes re-verified against red-team's own exact attack shapes, live, through the real installed hook against a fresh local clone (not just the unit tests): the glob-pathspec attack now correctly fails the gate (`FAIL`, non-zero, nothing lands) instead of silently passing.

**Stage-3 round-2 result: `red-team` go (clean-class, mutation-tested every round-1 fix) + `cross-domain-reviewer` APPROVE — round-1's findings (#187-#192) confirmed genuinely fixed, both closed completed.** Reports in `docs/reviews/path-b-precommit-secret-scan-{red-team,cross-domain}-round2-2026-09-14.md`; `docs/decisions.md`'s round-2 row. Red-team also found a real recurrence of this project's own most-repeated defect class (Issue #193, MED, security-class) and a second MED (Issue #194) — human preapproved autonomous fix-now continuation on both.

**Fix-now round-3, this session:**
- **Issue #193 [MED, security-class] fixed:** round-1's own fix-now quoted a fake AWS-key-shaped fixture literal in two `docs/qa/secret-scan-allowlist.json` `reason` fields, and added a self-referential `aws-access-key-id` grant on that file. Since `partitionAllowlisted` matches by `{path, patternId}` only, that grant blanket-exempted **every future** `aws-access-key-id` match anywhere in that file — red-team staged a DISTINCT, real-shaped key literal in a completely different `reason` field and it committed clean through both this pre-commit hook and CI's full-history scan. Fixed per red-team's own recommended, already-proven-to-work shape: the two `reason` fields reworded to describe the fixture literal instead of reproducing it, and the self-referential grant deleted entirely. Two new regression tests in `src/secret-scan/history-scan.test.ts` (`docs/qa/recurring-findings-registry.md`'s new row, below): (1) the allowlist file never grants itself a credential-shaped pattern (`aws-access-key-id`/`aws-secret-access-key`/`github-pat`/`github-fine-grained-pat`/`slack-token`/`private-key-block`/`generic-password-assignment` — every `SECRET_PATTERNS` id except the lower-severity `internal-hostname`/`ipv4-private`/`email-address`, where this project's own established convention already tolerates self-quoting); (2) the allowlist file's own raw text contains zero live credential-shaped matches anywhere, regardless of which entry's field it would sit in — a per-entry-own-patternId check alone would miss a secret hidden in an unrelated entry's `reason` field while a blanket grant for that pattern still exists elsewhere in the file. A third test proves both guards are mutation-sensitive (a planted literal/self-grant IS detected). **This is the false-negative sibling of GitHub Issue #136** (cifix, 2026-09-09 — same root cause: an allowlist entry exempts a pattern in a file forever, not just the literal that motivated it); logged as a new `docs/qa/recurring-findings-registry.md` row (2nd occurrence, QA-13), Status disclosed honestly as partially promoted: a real lint now guards the specific "allowlist file self-grants a credential pattern" sub-case, while Issue #136's own general class (any allowlist entry broader than intended) stays `pending lint`, since its own 3 `patterns.test.ts` entries remain deliberately unfixed (human-ruled ACCEPT-AS-IS, out of this story's scope per R6).
- **Issue #194 [MED] fixed (claim corrected + cheap UX fix, latency optimization itself deliberately out of scope):** `pre-commit-scan.ts` claimed the check was "fast enough to run on every real `git commit`" with no measurement (PRINCIPLES rule 18). Measured, not assumed: ~23-25s across 5 consecutive runs this session on this repo's own 312-tracked-file tree (red-team's own independent measurement the same day, on 310 files, was ~12.5-13.0s — both real, machine/session-dependent numbers for the same O(tree size) `history-scan.ts` `git cat-file`-per-blob architecture; root-caused by red-team, not reimplemented/optimized here — a separate, larger change to shared OSS-01 machinery). Module header now states this honestly instead of asserting an unmeasured property. Cheap, zero-risk UX fix folded in: a PASSING run's stdout no longer lists every `ALLOWLISTED` match (measured: on this repo's own clean commits, the `ALLOWLISTED` lines made up nearly the entire output) — `summarizeMatches`'s own result is unchanged (R2 still holds), only what this CLI prints on a clean run; a FAILING run still prints every detail line. New regression test pins both the suppression and the still-full FAIL-case output; the pre-existing R2 CLI-level test updated to match (the underlying "still reported, not silently dropped" property stays tested, just at the layer where it now actually lives).
- **3 LOW items folded in, cheap:**
  - `installGitHooks`'s guard now uses `git rev-parse --git-dir` instead of `existsSync(".git")` — the old probe silently skipped installing the security hook (exit 0) from any subdirectory of a real checkout. New regression test.
  - The claim that a Docker `COPY package*.json . && npm ci` layer is guarded was false (still hard-fails with `MODULE_NOT_FOUND` — no guard written inside a source file can run before that file exists on disk). Corrected in place: the module header now states exactly what IS guarded (a full source checkout with no `.git`) and explicitly discloses what a full fix would require (an inline, dependency-free `prepare` command, reintroducing the untestable one-liner shape Issue #191's own fix deliberately moved away from) — not attempted this round; this repo has no Dockerfile today (counted in code: neither of this repo's `npm ci` call sites is a COPY-only layer, both run after a full `actions/checkout`).
  - The `#190` `BLOCKED:` message named the cause but not the unlock (PRINCIPLES rule 2) — a brand-new repo's first-ever commit had no way forward. One added clause names it (`git commit --no-verify` once, for that specific case only). Regression test updated.
- **Verification, real (round-3):** `npm run typecheck`/`npm run lint` clean; full suite `npm test` **827/827 pass, 0 fail, 0 skipped** (822 carried + 5 net new this round); `node src/qa/completeness-claim-checker.ts` PASS; `node src/qa/recurring-findings-registry.ts` PASS (3 classes, the new Issue #136/#193 row included); red-team's own exact `#193` attack shape (a distinct key-shaped literal staged in `docs/qa/secret-scan-allowlist.json` itself) re-run live against the fixed code to confirm it is now actually caught, not just that the specific literals red-team used are gone — see the dedicated verification note below this entry.
- **Disclosed, not resolved this round — a real permission boundary, not a guess-patch:** fixing #193 by deleting the self-referential grant correctly closed the forward-looking vulnerability, but un-covered 2 of this branch's own earlier commits whose OWN historical version of `docs/qa/secret-scan-allowlist.json` still contained the raw literal the deleted grant used to cover — full-history scanning walks every commit forever, so those 2 blobs failed. `git filter-branch` and a `cherry-pick`-onto-new-branch alternative were both correctly blocked by the sandbox's `[Git Destructive]` classifier; stopped rather than working around it. 3 options presented to the human (`docs/decisions.md`'s matching round-3 row).

**Fix-now round-5, this session — closes GitHub Issue #199 (reopened) and Issue #200, both filed by `red-team`'s round-4 re-confirm (`docs/reviews/path-b-precommit-secret-scan-red-team-round4-2026-09-14.md`) against round-3's own #199 fix:**
- **Correction, stated plainly:** round-3's `c96a4d5` fix was real (`docs/STATE.md` genuinely has no live credential match, mutation-verified) but not generalized — its own `NARRATIVE_STATUS_FILES` was a hardcoded one-element array, the same hand-typed-opt-in shape Issue #199 itself was filed against. Red-team demonstrated a new `CHANGELOG.md` grant, a new `docs/decisions.md` row, and a new trailing comment in the already-granted `src/secret-scan/patterns.test.ts` each carrying a live AWS-key-shaped literal past the hook, `history-scan.ts`, and the full test suite.
- **Issue #199 (reopened) + Issue #200 fixed for real, this time by construction, not by widening a list:** `src/secret-scan/history-scan.test.ts` replaces the hardcoded file array with a check derived from the real `docs/qa/secret-scan-allowlist.json` every run (`deriveMutableCredentialGrants`) — every credential-shaped grant is in the checked set, minus exactly one named, in-code exclusion (a file under `docs/reviews/`, content-bounded by construction per PRINCIPLES rule 11 — a persisted report is never edited after the fact). Every remaining grant is checked against a pinned, sha256-hashed baseline of its already-reviewed occurrences (`REVIEWED_BASELINE`); a live match whose hash isn't in the baseline fails, whether it arrives via a brand-new grant (Issue #199's class) or a new occurrence in an already-granted file (Issue #200's class). `docs/decisions.md` and the 4 `*.test.ts` fixture files are no longer blanket-excluded — they're checked the same way as everything else, per red-team's own explicit direction.
- **Honest scope, stated once, so it doesn't need restating:** this closes the class red-team demonstrated (new/changed literal, `{path, patternId}`-granularity, credential-shaped patterns) — it does not, and cannot by this mechanism's own design, defend against a secret that never matches a `SECRET_PATTERNS` regex shape, or a swap where an old reviewed literal is deleted and a new one with the exact same byte value happens to replace it identically (the hash would still match). Both are pre-existing, disclosed OSS-01 design properties, not new residuals from this fix.
- **F3 (the hand-derived-taxonomy root cause) fixed structurally, not by writing a smaller list:** there is no remaining hand-typed "N files, M categories" claim in this file's own comments for a future edit to drift out of sync with. A new self-check test runs this test file's own real text through QA-15's `completeness-claim-checker.ts` on every `npm test`, so a reintroduced un-instrumented completeness claim here fails the same way one would in `docs/STATE.md`/`CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Mutation-verified, live, in throwaway clones:** (1) neutering the derivation's `docs/reviews/` exclusion check (so it excludes everything) turns exactly the dedicated derive-function unit test red, nothing else — proving the checked set is genuinely computed, not vacuously satisfied by the real repo's current clean state; (2) neutering the baseline-hash comparison (always reporting zero unreviewed occurrences) turns exactly the two attack-shaped mutation-proof tests red. Both mutations reverted after confirming red. Also re-ran red-team's own three round-4 live attacks (a new `CHANGELOG.md` grant, a new `docs/decisions.md` row, a new `patterns.test.ts` trailing comment) against the fixed code in fresh throwaway clones: the pre-commit hook and `history-scan.ts` still pass on every one of them (unchanged, pre-existing whole-file-grant design — this guard is CI-only, same as the original #199 fix, disclosed and correct per red-team's own F6) but the new guard test in `npm test` now correctly fails on every one of them, where it previously passed every one of them clean.

**Fix-now round-2 (2026-09-13, Stage-3 review round-1 REWORK — `cross-domain-reviewer` 1 HIGH + 1 MED, `red-team` 3 MED — human approved a fix-now round covering every real finding from both reports):**

- **Issue #170 [HIGH]:** the round-1 regression test above asserted byte-identical stdout across two live subprocess spawns of the full-tree-scanning probe — demonstrated flaky under real `npm test` concurrency (1 fail / 6 full-suite runs). Root cause: `collectFullTreeFileTexts`'s `readFile` catch was a blanket `catch { continue; }`, silently treating ANY read failure — not just a genuinely-gone file — as absence, which could silently miscount under a transient I/O hiccup. Fixed in **both** this file and its twin, `continuation-residual-probe.ts` (confirmed the identical defect was live there too): the catch now checks `err.code === "ENOENT"` (legitimate skip) vs. anything else (real failure, now propagates/fails loud). The flaky test itself is replaced: a stray CLI argument now fails loud before any file-walk starts (folds in #173, next), so proving it needs only one subprocess spawn, never two racing ones; `continuation-residual-probe.test.ts`'s matching test was reduced from two spawns to one the same way.
- **Issue #172 [MED]:** #164's fix above closed the machine-visible half only — the file LIST still resolved via `resolveChangedFiles(..., "HEAD")` (a `git ls-tree HEAD` read) while CONTENT came from the working tree, so a genuinely untracked, uncommitted, scannable new file was silently invisible to the count (demonstrated: baseline `total=989`, an untracked file with 3 bare `#N` citations left it at `989`; the same 3 citations in a tracked file raised it to `992`). Fixed for real: a new `GitOps.lsFilesWorkingTree()` (`src/lib/git.ts`) — `git ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard`, with the `--cached` half additionally mode-filtered to exclude submodule gitlinks (mode `160000`, e.g. this repo's own `adr/` — reading it as a file throws `EISDIR`, caught by this fix's own new regression test on its first run) — replaces the `resolveChangedFiles`/`ls-tree` read, so list and content are now drawn from the same working-tree state. `continuation-residual-probe.ts` carries the identical, still-live defect — deliberately not fixed here alongside a differently-scoped Issue; filed separately as **Issue #175**.
- **Issue #173 [MED]:** a stray positional argument or unknown flag (`not-a-ref-at-all`, `HEAD~5`, `--bogus-flag`) used to be silently swallowed — still a working-tree answer, exit 0, no warning — contradicting this file's own documented fail-loud convention, and 4 committed review reports carry a ref-passing command as copy-pasteable text that would silently misbehave. Fixed: a new `assertKnownArgs()`, called first in `main()`, rejects any argv token that isn't `--field=marked|unmarked|total` with a non-zero exit and a clear stderr message. Scoped to `marker-corpus-probe.ts` only, per this round's instructions — `continuation-residual-probe.ts`'s own argv handling is unchanged.
- **Issue #171 [MED]:** GitHub Milestone #19 ("S1 — Protect the baseline")'s description was corrected via the GitHub API to disclose the QA-14 residual (comma/list-continuation classification, Issue #154) honestly, instead of a bare "SHIPPED" claim — pointing at the live `reference-resolver.ts` command rather than freezing a count, per this project's own moving-corpus-metric convention. Milestone left open (not re-closed here — the Manager's call once this story is merged and re-reviewed clean).

**Verification, real (round-2 fix-now):** `npm run typecheck`/`npm run lint` clean; `npm test` **779/779 pass, 0 fail, 0 skipped**, confirmed across 9 consecutive full-suite runs (6 at default concurrency, then 3 more at `--test-concurrency=32`), specifically to re-confirm #170's flake is actually gone, not just theoretically — zero flakes across every run; `node src/qa/completeness-claim-checker.ts` PASS (2 files); ADR cache `HIT` (35 ADRs, fingerprint `83b2e3e`, unchanged); zero new blocking QA-14 citations — blocking count unchanged (151 → 151, out of 3915 → 3922 total citations scanned before vs. after this round's diff — measured via `git stash` isolation, same command both times).

**Fix-now round-3 (2026-09-13, Stage-3 review round-2 no-go — `red-team` 1 HIGH + 4 MED + 1 LOW, `code-reviewer` 1 HIGH (the identical defect, independently reproduced) + 2 LOW — human approved this third and final fix-now round before PRINCIPLES rule 16(c)'s mandatory council would trigger on a fourth REWORK-class round):**

- **Issue #176 [HIGH]:** the round-2 `Issue #172` proof-test itself reintroduced the exact two-live-subprocess-racing-a-real-working-tree-write shape `Issue #170` was fixed for elsewhere in the same diff — the only test in this repo writing into this repo's own live checkout instead of an isolated `mkdtemp` fixture (5 other test files already use `mkdtemp`). Demonstrated failing 6/6 under two concurrent `node --test` processes on one checkout (red-team) and ~29% even in serial full-suite runs (code-reviewer, independently). Fixed: the test now runs against a throwaway `mkdtemp` git repo fixture (never this repo's checkout) and asserts via a **direct, in-process call** to `collectFullTreeFileTexts()` + `computeMarkerCorpusStats()` — no CLI subprocess spawn of the probe at all, so there is nothing left for a second concurrent test run to race against. `collectFullTreeFileTexts()` and a new `readFileTexts()` helper are now exported (were module-private) to make this direct call possible. Fixing this exposed one more latent bug: `collectFullTreeFileTexts()` never resolved a listed path against its own `repoRoot` parameter before reading — silently correct only because every REAL caller happened to pass `process.cwd()` as `repoRoot`. Now resolved explicitly (`path.resolve(repoRoot, file)`) in both this file and its twin, `continuation-residual-probe.ts`, so a caller (a test fixture, or any future caller) passing a different directory reads the right file.
- **Issue #177 [MED]:** the #170 ENOENT-narrowing catch (both this file and its twin) was pinned by zero tests — a full-suite mutation reverting both back to a blanket `catch { continue; }` left the suite byte-identical (red-team, measured: 779/778/1 on both sides, the one known failure being the pre-existing worktree-only missing-`adr/`-submodule case). Fixed: `readFileTexts()` (extracted from each file's `collectFullTreeFileTexts()`) now takes an injectable `readFileImpl` (default: the real `fs/promises` `readFile`), and a new test in each file forces a non-ENOENT error (`EACCES`) via a fake reader and asserts it propagates rather than being silently swallowed, plus a companion test confirming ENOENT is still the one legitimate silent skip. Verified the mutation-proving property directly: temporarily reverted both narrowings back to a blanket catch, confirmed the new EACCES test goes red in both files, then restored the fix.
- **Issue #178 [MED]:** `Issue #170` + `Issue #172` composed into a new crash — `git ls-files --others` reports an untracked NESTED git repository as a directory entry (trailing slash, e.g. `zz-nested/`) rather than recursing into it; that entry passed `shouldScanFile` unfiltered and `readFile` on it threw an unhandled `EISDIR` (no path named in the error), exit 1, in both `--field` and default mode. Fixed at the source: `src/lib/git.ts`'s `lsFilesWorkingTree()` now excludes any `--others` entry ending in `/`. New tests: a fake-runner unit test in `git.test.ts` pinning the exclusion directly, and an end-to-end test in `marker-corpus-probe.test.ts` that creates a real nested git repo inside an isolated `mkdtemp` fixture and confirms the whole probe path no longer crashes (confirmed red — the exact `EISDIR` stack trace red-team's report quotes — before the fix, green after).
- **Issue #179 [MED]:** `continuation-residual-probe.ts`'s own `Issue #173`-class stray-argument-swallowing gap was disclosed in CHANGELOG prose only, with no Issue and no backlog line, and its own test asserted the silent-swallow (`exit 0` on a stray/unknown arg) as if it were correct behavior. Not fixed this round — deliberately deferred, given this round's own risk budget (the two prior rounds each introduced a new bug while fixing the previous round's findings, and this is the round immediately before PRINCIPLES rule 16(c)'s mandatory-council trip-wire). Filed as **Issue #179** (`bug`, `severity:med`, `qa`, Milestone S1); the test's title and comment now read "KNOWN GAP, tracked in Issue #179 — not a contract" instead of asserting the behavior is correct, so a future fix to #179 doesn't have to fight its own pinning test.

Both are LOW/cosmetic per PRINCIPLES.md's review-verdict table and do not trigger CLAUDE.md's same-turn Issue-filing rule (HIGH/MED only); left for a future pass rather than expanding this round's diff.

### Fixed — `qa14-issue137-precision-fastfollow` (Issue #137, R1 + R2): basename-index fallback for bare-filename `path:line` citations, `ADR_CANDIDATE_RE` digit-boundary tightening
CRITICAL tier (ratified: demonstrated regression history on this exact file — 3+ prior adversarial CRITICAL rounds — plus QA-14's CI-gate blast radius; `docs/run-log.jsonl`'s 2026-09-13T19:07:40 tier-ratified event, scope `qa14-issue137-precision-fastfollow`). Scope per the approved plan: `src/qa/reference-resolver.ts` + `.test.ts`. `classifyAdr`'s own `^ADR-(\d+)$` + exact-4-digit-length validation kept byte-identical, confirmed via `diff` on the extracted function body.

**Round-2 fix-now (Issue #166, `code-reviewer` + `red-team`, both same-session):** this entry's own first draft quoted real digit-bearing `ADR-`-prefixed example strings in backticks to illustrate R2 — QA-14 scans this file too, so those examples became new candidates under the very regex being documented, reintroducing blocking failures. Fixed the same way `reference-resolver.ts`'s own header comment already handles this (rewording, not a self-exemption): every example below uses a non-digit placeholder suffix (`ADR-tooshort`, `ADR-mixedsuffix`, `ADR-noentry`) instead of a real digit-bearing token — inert under `ADR_CANDIDATE_RE`'s digit-lookahead, so the illustration stays but nothing here becomes a new citation.

- **R2 — digit-boundary tightening:** `ADR_CANDIDATE_RE` gained a lookahead requiring at least one digit anywhere in the candidate's suffix before it is recorded as a candidate at all: `/\bADR-(?=[A-Za-z0-9]*\d)[A-Za-z0-9]+/g`. A zero-digit suffix (`ADR-amendment`, `ADR-cache`, a literal `ADR-NNNN` placeholder — the exact case Issue #137 named) is no longer a candidate in the first place — no false `unparseable` report for prose that was never trying to cite a real ADR id. A digit-bearing suffix — a wrong-length short id (`ADR-tooshort`), a digit-and-letter mix (`ADR-mixedsuffix`), a well-formed-length id absent from this repo's catalog (`ADR-noentry`), or a real accepted id already in this repo — is still a candidate exactly as before, still reaching `classifyAdr`'s own byte-identical validation. The pinned regression test at `reference-resolver.test.ts:47` (a two-digit id, `ADR-tooshort`-shaped → candidate → `unparseable`) passes unedited.
- **R1 — basename-index fallback, `path:line` shape only:** `classifyPath`'s `path:line` branch, when the cited path segment contains no `/` and fails today's literal `pathExists` check, now consults a new injected `deps.findByBasename(basename): string[]`: 0 matches stays `unresolved-authority` (unchanged); exactly 1 match resolves/bounds-checks against the matched real path; 2+ matches fails closed as `unresolved-authority` (never guesses which file). The bare-path/no-line-number branch and any `/`-containing path segment never call `findByBasename` (pinned by spy-count tests). Wired in `main()`: one single repo-tree walk (reusing the same `listFilesRecursive` pattern/exclusions already used for the ADR catalog) builds a `Map<basename, path[]>` once, never re-walked per query. No new direct `existsSync`/`readFileSync` call was added inside `classifyPath` — confirmed via `grep -n "existsSync(|readFileSync("` on the extracted function body: zero hits.
- **Flagged deviation from the plan's literal file list (necessary, not scope creep):** the new required `findByBasename` member on `ReferenceResolverDeps` also required a one-line `() => []` stub in 3 construction sites beyond `reference-resolver.test.ts`'s own 3 — `src/qa/continuation-residual-probe.ts` (2 sites) + `.test.ts` (1 site) and `src/qa/marker-corpus-probe.ts` (1 site) — none of which typechecks otherwise. Zero behavior change to either instrument: neither reads a path citation's basename-fallback resolution.

**Real-corpus measurement (Round-2 fix-now, re-measured fresh on the actual current tree state per Issue #166's own drift finding — do not reuse this entry's own now-superseded prior figures, and do not reuse this measurement's figures unmeasured either): before/after, full-tree, same command both times — `node src/qa/reference-resolver.ts <zero-sha> HEAD`, before = this diff `git stash`-ed against `master`, after = this diff applied exactly as it ships:** blocking failures **299 → 132** (of 3758 → 3722 total citations scanned; `unclassified` 498 → 501 — the small increase is this round's own fix-now prose adding a few new bare `#N` mentions, e.g. Issue #166/#167 above; `unclassified` is non-blocking and R1/R2 don't touch that population's classification logic).
- `unresolved-authority`: **234 → 117** (117 fewer). Unique bare-filename unresolved raw strings dropped from 103 to 11 — most of those 11 are genuinely ambiguous (2+ real files sharing that basename, e.g. `shell.ts`, `kernel.ts`) and correctly still fail closed rather than guess; a few remain 0-match citations to a basename absent from the tree entirely (e.g. `foo.ts`) — a pre-existing gap this round doesn't touch.
- `unparseable`: **52 → 2** (50 fewer). The remaining two are real digit-bearing ADR ids of the wrong length; no zero-digit prose false positive remains.
- `cross-repo-issue`: **13 → 13** (unaffected, as expected).

~~**Disclosed residual (found, not fixed here — out of this round's scope):** `docs/reviews/qa14-issue137-precision-fastfollow-code-2026-09-13.md` (`code-reviewer`'s own report for this round) quotes the same literal digit-bearing `ADR-`-prefixed example strings verbatim as evidence — correctly, per this project's own PRINCIPLES rule 10/19 verbatim-evidence discipline and `docs/decisions.md`'s 2026-09-09 row (obfuscating review-report evidence was explicitly rejected as "Path C"). Because it is a real, non-`.test.ts` file in this working tree, QA-14's full-tree scan also picks it up: measured directly, `node src/qa/reference-resolver.ts <zero-sha> HEAD` against the literal current full working tree (review reports included, nothing stashed) reports blocking failures of **135** (of 3735 total citations scanned; `unparseable` 5, not 2) — 3 higher than this entry's own diff-scoped 132 above. This is not this round's own contamination (the fix above is complete and verified against it in isolation); it is the reviewer's own report doing exactly what verbatim-evidence discipline requires. Flagged for the Manager/`code-reviewer`, not silently edited by `story-implementer` (review reports are not this role's artifact to rewrite) — same disposition this project already gave the qa1415fix precedent's own "ADR- fixture/placeholder examples in prose (pre-existing)" residual.**~~

**CORRECTED (Issue #168, `code-reviewer`'s own re-confirm pass): the note above was factually wrong about the cause of the 135-vs-132 gap.** `docs/reviews/qa14-issue137-precision-fastfollow-code-2026-09-13.md` is untracked (`git ls-files` against it returns 0 entries), and `resolveChangedFiles`'s zero-SHA fallback walks only committed/tracked blobs — an untracked file structurally cannot contribute to this measurement, so the report file was never the source of the +3. The real cause: `src/qa/completeness-claim-checker.ts`'s own Issue #167 explanatory comment (a real, tracked, non-`.test.ts` file — not exempt from QA-14's scan) quoted the same real digit-bearing example strings Issue #166 itself used, verbatim, in backticks — those strings became new QA-14 candidates under `ADR_CANDIDATE_RE`, the exact regex this round's own R2 fix documents. Isolated via `git stash` (stashing only that one source file dropped the full-tree count from 135 back to 132 — the entire +3 traced to that one comment, not to any review report). Fixed by rewording those examples to non-digit placeholders (`ADR-tooshort`/`ADR-mixedsuffix`/`ADR-noentry`), matching this story's own already-established discipline (see this entry's own round-2 paragraph above, which handles the identical hazard the same way).

**Test changes:** 10 new tests in `src/qa/reference-resolver.test.ts` — 4 pin R2 (a digit-bearing suffix still candidates/`unparseable`; 3 zero-digit prose shapes, including Issue #137's own named `ADR-NNNN` case, are never candidates at all), 6 pin R1 (0/1/2+ basename-match outcomes, in-range and out-of-range, plus the two never-called-basename guards — bare-path branch, `/`-containing segment). The remaining named acceptance checks (the pinned `ADR-tooshort`-shaped test unedited, `classifyAdr`'s byte-identical diff, the zero-`existsSync`/`readFileSync` grep, the untouched `resolveWithinRepo` containment tests) are confirmed via real commands, not new test code.
