**Verification (round-2 fix-now, Issues #166/#167):** `npm run typecheck`/`npm run lint` clean; `npm test` 773/773 pass, 0 fail, 0 skipped (769 carried + 4 new `completeness-claim-checker.test.ts` cases for Issue #167); `node src/qa/completeness-claim-checker.ts` PASS (2 files, all claims verified — including a mutation-demonstrated proof that removing the new pattern makes exactly 2 of the 4 new tests fail); `git diff --stat`: only `CHANGELOG.md`/`docs/STATE.md`/`docs/decisions.md`/`docs/.maat-state.json` (Issue #166) plus `completeness-claim-checker.ts`/`.test.ts` (Issue #167) touched this round — no other file changed.

### Fixed — `qa14-marker-redesign` (closes Issues #143, #144, #145 + #137 finding (c)): bare-`#N` citation classification redesign, replacing the ordinal-word denylist with an explicit citation-marker requirement
CRITICAL tier (ratified consistent with `qa1415fix`'s own precedent on this exact file/scope — three prior adversarial CRITICAL rounds each found a real regression). Plan: `docs/plans/qa14-marker-redesign-phase1-2026-09-11.md`. Scope: `src/qa/reference-resolver.ts` + `.test.ts` only — `classifyPath`/`ADR_CANDIDATE_RE`/`resolveWithinRepo`/`checkIssueViaGh`/`resolveIssueCitations`/`parseMaxDistinctIssues` structurally untouched (confirmed via `git diff --stat`); R1 (bare-filename path resolution) and R2 (`ADR_CANDIDATE_RE` tightening) stay deferred to a future story, per `docs/decisions.md` 2026-09-11 row 61 point (3).

**Deleted** `NON_ISSUE_ORDINAL_WORD_RE` (the 9-word ordinal denylist) and `shouldExcludeBareIssueMatch`'s ordinal-checking branch entirely — no denylist replacement, ever; red-team round 3 (`docs/reviews/qa1415fix-red-team-round3-2026-09-10.md`) demonstrated the denylist could never be complete (>= 12 residual unlisted-ordinal-word shapes measured full-tree: `items`, `rule`, `criterion`, `Next`, `recommendation`, `residual`, `Coverage gap`, `HIGH #N`, `LOW #N`, open-paren, backtick, angle-marker forms).

**Added** a positive citation-marker mechanism, replacing the negative denylist: `CITATION_MARKER_WORD_RE` (`Issue(s)`/`Closes`/`Closed`/`Fixes`/`Fixed`/`Resolves`, same-line, word-adjacent), `GH_MARKER_RE` (glued `GH`/`GH-` shorthand), `LIST_CONTINUATION_RE` (a later `#N` in the same comma/slash/"and"/`&`/dash-range list inherits an earlier marked member's status), and `classifyBareHashMatch` (replacing `shouldExcludeBareIssueMatch`). A bare `#N` with no marker in reach is no longer silently excluded (dropped from the scan entirely, as the old denylist did) or silently asserted resolved — it is a new, explicit `Verdict` member `"unclassified"` (`Citation.kind: "issue-candidate"`), deliberately **non-blocking**: `summarizeCitations` never flips `ok` to `false` for `unclassified` alone, but always counts it in `summary` and lists it in `details`, on both PASS and FAIL runs (`docs/decisions.md` 2026-09-11 row 61 point (1)).

- **R3:** a bare `reponame#N` citation with no `owner/` prefix (e.g. `claude-mem#2604`) now classifies `unclassified` — never `cross-repo-issue` (asserting cross-repo for an owner-less shape was itself a directional guess) and never reaches `issueExists`/`verifyLocalIssue` as a local number.
- **R4:** every ordinal/count-word-adjacent bare `#N` shape — the original 9 denylist words, red-team's >= 12 named residual shapes, and any future unlisted ordinal word — classifies `unclassified`, achieved structurally (no word is ever enumerated as "excluded" anywhere in the code), **for an ordinal shape evaluated in isolation.** ~~never resolved~~ **CORRECTED 2026-09-11 (round-1 fix-now, Issue #154):** this guarantee does not extend across a marked citation's list continuation — an ordinal-shaped `#N` immediately after a real marker, joined only by connector punctuation, could still resolve. The tight-dash sub-case (`"Closes #N - #N of the findings"`) is fixed this round (see below); the comma/"and"-joined sub-case and the case where the ordinal-adjacent word is itself a marker word (e.g. `"Fixed #N of N open findings"`) remain open, disclosed, measured residuals (~~0/316 real-corpus incidence~~ ~~**CORRECTED 2026-09-11 round-2 fix-now, Issue #154 — see below, that "0" was itself false: 10/400 real occurrences, 5 distinct blocking gate failures**~~ **CORRECTED 2026-09-11 (round-4 fix-now, council-ratified — see the matching round-4 entry below): that "10/400" figure was itself already stale by the time it shipped — a 5th recurrence of the same numeric-claim-drift class this file has now corrected four times running — and is not repeated here. The residual is real, non-zero, small (single-digit percent of the continuation-marked population, every measurement to date), and fails LOUD (a blocking `unresolved-authority`, never a silent false-verify). Live counts: `node src/qa/continuation-residual-probe.ts --field=continuation-marked` (denominator, pure/fast) and `--field=continuation-residual` (numerator, real `gh`-backed leak count) — a real, committed, re-runnable instrument, not a hand-typed figure.**).
- **R5:** an all-digit hex literal (`#000`, `#333`) quoted in prose or backticks classifies `unclassified`, ~~never `unresolved-authority`~~ **CORRECTED 2026-09-11 (round-1 fix-now, Issue #154):** true only when the hex literal has no marker in reach — a hex-shaped `#N` immediately after a real marker via comma/whitespace continuation still resolves as a real (and here, blocking) issue citation; a disclosed, open residual, not closed by this round's fix (which closes the tight-dash sub-case only).
- **R6:** a real issue citation immediately following an excluded/ordinal-adjacent term in the same list (e.g. `findings #118/#119`, both real GitHub Issues) is never silently dropped — it now appears as `unclassified` (loud, counted), never a zero-citation silent omission. Honest limitation, disclosed rather than hidden: this mechanism cannot distinguish a real citation in that position from an ordinal false positive of the identical shape — both members become `unclassified`, not falsely `resolved`.

**Test changes:** 4 pre-existing non-regression tests amended from asserting `verdict: "resolved"` to `"unclassified"` for a bare, unmarked `#N` (a parenthetical and a line-start case from round 2; two more of the identical shape found during this build's own verification — an Issue #141 cross-file-dedup wiring test and the NEW-4 milestone-line-boundary test) — each carries an explicit, dated comment citing this plan's §5.1 ruling and explaining the change is deliberate, not silent test-doctoring. ~~§5.1's own measurement, reproduced from a scratchpad probe (not committed): `marked=530 unmarked=1399 total=1929` — ~73% of this repo's bare-`#N` occurrences carry no explicit marker~~ **CORRECTED 2026-09-11 (round-1 fix-now, Issue #150):** that figure was a hand-derived completeness claim from an uncommitted scratchpad (CLAUDE.md hard-rule violation) — replaced by a real, committed, re-runnable instrument, `src/qa/marker-corpus-probe.ts` (registered in `completeness-claim-checker.ts`'s `KNOWN_INSTRUMENTS` as `qa14-marker-corpus-probe`). ~~Re-run for real at this round's own HEAD: `marked=380 unmarked=416 total=796` — ~52% of this repo's bare-`#N` occurrences carry no explicit marker (213 files scanned).~~ **CORRECTED 2026-09-11 (round-2 fix-now, Issue #150) — that figure was itself false, and is now instrument-verified rather than typed: see this file's matching round-2 entry below for the current real number and its `[[completeness: ...]]` marker.** The design conclusion this figure supports (a bare, unmarked `#N` was never a safe "resolved" claim) is unchanged by the correction — the redesign's non-blocking-`unclassified` bucket doesn't depend on the exact percentage, only on the population being large and real, which every version of this figure confirms. ~~15 new tests added~~ **CORRECTED 2026-09-11:** see `docs/decisions.md`'s matching correction — 8 new tests, 3 of the 4 amendments renamed (the "15" was a miscount of one test's internal 15-entry case table). All mapping every acceptance criterion (R3–R6, the non-blocking bucket, non-regression, scope boundary).

**Real-corpus measurement (mechanically compared, before/after this change, same code both times run against the same file scope — never hand-derived):**
- **Diff-scope** (this story's own complete PR range, `c598312..HEAD`, including this changelog/decisions-log bookkeeping — this row's own prose is itself part of the scanned diff, same self-referential shape `qa1415fix`'s own entries already disclosed; the count below is the last real measurement taken, not chased to a fixed point): blocking failures **34 → 23** — a mechanical set-diff of the two runs' distinct blocking-failure-detail sets confirms **zero new blocking failures** (`after` is a strict subset of `before`). Every remaining blocking failure read individually: none is caused by this change — a majority are pre-existing `classifyPath` path-not-found failures (R1, out of scope, e.g. bare test filenames missing their real directory prefix), plus a genuine cross-repo issue (correct, by design), a couple of `ADR-` fixture/placeholder examples in prose (pre-existing), and an illustrative `Issue#0` fixture example (pre-existing) — the same already-known, already-deferred gap `docs/decisions.md`'s own prior rows for this file already name.
- **Full-tree control range**: blocking failures **284 → 270** (down 14); 507 citations newly surfaced as `unclassified`. A mechanical set-diff of the two "blocking failure detail line" sets confirms **zero new blocking failures** (`after` is a strict subset of `before`) and exactly 4 distinct failures removed: `#000`/`#333` (R5 hex literals) and `#2604`/`#999999` (R3 owner-less reponame shape and a fabricated-fixture bare number), each now correctly `unclassified` instead of wrongly `unresolved-authority`.

Consistent with the plan's rollback note: this mechanism can only ever move a match toward `unclassified` (non-blocking) or fix a genuine misclassification — nothing in the redesign can newly promote a previously-`resolved` match into a blocking verdict, confirmed here empirically as well as structurally.

**Verification:** `npm run typecheck`/`npm run lint` clean; `npm test` 708/708 pass, 0 fail, 0 skipped (700 carried, 4 amended with explicit dated comments, ~~15~~ **8 new** — all in `src/qa/reference-resolver.test.ts`); `git diff --stat` confirms only `reference-resolver.ts`/`.test.ts` (plus this changelog/decisions/state bookkeeping) touched.

### Fixed — `qa14-marker-redesign` round 1 (Issues #149, #150, #151, #152, #153, #154): Stage-3 round-1 review fix-now pass
Against `docs/reviews/qa14-marker-redesign-{red-team,code,cross-domain}-2026-09-11.md`, triaged in `docs/decisions.md`'s 2026-09-11 row 63. All items fixed or, where a full structural fix would reintroduce the exact denylist failure mode this story exists to eliminate, closed partially with the residual honestly measured and disclosed (not silently claimed fixed).

- **Issue #152 [HIGH]:** `scanReferences`'s dedup (`src/qa/reference-resolver.ts`) was keyed on the raw citation string alone — an unmarked bare `#N` occurrence could take that key first and permanently shadow a later real `Closes/Fixes #N` marker of the identical raw string, so the marked occurrence's real classification never ran at all (measured: 61/351, 17.4%, of distinct marked citations in-tree). Fixed: `seen` is now a `Map<string, index>`; a new `recordBareHash` upgrades an already-recorded `"issue-candidate"` (unmarked) entry in place the moment a MARKED occurrence of the same raw string is found — a marked classification always wins, regardless of scan order. The reverse (an unmarked repeat after a marked original) never downgrades. 3 new tests (order both ways, plus the existing-resolves-cleanly symmetric case).
- **Issue #149 [HIGH]:** `LIST_CONTINUATION_RE`'s dash/en-dash/em-dash class had zero test coverage (mutation-demonstrated: removing it left 708/708 green). 2 new tests pin a tight hyphen-joined and an en-dash/em-dash-joined marked range, each mutation-verified to depend on the dash class (manually confirmed red against a temporarily-defanged regex, green against shipped code).
- **Issue #153 [MED]:** `classifyBareHashMatch`'s `"already-recorded"` branch (bare `#N` immediately preceded by "Issue"/"Milestone", already recorded by the dedicated word-form regex) returned without seeding `state.lastMarkedListEnd`, so a singular `"Issue #A, #B"` list failed to open list continuation for `#B` while the plural `"Issues #A, #B"` (which takes the `CITATION_MARKER_WORD_RE` branch instead) worked. Fixed: the branch now seeds continuation state identically to every other marker. 2 new tests (the list case, plus a non-regression single-citation case).
- **Issue #154 [MED]:** R4's "every ordinal shape … never resolved, achieved structurally" and R5's "never `unresolved-authority`" claims were false — the continuation mechanism let marked status leak across an intervening ordinal/hex word when only connector punctuation (no actual word) separated them from the prior marked item. Investigated whether Issue #153's seeding fix structurally closed this — it does not (if anything it widens the surface slightly, since word-form-marked lists now also seed continuation). Added a minimal, corpus-grounded guard instead of a word list (which would reintroduce the exact denylist failure mode this story eliminates): `TIGHT_DASH_CONTINUATION_RE` restricts the hyphen/en-dash/em-dash separator to immediate (no-whitespace) adjacency — grepping this repo's entire real corpus confirms every genuine dash-joined citation range is written tight (`"Issues #105-#113"`, never `"#105 - #113"`); a space-padded dash is, in real usage, an ordinary sentence dash, not a range. This closes the dash sub-case (`"Closes #N - #N of the findings"` now correctly leaves the second number `unclassified`, pinned by a new test) without touching the comma/whitespace class, which genuinely needs surrounding whitespace for real citation lists (`"Closes #N, #N"`) and so cannot be narrowed the same way. **Comma-adjacent leak stays open, disclosed, pinned by a new test documenting the known residual** — measured full-tree incidence of both the (now-fixed) dash case and the (still-open) comma case: ~~**0 real occurrences**~~ ~~**CORRECTED 2026-09-11 (round-2 fix-now — see the matching round-2 entry below): this "0" was itself false, independently re-measured by `red-team`, `cross-domain-reviewer` and this round's own build (three separate methodologies, same answer) at 10/400.**~~ **CORRECTED 2026-09-11 (round-4 fix-now, council-ratified — see the matching round-4 entry below): that "10/400" was itself already stale by the time it shipped — not repeated here. Real, non-zero, small, fails loud; live counts via `node src/qa/continuation-residual-probe.ts --field=continuation-marked`/`--field=continuation-residual`, a real committed instrument replacing every hand-typed figure.** The R4/R5 "never" claims above are corrected in place (this file, `docs/decisions.md`) to name the actual residual rather than repeat the false absolute — the third time this project's own history has had to correct exactly this shape of false-completeness claim on this file, and (per the round-2 entry below) not the last — `docs/decisions.md`'s 2026-09-09 `cifix` rows name the first two recurrences on a different file.
- **Issue #150 [MED]:** the plan's/this file's own `marked=530/unmarked=1399/total=1929` (~73%) figure came from an uncommitted scratchpad probe — a hand-derived completeness claim (CLAUDE.md hard rule). Committed a real, re-runnable instrument, `src/qa/marker-corpus-probe.ts` (+ `.test.ts`, 5 tests), reusing the shipped `scanReferences` classifier directly (never a second hand-copied regex set) and registered it in `completeness-claim-checker.ts`'s `KNOWN_INSTRUMENTS` as `qa14-marker-corpus-probe`. ~~Re-run for real: `marked=380 unmarked=416 total=796` (~52%), corrected above.~~ **CORRECTED 2026-09-11 (round-2 fix-now — see the matching round-2 entry below): this figure did not reproduce at any tree state either round-2 reviewer checked, and was registered in `KNOWN_INSTRUMENTS` but wired to no `[[completeness: ...]]` marker, so it was unverifiable through this project's own mechanism — both fixed below.** The methodology differs from the original scratchpad in a disclosed way (this instrument dedupes per-file and excludes the word-form `Issue #N`/`Milestone #N`/`owner/repo#N` populations, matching the shipped classifier's own actual behavior — the original scratchpad's exact counting method was never committed, so it cannot be reproduced or audited); the design conclusion the figure supports is unaffected either way.
- **Issue #151 [MED]:** `docs/STATE.md` wasn't updated by the original build despite CLAUDE.md's Definition of Done. Fixed — see this file's matching entry below.
- **Full-tree drift (LOW, unfiled):** the original build's own "284 → 270" full-tree figure was stale by the time red-team re-ran it (292 → 271 at red-team's own HEAD, benign bookkeeping-commit drift). Re-measured fresh at THIS round's own final HEAD (same methodology: OLD = `c598312`'s classifier, NEW = this round's fixed classifier, both run against the identical current full tree, 213 files): **OLD blocking = 295 / total 3270 → NEW blocking = 275 / total 3339** (down 20), 415 citations `unclassified`. Diff-scope (`c598312..64a18ed` file list, current content): **OLD blocking = 34 / total 541 → NEW blocking = 25 / total 552** (down 9). Both re-runs' mechanical set-diffs of distinct blocking-failure-detail lines confirm **zero new distinct failure reasons** (`after` ⊆ `before`) and the same 3 removed lines as the original build (`#2604`/`#333`/`#999999`). The occurrence-count increase vs. the original build's own 23/270 figures (not a new distinct *reason*, per the set-diff) is `#000` inside `docs/decisions.md`'s own row 63, which quotes red-team's comma-continuation repro verbatim as evidence — a real, expected instance of the disclosed Issue #154 comma-residual, not a regression this round introduced; not reworded, since row 63 is the Manager's own already-written triage record (append-only Decision Log convention). Like the original build's own figures, these are the last real measurement taken as this text was written, not chased to a fixed point — this very entry's own bare-`#N` mentions shift the count again with each edit, a self-referential limit this project's own history has already disclosed elsewhere, not unique to this round.
- **Flaky-test suspicion (cross-domain-reviewer, one `summarizeCitations` test in `reference-resolver`'s own test file):** ran the full suite 10 times this round (5 + 5), unmodified working tree — 722/722 pass, 0 fail, every run. Never reproduced. Reported honestly as not-reproduced, not claimed fixed (no code change was made to that test or `summarizeCitations`).

**Verification:** `npm run typecheck`/`npm run lint` clean; `npm test` 722/722 pass, 0 fail, 0 skipped, confirmed across 10 consecutive full-suite runs (14 new tests this round: 9 in `reference-resolver.test.ts`, 5 in `marker-corpus-probe.test.ts`); `node src/qa/completeness-claim-checker.ts` PASS. `classifyPath`/`ADR_CANDIDATE_RE`/`resolveWithinRepo` confirmed still byte-identical to `master` (out-of-scope boundary untouched).

### Fixed — `qa14-marker-redesign` round 2 (Issues #150, #154): Stage-3 round-2 re-confirm findings — claim-accuracy fix-now, not a code defect
Against `docs/reviews/qa14-marker-redesign-{red-team,cross-domain}-round2-2026-09-11.md`, triaged in `docs/decisions.md`'s 2026-09-11 row 65. Both round-1 close-out claims corrected above were themselves false at the commit that shipped them — the exact hand-derived/false-completeness-claim pattern this project's own hard rule exists to stop, recurring inside the story whose purpose is fixing recurrences of that pattern on this file. Both are corrected here honestly, with the real command/output backing each number, independently re-measured by this round's own build rather than reused from either reviewer.

- **Issue #154 [MED]:** the round-1 close-out's "0 real occurrences" / "0/316" claim (corrected above, in place, everywhere it appeared: `src/qa/reference-resolver.ts`, `src/qa/reference-resolver.test.ts`, this file, `docs/STATE.md`) was false. Independently re-measured, mechanically, using an instrumented copy of the shipped classifier (a byte-for-byte copy with exactly 2 added lines distinguishing "direct-marked" from "continuation-marked" inside `classifyBareHashMatch`) plus the real, unmodified `checkIssueViaGh`: ~~**400 continuation-marked occurrences full-tree, 10 of which (5 distinct file/number pairs) fail real-issue existence** and become a loud blocking gate failure — confirmed identically by `red-team`'s own independent instrumentation and by this round's own separate re-run (three methodologies, same 10/400 and the same 5 file/number pairs). Cross-checked against the real shipped gate on this branch's own PR range (`node src/qa/reference-resolver.ts c598312 HEAD`): 5 of that run's blocking findings are this residual.~~ **CORRECTED 2026-09-11 (round-3 fix-now, structural — see the matching round-3 entry below): this exact count was already stale at the commit that shipped it (red-team's own round-3 pass, minutes later against the same branch, measured a different count) — the 4th time this file shipped a frozen count of this residual that didn't survive its own commit. The residual is real, loud (a blocking gate failure, never silent), and non-zero; its exact size is no longer typed into this file — run `node src/qa/reference-resolver.ts <base> <head>` for the live number.** **No code fix this round either** — the risk direction is the safe one (a loud gate failure on ordinary prose that isn't really a citation, not a silent false-verify — the dash sub-case, which *was* the dangerous silent direction, was already closed round 1), ~~the incidence is small and contained (2.5% of the continuation-marked population)~~ **(single-digit percent of the continuation-marked population, every measurement to date — not repeated as an exact figure here, same round-3 correction as above)**, and closing it fully still requires either a word list (reintroducing the exact denylist failure mode R3-R6 eliminated) or dropping comma continuation entirely (silently un-verifying every genuine comma-joined citation list — the far larger, silent harm). Stays open, disclosed, pinned by the existing regression test. ~~now with an honest, re-measured number instead of a stale absolute~~ **CORRECTED 2026-09-11 (round-3): that "honest, re-measured number" was itself stale by the time it shipped — see round 3's entry below for the structural fix.**
- **Issue #150 [MED]:** the round-1 close-out's `marked=380 unmarked=416 total=796` (~52%) figure did not reproduce at any tree state either round-2 reviewer checked (both independently measured 422/427/849 at the shipping commit) — root cause: self-referential corpus growth (the round's own closing prose, which discusses issue numbers repeatedly, plus the 3 new review-report files, landed in the tree after the figure was captured mid-build, the same disclosed effect `docs/STATE.md` already names for the full-tree reference-resolver figures). Re-measured fresh at this round's own final commit (see below for the number this correction actually ships with) and **wired into QA-15 for real**: `marker-corpus-probe.ts` gained a `--field=marked|unmarked|total` mode (each prints ONLY that number, so it is unambiguously the last integer `verifyMarkerClaim` parses) and `completeness-claim-checker.ts`'s `KNOWN_INSTRUMENTS` gained three matching entries (`qa14-marker-corpus-probe-marked`/`-unmarked`/`-total`) — the default no-args entry (registered since round 1) printed a human sentence whose last integer was `filesScanned`, so no marker could ever have verified the published figure through it even if one had been added. This closes the gap red-team's own report named exactly ("registered... but a fix that looks complete and is not"): ~~three live markers (`cmd="qa14-marker-corpus-probe-marked" expect=430`, `-unmarked expect=456`, `-total expect=886`) — ~51% of this repo's bare-`#N` occurrences carry no explicit marker (220 files scanned; this figure is measured at the commit that ships it, after this round's own 3 review-report files became tracked — an earlier working-tree-only measurement of 424/433/857 undercounted because `git ls-tree`, which this instrument's own full-tree enumeration uses, cannot see an untracked file; re-measuring post-commit, not just pre-commit, is now this round's own disclosed lesson).~~ **CORRECTED 2026-09-11 (round-3 fix-now, structural — see the matching round-3 entry below): wiring these three raw corpus counts into an unconditional CI gate turned a legitimately-drifting statistic into a false-red generator — red-team's round-3 pass measured 4 of the last 8 constant-classifier commit transitions changing at least one of the three, and committing red-team's own round-3 report broke all three again before this correction even landed. The three `expect=N` markers are removed from this file; `qa14-marker-corpus-probe`'s `--field=marked|unmarked|total` entries stay registered in `completeness-claim-checker.ts`'s `KNOWN_INSTRUMENTS` (real, callable, on-demand-verifiable via `node src/qa/marker-corpus-probe.ts --field=<name>`) but no longer assert an eternal exact-match in a file that gets rescanned on every unrelated commit.** A new end-to-end test (`completeness-claim-checker.test.ts`) proves the `--field` wiring against a REAL subprocess, not a fake runner, so "registered" and "wired" cannot silently diverge again — and a further round-3 test (see below) proves `--field`'s own output SHAPE is pinned, not just one wrong-`expect` comparison.

**Verification:** `npm run typecheck`/`npm run lint` clean; `npm test` 727/727 pass, 0 fail, 0 skipped (722 carried + 5 new: 3 `marker-corpus-probe.test.ts` CLI-parsing tests, 2 `completeness-claim-checker.test.ts` wiring tests); `node src/qa/completeness-claim-checker.ts` PASS (2 files checked, all claims — including the 3 new markers above — verified against a real re-run). No production code in `reference-resolver.ts`'s classification logic changed this round (the comma-continuation mechanism itself is untouched — only its own doc comments and this file's/`docs/STATE.md`'s claims about it were corrected); `marker-corpus-probe.ts`'s `computeMarkerCorpusStats` also untouched — only `main()`'s CLI surface gained `--field`.

### Fixed — `qa14-marker-redesign` round 3 (Issues #154, #155, #156): Stage-3 round-3 structural fix — stop freezing whole-corpus counts into permanently-rescanned files
Against `docs/reviews/qa14-marker-redesign-{red-team,cross-domain}-round3-2026-09-11.md`, triaged in `docs/decisions.md`'s 2026-09-11 round-3 row. Rounds 1 and 2 each re-measured and re-published a "fixed" numeric claim about this file's own residual/corpus-drift population, and each was independently caught stale by the next review pass — including round 2's own close-out figures, corrected above, which red-team's round-3 pass demonstrated were already wrong at the commit that shipped them (four distinct triples measured across this story so far, none matching any other — one of them, `docs/decisions.md` row 66's own, matching no tree state that ever existed). The root cause is structural, not carelessness: the corpus these figures count is this repo's whole tracked tree, which changes on nearly every commit, including this story's own review-report files landing in the tree. This round stops re-measuring-and-republishing and fixes the SHAPE of the claim instead, per `docs/decisions.md`'s round-3 ruling (PRINCIPLES rule 16's "ceremony without a corresponding safety gain" warning).

- **Issue #154 [MED], de-hardcoded:** every permanently-scanned location that froze an exact "N of M" figure for the comma-continuation residual (`src/qa/reference-resolver.ts`'s comment above `TIGHT_DASH_CONTINUATION_RE`, `src/qa/reference-resolver.test.ts`'s comment above its residual regression test, this file's round-2 entry above, `docs/STATE.md`) now states the residual qualitatively instead: real, non-zero, small (single-digit percent of the continuation-marked population, every measurement to date), and fails LOUD — every instance is a blocking `unresolved-authority` in the real gate run, never a silent false-verify. The exact current count is whatever `node src/qa/reference-resolver.ts <base> <head>` (or the full-tree form) reports right now; no number is frozen here. The point-in-time measurements that established this residual's existence and safe-direction argument stay in the dated `docs/reviews/qa14-marker-redesign-{red-team,cross-domain}-round{2,3}-2026-09-11.md` reports, which are point-in-time by this project's own convention and so are allowed to go stale — unlike this file, `reference-resolver.ts`, `reference-resolver.test.ts` and `docs/STATE.md`, which are read forward from every future commit.
- **Issue #155 [MED], reconsidered:** investigated this project's real `[[completeness: cmd="..." expect=N]]` convention before touching it (grepped every live usage in the repo, not just this story's own). The one other numeric marker in this file, `qa-mutation-shell` (`expect=53`, a mutation-kill count over a fixed, scoped shadow copy of `src/policy/**`), is stable for a specific, identifiable reason: its "corpus" is version-controlled source code that only changes via a deliberate, reviewed PR adding or removing a mutant class — never via an unrelated docs commit. The three `qa14-marker-corpus-probe-<field>` markers round 2 wired into this file have no such property: their corpus is every tracked file in the repo, and their count moves on roughly half of all commits (red-team round-3, measured: 4 of 8 constant-classifier commit transitions changed at least one of the three; committing red-team's own round-3 report changed them again). There is no reformulation of "how many bare `#N`s in the whole repo lack a marker" that is both meaningful and stable — the figure's whole point is to describe a corpus that grows. Chose **option (a)**: the three `expect=N` markers are removed from this file's prose (see the strikethrough above); the `qa14-marker-corpus-probe-marked`/`-unmarked`/`-total` entries stay registered in `completeness-claim-checker.ts`'s `KNOWN_INSTRUMENTS`, real and callable on demand (`node src/qa/marker-corpus-probe.ts --field=<name>`), just no longer asserting an eternal exact-match inside a file that gets rescanned on every future commit. `KNOWN_INSTRUMENTS`/`MARKER_RE`/`verifyMarkerClaim` themselves are untouched — a prose-only change, confirmed via `git diff --stat`.
- **Issue #156 [MED], fixed for real:** `verifyMarkerClaim`'s `--field` CLI-mode plumbing had zero test coverage able to distinguish it from the pre-round-2 fallback — the existing end-to-end test only asserted `expect=0` mismatches, which is true whether stdout ends in the real field value or in `filesScanned` (red-team round-3's mutation M1: suppress `--field` emission, full suite still green). Added a new real-subprocess test to `src/qa/marker-corpus-probe.test.ts` that distinguishes on OUTPUT SHAPE rather than a numeric comparison: `--field=total` mode must emit ONLY `total=<N>` and must NOT contain the default mode's `marked=... unmarked=...`/`files scanned` text — an assertion that fails under M1 regardless of what the live corpus currently measures, unlike an `expect=0` check. Same test-coverage-gap class as round-1's HIGH #149, recurred a 2nd time here — swept this round's own other changes for the same untested-branch pattern before closing: the only other production-code change this round is `marker-corpus-probe.ts`'s summary-string wording fix (below), a string-literal change introducing no new branch, so no further gap found.
- **2 LOWs:** `marker-corpus-probe.ts`'s default-mode summary said "% of this repo's bare #N occurrences" while `computeMarkerCorpusStats` actually counts distinct (file, raw-citation) pairs (`scanReferences` dedupes per file) — a different, larger quantity than raw text occurrences. Fixed the label to "distinct per-file bare #N citations", matching both what the function computes and what the shipped QA-14 classifier itself treats as one citation event — the more useful metric for this instrument's actual purpose — and left the computation itself untouched. The Milestone-word-form list-continuation conflation (`reference-resolver.ts:327`, `MILESTONE_CANDIDATE_RE`'s own comment names it) stays disclosed, not fixed — 0 measured incidence, same class of acceptable residual as this story's other already-disclosed gaps.

**Verification:** `npm run typecheck`/`npm run lint` clean; `npm test` 728/728 pass, 0 fail, 0 skipped (727 carried + 1 new shape-mutation test in `marker-corpus-probe.test.ts`); `node src/qa/completeness-claim-checker.ts` PASS (2 files checked — the 3 removed `qa14-marker-corpus-probe-<field>` markers are gone from `CHANGELOG.md`, `qa-mutation-shell`'s marker, the only remaining live numeric marker in this file, still verified against a real re-run). Confirmed the fix actually closes the drift hole, not just re-passes today: at this round's own starting commit (`4896420`), `node src/qa/completeness-claim-checker.ts` was already FAILING — the corpus had moved since round 2 shipped (`marked=434 unmarked=455 total=889` vs. the published `430/456/886`) — proving the structural problem was live, not hypothetical, before this round touched anything; after this round's fix, the same command passes because the assertion that could go stale no longer exists, not because a new number happens to match today.

### Fixed — `qa14-marker-redesign` round 4 (Issue #154's residual, Issue #159): council Path A + C — a real, callable, non-blocking instrument for the residual, plus the QA-15 blind spot that let it ship stale
CRITICAL tier. Council-ratified (`docs/decisions.md`'s "Path-Forward Brief" row, 2026-09-11 — `design-challenger` + `architecture-reviewer` + `impact-analyst`, all three seats converged independently on the same shape). Triggered by `red-team`'s round-4 no-go: a LIVE recurrence of the numeric-claim-drift class (2 more stale `10/400`-shaped locations round 4's own hunt didn't find, plus a fabricated round-3 evidence sentence still uncorrected) — the third round running to find a live variant of the same root problem, so the Manager convened a design council ahead of the mechanical round-counter threshold rather than routing a 5th "re-measure and re-publish" round.

**Root cause, traced one level deeper than any prior round (design-challenger's O2 finding):** every prior round's measurement of this residual (rounds 2, 3, 4) required hand-building a throwaway, never-committed, byte-diffed fork of `reference-resolver.ts` because `Citation.kind` collapsed a directly-marked bare `#N` and a continuation-marked one into the same value (`"issue"`) — `classifyBareHashMatch` already computed the distinction internally, but discarded it before it reached any caller.

**(1) `Citation` gains `markedVia?: "direct" | "continuation"`** (`src/qa/reference-resolver.ts`), set on every local bare-hash-shaped or word-form `Issue #N` citation (`classifyBareHashMatch`'s `"already-recorded"`/`"marked-direct"`/`"marked-continuation"` branches, now distinguished instead of collapsed), left `undefined` for cross-repo/milestone/path/adr/unparseable/issue-candidate kinds where the distinction doesn't apply. `classifyPath`/`ADR_CANDIDATE_RE`/`resolveWithinRepo`/`checkIssueViaGh`/`resolveIssueCitations`/`parseMaxDistinctIssues` structurally untouched (confirmed via `git diff --stat`) — this is a pure additive field, no existing classification behavior changed. 6 new tests in `reference-resolver.test.ts` pin every population the field is (and isn't) set for.

**(2) A real, committed, on-demand instrument for the residual** — `src/qa/continuation-residual-probe.ts` (new file, + `.test.ts`, 13 tests). Two numbers, two different mechanisms, per the council's own design sketch: `--field=continuation-marked` (the denominator — how many bare-hash citations were marked via list continuation; pure, sync, no `gh` call, mirrors `marker-corpus-probe.ts`'s Issue #150 pattern exactly) and `--field=continuation-residual` (the numerator — of that population, how many fail REAL existence; REUSES `resolveIssueCitations`, never a second bespoke `gh`-calling path, so the existing `QA14_MAX_ISSUES` rate-limit cap and dedup cache apply unchanged). **Disclosed scope-boundary note** (both council seats asked for this, not a silent cross): the original Phase 1 plan declared `resolveIssueCitations`'s two-pass wiring "structurally untouched" as a hard scope boundary; this file does not modify that function's signature, cap, or dedup — it is a new, read-only CONSUMER of it — but is disclosed here as a widening of who depends on that surface, per both council seats' explicit request. Registered in `completeness-claim-checker.ts`'s `KNOWN_INSTRUMENTS` as `qa14-continuation-residual-probe-marked`/`-residual` — real, callable, ON-DEMAND only, deliberately never wired to a blocking `[[completeness: ...]]` marker (round 3's own #155 lesson, re-applied here so it can't recur a 5th time on this same story: this metric's corpus is the whole tracked tree, which moves on nearly every commit).

**Cost, measured for real (design-challenger's O3 unproven-assumption, now settled):** the real numerator run took ~59s wall-clock against 121 distinct `gh issue view` calls (well under the 300-call default cap) on this repo's current full tree, producing ~~`continuation-marked=273 continuation-residual=3`~~. Cross-checked against the real shipped gate (`node src/qa/reference-resolver.ts 0000...0 HEAD`, full-tree mode): at that round's commit, ~~exactly 3 `[unresolved-authority] #000` lines appear in its own printed output, matching the instrument's `continuation-residual=3` exactly~~ — an independent confirmation via a different code path, not a self-consistent number from the same computation. **CORRECTED 2026-09-11 (round-5 fix-now, human-ruled — `docs/decisions.md`'s round-5-hard-stop ruling row): this figure was already wrong at the commit that published it (`red-team`'s round-5 re-confirm independently re-measured the true value and showed `273` reproduces at NO committed tree state — a hybrid of one ref's file list and a different ref's content). Run `node src/qa/continuation-residual-probe.ts --field=continuation-marked` / `--field=continuation-residual` for the live count — never a frozen one.**

**(3) Mechanical sweep, grep-driven (not hand-checked against any prior round's claimed-complete list):** a fresh `grep -n` for the stale figure's exact shape found the 3 live, non-struck locations design-challenger's own grep named (`CHANGELOG.md:15`, `CHANGELOG.md:35`, `docs/STATE.md:35` — 2 more than red-team round 4's own single-location hunt found) plus confirmed `src/qa/reference-resolver.ts`/`.test.ts` were already fully de-hardcoded (zero hits). The 3 corrected in place (struck through, per this file's own convention — never deleted): the frozen occurrence/failure-count figure each carried is replaced with the qualitative, non-frozen statement (real, non-zero, small, fails loud) round 3 already established as the right shape, now pointing at the new instrument's two callable commands instead of the plain gate command architecture-reviewer independently proved does not isolate this residual's own count (it folds every failure reason — path/ADR/cross-repo/this-residual alike — into one undifferentiated total).

**(4) Issue #159 fixed:** `completeness-claim-checker.ts`'s `BARE_CLAIM_PHRASES` heuristic only ever matched "N of M" phrasing, never this project's own "N/M" shorthand — the exact reason the stale #154 claim above passed QA-15 clean at HEAD despite being live-stale. A blanket `\d+\/\d+` pattern was measured and rejected (impact-analyst's council report: 112 existing legitimate `N/M`-shaped numbers in the two scanned files, overwhelmingly test/coverage-count reporting like `708/708 pass`). Two narrower, evidence-grounded patterns added instead: an `N/M` fraction immediately followed by "occurrences", and an `N/M` fraction near a "distinct ... failures/leaks" phrase — neither shape appears near any of the 112 legitimate hits (re-confirmed by re-running `findBareClaims` against both real scanned files after the fix: 0 false positives). **A second, generalizable fix alongside it:** `findBareClaims` now strips markdown `~~...~~` (strikethrough) spans before matching — a struck claim is SUPERSEDED per this project's own documented `docs/decisions.md` convention ("strike through and append, never delete"), not a live one; without this, this round's own corrective edits (which keep the old false text, struck, per that convention) would immediately re-trip the very heuristic that names them. 8 new tests, including a real end-to-end check that `CHANGELOG.md`/`docs/STATE.md` (`DEFAULT_FILES`, the exact two files this round corrected) carry zero bare claims after this round's own edits.

**Verification:** `npm run typecheck`/`npm run lint` clean; `npm test` 755/755 pass, 0 fail, 0 skipped (728 carried + 27 new: 6 `reference-resolver.test.ts`, 13 `continuation-residual-probe.test.ts`, 8 `completeness-claim-checker.test.ts`); `node src/qa/completeness-claim-checker.ts` PASS (2 files, all claims verified — including the Issue #159 mutation-demonstration: the exact former CHANGELOG.md text, re-tested standalone, is now caught by `findBareClaims`). Real `gh`-backed numerator re-run (`node src/qa/continuation-residual-probe.ts --field=continuation-residual`): ~~`continuation-residual=3`~~, cross-checked against `node src/qa/reference-resolver.ts` full-tree mode's own 3 matching `#000` failures (independent confirmation, not the same code path). **CORRECTED 2026-09-11 (round-5 fix-now, human-ruled — same stale figure as the "Cost, measured for real" paragraph above; see that paragraph's correction for the live command and true value.)** `classifyPath`/`ADR_CANDIDATE_RE`/`resolveWithinRepo`/`checkIssueViaGh`/`resolveIssueCitations`/`parseMaxDistinctIssues` confirmed structurally untouched via `git diff --stat` (the disclosed new CONSUMER of `resolveIssueCitations` lives entirely in the new `continuation-residual-probe.ts` file). Candidate B (diff-scoping) deliberately not attempted — council-rejected as WIDENS, would activate a dormant, currently-harmless `ref`/content-mismatch bug in `marker-corpus-probe.ts` (content always read from the working tree regardless of the `ref` passed); that code path is untouched this round.

**Round-5 fix-now, human-ruled (`docs/decisions.md`'s round-5-hard-stop ruling row, 2026-09-11) — exactly 3 items, no scope expansion**, against `red-team`'s round-5 re-confirm (`docs/reviews/qa14-marker-redesign-red-team-round5-2026-09-11.md`), which found the round-4 build itself froze a live, already-stale figure into the two files above:

**(1) Struck the live stale figures.** `CHANGELOG.md:70`/`:76` and `docs/STATE.md`'s matching build-entry line carried a non-struck ~~`continuation-marked=273 continuation-residual=3`~~ figure that reproduced at no committed tree state (red-team's independent re-measurement found different, true counts at both the round-4 commit and its own baseline). Struck through, never deleted, each now pointing at the live instrument command (`node src/qa/continuation-residual-probe.ts --field=continuation-marked|continuation-residual`) instead of a frozen number. A third, previously-undetected live instance — `docs/STATE.md`'s "Last updated" narrative line, added in a later commit (`fb3da56`) than red-team's own report, quoting the same stale figure — struck too (found because the new BARE_CLAIM_PHRASES pattern below now catches it, not by manual re-grep).

**(2) Extended `BARE_CLAIM_PHRASES`** (`src/qa/completeness-claim-checker.ts`) with `/\bcontinuation-(?:marked|residual)=\d+\b/i` — this project's own real instrument-output naming convention (`continuation-residual-probe.ts --field=...`'s two field names), which the round-4 fix's two `N/M`-anchored patterns never covered (red-team's report measured every one of a set of four plausible next phrasings as missed, including the two figures live in the tree at that round's own HEAD). A blanket `identifier=\d+` shape was considered and rejected — it would false-positive on this project's own existing, legitimate identifier-equals-number pairs already live in the two scanned files (the marker-corpus-probe's marked/unmarked/total figures, a `blocking` count, a `p99` latency figure, an `exitCode`, and a RECEIPT checksum's demonstrated/code-traced tally, plus every marker's own `expect=` value) — same narrow-but-evidence-grounded trade Issue #159 made for the `N/M` shape. Mutation-demonstrated: the exact former stale text is now caught; re-ran the false-positive check against the real corpus (both `DEFAULT_FILES`, plus every legitimate identifier-equals-number shape named above) — zero new false positives. A pre-existing, unrelated bare claim (an "N of M ratified items" phrase, matching the round-3-era `N of M` pattern, introduced by a later untested `docs/STATE.md` narrative edit) also had to be reworded to spell the numbers as words, to get the gate green — disclosed here as a necessary companion fix, not new scope: `node src/qa/completeness-claim-checker.ts` was failing on it at this round's own starting commit, before any of this round's 3 named items were touched.

**(3) Deleted `continuation-residual-probe.ts`'s positional `ref` parameter.** It reproduced verbatim the ref/working-tree content-mismatch bug the design council explicitly rejected Candidate B over in `marker-corpus-probe.ts` (`collectFullTreeFileTexts` took its file LIST from the passed `ref` but always read CONTENT from the working tree via `readFile`, regardless of `ref` — demonstrated: `--field=continuation-marked a26e55a` reported `273` against `a26e55a`'s true content of `271`). Confirmed via grep that no real caller anywhere in this repo (production code, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `package.json` scripts, or the file's own test suite) ever passed a non-default ref, so deleting the parameter — always reading the current working tree, the only state this function ever correctly supported — is the minimal closure, not the larger fix-the-mismatch-properly change. `marker-corpus-probe.ts` deliberately left untouched (a separate, already-closed issue, #150/#155) — no matching one-line change was needed there since this deletion doesn't touch any shared code path. New regression test pins that a stray positional argument (e.g. `a26e55a`) now has zero effect on the real-subprocess output.

**Verification:** `npm run typecheck`/`npm run lint` clean; `npm test` 756/756 pass, 0 fail, 0 skipped (755 carried + 1 new); `node src/qa/completeness-claim-checker.ts` PASS (2 files, all completeness claims verified). Real-subprocess confirmation that the ref bug is gone: `--field=continuation-marked` and `--field=continuation-marked a26e55a` now produce byte-identical stdout — the positional argument has no effect on the reported count (deliberately not quoting that count here — the whole point of this round's fix is to stop freezing this instrument's output into permanently-rescanned prose; run the command for the live number). No ADR governs this surface (re-confirmed against the 35-ADR catalog, sixth+ independent confirmation, unchanged from every prior round). Evidence: this round's own build; `CHANGELOG.md`/`docs/STATE.md`'s own struck corrections; `src/qa/completeness-claim-checker.ts`/`.test.ts`; `src/qa/continuation-residual-probe.ts`/`.test.ts`; `docs/decisions.md`'s human-ruling row (2026-09-11).

**Round-6 fix-now (`docs/decisions.md`'s round-6-re-confirm ruling row, 2026-09-11) — exactly the 2 mechanical items `red-team`'s round-6 re-confirm named (`docs/reviews/qa14-marker-redesign-red-team-round6-2026-09-11.md`), routed directly with no further `red-team` round:**

**(1) Deleted the frozen numbers from the round-5 CORRECTED annotations themselves**, rather than writing a replacement number: `docs/STATE.md:14`/`:21`'s "true value 271/5" was itself wrong (271 is the `a26e55a` baseline commit's value, not round-4 commit `f966a7b`'s true value, 281 — independently confirmed by red-team via git-object recomputation at 5 refs, cross-checked against the shipped `continuation-residual-probe.ts`), and `CHANGELOG.md:70`'s "281/5" — correct but still a fresh frozen count in a permanently-rescanned file — is removed the same way. Both sentences now say only that the prior figure was wrong and point at the live command (`node src/qa/continuation-residual-probe.ts --field=continuation-marked|continuation-residual`) they already carried, per the exact convention every other de-hardcoded claim in this story uses: a correction names the command, never a number.

**(2) Added the missing test coverage for the human-ruled `BARE_CLAIM_PHRASES` extension.** Round-5's `/\bcontinuation-(?:marked|residual)=\d+\b/i` pattern shipped with zero pinning test — `completeness-claim-checker.test.ts` was untouched in that round's diff, and red-team mutation-demonstrated that deleting the pattern left `npm test` 756/756 pass and `node src/qa/completeness-claim-checker.ts` PASS, both green. Three new tests added: `findBareClaims` catches a reintroduced `continuation-marked=N`, catches a reintroduced `continuation-residual=N`, and correctly ignores the same shape struck through (`~~...~~`, superseded per this project's own convention). Mutation-verified directly: removed the pattern, confirmed both catch-cases went red (`0 !== 1`), reverted (byte-identical restore), confirmed every new case green again. No change to the pattern's own shape or coverage ceiling — `marker-corpus-probe.ts` and `continuation-residual-probe.ts`'s (already-fixed) ref handling deliberately untouched, exactly as ruled.

Commented on Issue #161 (closed by `red-team`'s round-6 re-confirm; not reopened) noting its title names `marker-corpus-probe.ts`'s ref/content-mismatch bug but its body and close comment are scoped to the now-fixed `continuation-residual-probe.ts` twin — the still-live `marker-corpus-probe.ts` bug is tracked separately by Issue #164.
