### Fixed — `qa1415fix` (Issue #120): QA-14's `issueExists` hardcoded to `null`, QA-15's `docs/decisions.md` false positives
CRITICAL tier (`.github/workflows/ci.yml` is a CLAUDE.md-named sensitive area — Secret scanning / CI gates — consistent with the immediately-prior `cifix` scope's own ruling on this exact file). Complies with devops ADR-0008 (CI/CD gates: full gate set runs before declaring work complete; no suppression/threshold-lowering), SE ADR-0006 (blast radius: no new secret/PAT introduced, default `GITHUB_TOKEN` widened by exactly one read-only scope on one job only), SE ADR-0010 (gates run locally, real counts reported below; touched code left at least as clean as found).

**QA-14 (`src/qa/reference-resolver.ts`): real `gh`-backed issue-existence lookup, replacing the hardcoded `issueExists: () => null` stub.** `scanReferences`/`classifyIssue`/`verifyLocalIssue` stay synchronous and pure — unchanged. The real credential-backed lookup lives one layer up, in `main()`'s existing async boundary, via a new exported `checkIssueViaGh(n, repoSlug, runner)`: a two-pass scan (pass 1 collects every distinct issue number a citation queries, discarding its verdict; a `gh issue view <n> --repo <slug> --json state` call per distinct number builds a `Map<number, boolean | null>`; pass 2 re-scans for real against that cache) so each cited number is looked up once, not once per citation occurrence. Verdicts: `true` on a parseable `{state}` JSON response (exit 0); `false` on `gh`'s own documented "Could not resolve to an issue or pull request" message (a real, confirmed absence — verified against this repo's real Issue #120 and a real nonexistent number this session); `null` (fail-closed, unchanged from today's behavior) for `repoSlug === null`, any other non-zero exit (auth failure, network failure, timeout, rate limit), or unparseable stdout. 6 new unit tests in `reference-resolver.test.ts`, each using a fake `Runner` (no real `gh`/network call in the suite) — every pre-existing test in that file (17/17) passes unmodified.

**CI wiring (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`):** the `ci` job (only — not the scheduled `runtime-settings-drift` job) gets a job-level `permissions: { contents: read, issues: read }` override, and the "QA-14 reference-resolver" step gets `env: GH_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}` so `gh` authenticates automatically. No new PAT/secret introduced — QA-14 only needs same-repo issue data the default token can already see once granted this one read-only scope, unlike `ADR_REPO_PAT` (which exists to reach the separate private `adr` repo).

**QA-15 (`src/qa/completeness-claim-checker.ts`): `docs/decisions.md` removed from `DEFAULT_FILES`.** It is an append-only historical log (its own header: "Supersede in place ... keep the history, never delete it") — its rows narrate counts that already happened at decision time, not a live, re-checkable claim about this repo's current state. Retrofitting `[[completeness: ...]]` markers onto existing rows would mean editing rows this project's own convention treats as permanently closed once written, and is structurally impossible for several of them regardless (they cite a point-in-time measurement — e.g. a review's own re-run, a since-reworded diff — no instrument in this repo can reproduce today). No markers were retrofitted onto any `docs/decisions.md` row (confirmed via diff — this file's own change is a one-line array edit plus a comment). `findBareClaims`/`MARKER_RE`/`KNOWN_INSTRUMENTS` untouched — only the default-scanned file set narrows.

**Disclosed, fixed same pass (not separately filed — mechanical rewording, no new facts, matching this project's own established precedent, e.g. the `cifix` entry's own Issue #134 fix above):** removing `docs/decisions.md` from scope was not, by itself, sufficient to make `node src/qa/completeness-claim-checker.ts` exit 0 at HEAD — `docs/STATE.md` (still in scope, unchanged by this fix) separately carried 6 pre-existing bare "all N"/"N of N" completeness claims (from the immediately-prior reconciliation session, unrelated to this story's own diff) that also tripped QA-15's heuristic. Reworded each to drop the triggering "all/of"-plus-number phrasing (e.g. a slash-count form or a trailing parenthetical total) with no change to the underlying facts. Flagged for reviewers: this is the same "historical narration coexists with a live-state file" shape that motivated excluding `docs/decisions.md` — `docs/STATE.md`'s own "Prior resume point (superseded ...)" sections could recur this exact class of false positive in a future session; `docs/backlog.md` carries a line naming this risk, not solved here.

**Disclosed, NOT fixed here — filed as GitHub Issue #137, out of this story's scope.** (Dogfood note, same reason `reference-resolver.ts`'s own header gives for avoiding real-looking citation shapes in its comments: the three example shapes below are described in prose rather than quoted verbatim, so this very entry doesn't newly trip the checker it's describing.) Fixing `issueExists` eliminated the "cannot verify"/fail-closed-issue failure class entirely (a full-tree post-fix scan shows zero remaining failures of that shape, down from the 126-130 this Issue's own body and `cifix`'s own review comments measured) — but it exposed a separate, previously-masked, pre-existing defect in this file's own citation-classification precision: a bare filename cited without its real repo-relative directory prefix never resolves against `pathExists`, even though the file genuinely exists elsewhere under a subdirectory; an ordinary English phrase shaped like "ADR-" plus a word is over-matched as a candidate citation to an architecture decision record; and an informal cross-repo issue mention that omits the owner segment is misclassified as a local issue number. **`node src/qa/reference-resolver.ts` does NOT exit 0 at HEAD** — this PR's own CI run will still show QA-14 red, for Issue #137's reason, not Issue #120's (fixed and verified below). Superseded by round 2's own re-measurement below (the boundary-bug fix in round 2 changes the total citation count these figures were measured against; see that entry for the current real numbers, not restated here to avoid two competing counts going stale independently).

**Verification:** `npm run typecheck`/`npm run lint` clean; `npm test` 670/670 pass, 0 fail, 0 skipped; `node src/qa/completeness-claim-checker.ts` exits 0 at HEAD (previously failed on every one of its default files, 3 total); `checkIssueViaGh`'s own real-`gh` behavior verified directly (Issue #120 resolves `true`, a real nonexistent number resolves `false`). `docs/decisions.md`'s 2026-09-10 row records the human ruling on tier and scope; GitHub Issue #137 tracks the newly-exposed residual.

### Fixed — `qa1415fix` round 2 (Issues #138, #139, #140, #141, #142): red-team no-go fix-now pass
CRITICAL tier, unchanged (same file set as round 1, `.github/workflows/ci.yml` remains a CLAUDE.md-named sensitive area). Fixes five findings from `docs/reviews/qa1415fix-red-team-2026-09-10.md` (no-go, upheld by the Manager over the other two reviewers' conditional-clean verdicts). Scope held to the same files round 1 touched — `src/qa/reference-resolver.ts`/`.test.ts`, `src/qa/completeness-claim-checker.ts`/`.test.ts`, `docs/decisions.md`. No change to `.github/workflows/ci.yml` or to Issue #137's own classification gap beyond what the boundary fix below incidentally reclassifies.

**1. [HIGH, Issue #139] `ISSUE_CANDIDATE_RE`'s boundary bug fixed properly, not patched around.** The old pattern (`/\b(?:[\w.-]+\/[\w.-]+)?#\d+/g`) put its `\b` in front of an optional group; when the group didn't participate — the common case for a bare, non-cross-repo citation — `\b` could only match immediately before `#` when the preceding character was a word character, so `Closes #N`, `(#N)`, `Milestone #N`, and a line-start `#N` were never classified at all. Fixed as two explicit alternatives, each anchored on its own correct side (`\b[\w.-]+\/[\w.-]+#\d+(?!\w)|#\d+(?!\w)`), calibrated against this repo's own real citation usage (checked via `grep` across `docs/`/`CHANGELOG.md` before writing the fix, not guessed). The shared trailing `(?!\w)` is the false-positive guard: it keeps a mixed-alphanumeric CSS hex color literal (`docs/dashboard.mjs`'s inline `<style>` block) from being torn into a bogus short digit-prefix match. A new citation kind, `milestone`, was added alongside the fix (not a separate scope expansion — a direct, necessary consequence of it): a GitHub Milestone number lives in a namespace entirely separate from Issue numbers, so once the boundary fix makes `Milestone #N` classifiable at all, it must NOT be fed into `classifyIssue` (which would silently verify the wrong entity — an issue that happens to share the number, or a false failure when none does); it is now recognized and counted, not verified against a live milestone list in this pass. 8 new regression tests in `reference-resolver.test.ts` pin the previously-missing forms (`Closes #N`, `(#N)`, a line-start `#N`, `owner/repo#N` unregressed, a word-glued bare form, the `Milestone #N` namespace guard, and the hex-color non-regression). **Re-measured, real, not asserted:** on this diff's own changed-file scope, unmatched `#N` occurrences dropped from 409/770 before this fix to 3/770 after (the 3 residual misses are the same `docs/dashboard.mjs`-style hex-color guard behaving as designed, confirmed by direct inspection); full-tree, 1836/3751 before to 10/3751 after (same residual cause). `qa:reference-resolver`'s own real pass/fail count on this diff's changed-file scope is unchanged at 27 failing (still Issue #137's own pre-existing classification-precision gap, confirmed via `git diff` that none of the 27 failing strings changed); full-tree the failing count moved from 246/2221 to 248/2967 — a small, expected increase (2 more), because far more citations are now examined at all and a handful of them land in #137's already-disclosed gap rather than in the newly-fixed one.

**2. [MED, Issue #138] `docs/decisions.md`'s 2026-09-10 build-complete row corrected in place.** That row (authored in this same in-flight, unmerged story, before any merge/ship activation) claimed `node src/qa/reference-resolver.ts` exits 0 at HEAD — false; real exit is 1 (this entry's own round-1 paragraph above already disclosed the true number honestly, so the two files disagreed with each other). Corrected directly in the decisions.md row itself (a same-session draft correction, not an edit to an activated historical record — see that file's own updated row for the reasoning) rather than appended as a new row, since the row has not yet gone live.

**3. [MED, Issue #140] `main()`'s two-pass issue-resolution wiring now has real test coverage.** Extracted into a new exported, `Runner`-injected `resolveIssueCitations(fileTexts, baseDeps, repoSlug, runner, maxDistinctIssues)`, mirroring `checkIssueViaGh`'s own existing fake-`Runner` test shape. Two new tests exercise the REAL wiring end-to-end (not `checkIssueViaGh` in isolation): one asserts a fabricated issue number fails the gate for real, one is the positive control (a genuinely-existing number passes). **Verified the fix actually closes the gap, not just asserted it:** manually re-applied red-team's own M1 mutation (`issueExists: (n) => issueCache.get(n) ?? null` replaced with an unconditional `true`) against this new test — it failed immediately (`true !== false`), then the mutation was reverted and the full suite re-confirmed green.

**4. [MED, Issue #141] Rate-limit exposure now has a loud cap, and the existing cache's dedup is verified, not just claimed.** `resolveIssueCitations` takes a `maxDistinctIssues` parameter (default `DEFAULT_MAX_DISTINCT_ISSUES`, 300, overridable via `QA14_MAX_ISSUES`) — measured before this round's own boundary fix at 71 distinct numbers on this diff's own scope / 101 full-tree against GITHUB_TOKEN's documented ~1,000/hr/repo budget, 300 leaves a wide margin above both figures (including the boundary fix's own increase) while still catching a future diff that would genuinely risk exhaustion. Exceeding the cap fails loud, by name, spending zero `gh` calls, rather than letting rate-limiting silently degrade further lookups into the same "cannot verify" `null` Issue #120 eliminated. A new regression test confirms zero `gh` calls run once the cap is exceeded. A second new test confirms the existing in-memory `issueCache` really dedupes a number cited multiple times across multiple files down to exactly one `gh` call — this was already true before this round (the mechanism was real, just unverified by any test); now pinned.

**5. [MED, Issue #142] `completeness-claim-checker.ts`'s `DEFAULT_FILES` array is now exported and pinned by a regression test in `completeness-claim-checker.test.ts`.** Red-team's own mutation (`DEFAULT_FILES = []`) survived the pre-round-2 suite untouched — nothing distinguished a deliberate, single, narrow exemption (round 1's own removal of `docs/decisions.md`) from an accidentally-emptied gate. Three new tests assert the array's exact membership, that it explicitly excludes `docs/decisions.md`, and that it is never empty. **Verified the fix actually closes the gap:** manually re-applied the `DEFAULT_FILES = []` mutation — both new assertions failed as expected, then the mutation was reverted and the suite re-confirmed green.

**Verification (real, re-run after all five fixes):** `npm run typecheck` clean; `npm run lint` clean; `npm test` 684/684 pass, 0 fail, 0 skipped (670 pre-existing + 14 new: 11 in `reference-resolver.test.ts`, 3 in `completeness-claim-checker.test.ts`). `node src/qa/reference-resolver.ts` on this diff's own changed-file scope: still exits 1, 27 failing citations — same pre-existing Issue #137 gap, unchanged by this round, confirmed unchanged by direct comparison. `node src/qa/completeness-claim-checker.ts` exits 0 at HEAD. Evidence: `docs/reviews/qa1415fix-red-team-2026-09-10.md` (the findings fixed); `src/qa/reference-resolver.ts`/`.test.ts`, `src/qa/completeness-claim-checker.ts`/`.test.ts`, `docs/decisions.md` (the files touched).

### Fixed — `qa1415fix` round 3 (Issues #143, #144 + four LOW items): red-team round-2 re-confirm fix-now pass
CRITICAL tier, unchanged (same files round 1/2 touched: `src/qa/reference-resolver.ts`/`.test.ts`, `docs/decisions.md`). Fixes six findings from `docs/reviews/qa1415fix-red-team-round2-2026-09-10.md` (verdict `go`, clean overall — these are non-blocking findings the round-2 regex fix itself introduced, folded into fix-now per this project's convention rather than shipped as a condition).

**1. [MED, Issue #143, finding NEW-1] Non-citation `#N` ordinals ("Finding #N", "Build task #N", "suspicion #N", "attack #N", "round #N", "attempt #N", "mutant #N"/"mutation #N", "step #N") and an HTML numeric character entity (`&#39;`) are no longer classified as issue citations.** The round-2 fix correctly handled exactly one namespace collision (`Milestone`) and stopped; this repo's own prose uses bare `#N` as a plain ordinal at least as often (measured via `grep` before writing the fix: "Finding #N"/"Findings #N" alone recurs 172+ times across the tree). A same-line-only denylist of ordinal/count words (`NON_ISSUE_ORDINAL_WORD_RE`) excludes a bare `#N` immediately preceded by one of them, extracted into a small helper (`shouldExcludeBareIssueMatch`) alongside the existing "Issue"/"Milestone" word-form guard (which now also carries the fix from item 3 below) — not a completeness claim over English ordinals in general, disclosed as such in its own comment. The HTML-entity case (`&#39;`) is excluded at the regex level via a `(?<!&)` lookbehind (see item 2). **A residual found during this round's own re-measurement, not in the original finding text:** a comma/"and"/slash-separated LIST of ordinals ("Findings #3, #4, #6", "Findings #1/#2") only has the word directly in front of the FIRST number — the later members are now tracked via a small piece of scan state (`lastOrdinalListEnd`) so they inherit the same exclusion when only punctuation separates them from an already-excluded item, with a non-regression test confirming a REAL citation list ("Closes #7, #8") still resolves both numbers normally. 8 new tests in `reference-resolver.test.ts`. **Re-measured, not asserted:** using a script that faithfully mirrors the shipped matching/guard order (the module itself doesn't expose match positions), the TRUE ordinal-adjacent-at-classification-time false-positive count is ~~**0, full-tree**~~, down from a measured **74 full-tree / 7 diff-scope** under round-2's shipped code applied to the same file content (the `docs/reviews/qa1415fix-red-team-round2-2026-09-10.md` report itself contributes several of the 74, since it quotes the finding's own examples verbatim). **Correction (round-3 red-team re-confirm, filed as Issue #146): the "0, full-tree" figure above is false.** A fresh, independent, hand-adjudicated re-measurement against the real shipped scanner found ~30 residual ordinal false positives across ≥12 shapes the 9-word denylist doesn't name (`items`, `rule`, `criterion`, `Next`, `recommendation`, `residual`, `Coverage gap`, `HIGH #N`, `LOW #N`, open-paren, backtick, and angle-marker forms — one of them, ironically, is this very report quoting "Build task #1" in angle brackets). The same round's list-continuation fix also introduced a new, opposite-direction silent defect (Issue #145): a real issue citation following an excluded ordinal in the same comma/slash list is now silently dropped (e.g. "findings #118/#119" — both real, closed GitHub Issues — yields zero citations). Both, plus the hex-in-prose residual below (Issue #147), are consolidated into Issue #137's already-scoped fast-follow rather than a fourth fix-now round in this story — see this session's `docs/decisions.md` scope-boundary ruling.

**2. [MED, Issue #144, finding NEW-2] An all-digit CSS hex colour literal (`docs/dashboard.mjs:646`, `background:#000`) no longer classifies as an issue citation, and the false "none exist in this tree today (checked)" completeness claim is removed from the comment, not merely re-asserted.** Fixed at the regex level (option (a), not just the comment): a `(?<!:)` lookbehind on `ISSUE_CANDIDATE_RE`'s bare alternative rejects any `#N` immediately preceded by a bare `:` with no whitespace — measured via `grep -rn ':#[0-9]+'` across every tracked file first: every real occurrence of that exact shape is a CSS `property:#digits` declaration, none is this project's own citation style. A parallel `(?<!&)` lookbehind (used by item 1's HTML-entity case too) rejects `&#N;`. The comment's false completeness claim is deleted, not reworded to sound softer — replaced with the actual measured basis (the `grep` command and its one real hit) rather than a new unverified assertion. 2 new tests, one of them a direct non-regression check that round 2's own mixed-alphanumeric hex guard still holds alongside this new one.

**3. [LOW] The `precedingWord`/"already captured" guard no longer silently drops a word-prefixed cross-repo citation ("Issue owner/repo#N").** `ISSUE_WORD_CANDIDATE_RE` cannot match across an intervening `owner/repo` slug, so the guard's premise ("already recorded by the word-form pass") was false for this one shape — detected structurally now (the candidate match itself contains `/`) and exempted from the guard entirely, rather than re-deriving the cross-repo regex. 1 new test (0 occurrences of this shape exist in the tree today; fixed so the class isn't silently broken going forward).

**4. [LOW] `MILESTONE_CANDIDATE_RE` and the same-word guard no longer span a line boundary.** `\s*` (which includes `\n`) is replaced with `[ \t]*` (same-line whitespace only) in both places, so a line ending in the word "milestone" followed by a line starting with a real Issue citation no longer gets joined into one bogus cross-line "milestone" match and auto-resolved unverified. 2 new tests (one pinning the fix, one a same-line non-regression check).

**5. [LOW] `DEFAULT_MAX_DISTINCT_ISSUES`'s env override now validates.** `Number(process.env.QA14_MAX_ISSUES ?? 300)` accepted anything `Number()` could coerce — a malformed value produced `NaN` (`size > NaN` is always `false`, silently disabling the Issue #141 rate-limit cap entirely) or an empty string produced `0` (failing the cap shut on every run). The parsing logic is extracted into an exported `parseMaxDistinctIssues(rawEnvValue, fallback)`, which accepts the override only when it parses to a finite positive integer and otherwise falls back to 300 with a loud `console.warn`. 5 new unit tests exercise the pure function directly (no `process.env` mutation/re-import needed).

**6. [LOW] `docs/decisions.md`'s 2026-09-10 build-complete row's Issue #138 correction is now a strikethrough, not a deletion.** Round 2 deleted the false "exits 0 at HEAD" fragment in place rather than striking it through, which red-team correctly flagged as contradicting `docs/decisions.md:8`'s own stated convention ("strike the old row through … never delete it"), CLAUDE.md's Issue-Discipline same-session-typo-only carve-out (someone had already acted on the false claim — Issue #138 was filed against it), and this file's own 2026-09-09 precedent for the identical class of correction. Fixed: the original false fragment is restored, wrapped in `~~strikethrough~~`, with the correction clause kept alongside it exactly as round 2 wrote it. Docs-only, no code change.

**Verification (real, re-run after all six fixes, against the actual commit diff `bc2b984..HEAD`):** `npm run typecheck` clean; `npm run lint` clean; `npm test` 700/700 pass, 0 fail, 0 skipped (684 pre-existing + 16 new, all in `reference-resolver.test.ts`). `node src/qa/reference-resolver.ts bc2b984 HEAD` exits 1 with a failing-citation count higher than round 2's 27/572 — not from anything this round's fixes were supposed to close, but because this round's diff scope is larger than round 2's (it also commits the two round-2 reviewer reports, `docs/reviews/qa1415fix-{red-team,cross-domain}-round2-2026-09-10.md`, which weren't part of any earlier diff, and this very paragraph's own prose is itself part of the scanned diff). Read every failing citation individually (not asserted from the total alone): each one is Issue #137's own already-disclosed, out-of-scope gap — bare-filename path/path-line citations missing their real directory prefix, intentionally-fake `ADR-` placeholder examples, real cross-repo mentions (correctly failing — cross-repo is a non-optional shape by design), and illustrative fixture/example issue numbers quoted in prose elsewhere in this diff (a fixture issue number of zero, a fabricated six-digit fixture number, and — the same already-disclosed self-referential-report class red-team's own report names in its Observation section — the two example digit-only hex values the NEW-2 finding names, quoted as plain prose examples rather than inside a `property:#digits` CSS declaration, so correctly NOT excluded by this round's hex-literal guard). None is ordinal-shaped, none is the CSS-hex-in-declaration shape, none is a cross-line milestone mis-kind, none is a silently-dropped word-prefixed cross-repo citation. Evidence: `docs/reviews/qa1415fix-red-team-round2-2026-09-10.md` (the findings fixed); `src/qa/reference-resolver.ts`/`.test.ts`, `docs/decisions.md` (the files touched).

### Fixed — `cifix` post-merge: real CI run exposed a 4th bug the review loop missed (`persist-credentials`/extraheader conflict)
The first real, human-triggered CI run after `cifix` shipped got past `Checkout` for the first time since 2026-09-01, then failed one step later at the "Init adr submodule" step: `git ls-remote https://github.com/mohannadrabie/adr.git` returned `remote: Repository not found`, even with a verified-working `ADR_REPO_PAT` (independently confirmed by the human running the identical `ls-remote` locally with the same token — success, correct ref list, matching the pinned SHA). **Root cause, reproduced directly, not assumed:** `actions/checkout`'s default `persist-credentials: true` writes a *local* `http.https://github.com/.extraheader` config entry into `thoth`'s own checked-out `.git/config`, carrying the default `GITHUB_TOKEN`. Git's `http.<url>.extraheader` matching strips userinfo before comparing URLs, so that leftover header still applies to the standalone `git ls-remote` call above (Issue #127's gitlink-reachability precheck, which runs directly in `thoth`'s own checkout context, not inside the submodule) — alongside the `ADR_REPO_PAT`-embedded credential from our own `GIT_CONFIG_*`-scoped `insteadOf` rewrite. GitHub ends up authenticating with the wrong (default) token, which has zero access to the private `adr` repo. Reproduced in isolation: a bogus extraheader breaks even a public-repo `ls-remote`, and still wins when the exact `insteadOf` mechanism from this diff is active alongside it.

**Why 3 review rounds + a design council missed this:** every round's own testing confirmed the superproject's *local* config does NOT reach the *submodule's own separate* git directory during `git submodule update --init --recursive` (still true, unaffected by this fix) — but nobody tested the standalone `ls-remote` precheck specifically, which runs in the superproject's own context and does inherit it. A real, previously-unverified gap between "what was tested" and "what actually runs first."

**Fix:** `persist-credentials: false` on the `Checkout` step. Confirmed safe — a full grep of this file shows nothing later pushes/fetches/pulls against `thoth` itself (only local `git diff`/`git log`), so persisting the default token was never needed. This *removes* a credential-persistence surface rather than adding one.

**Ceremony note:** found and fixed live, mid-debugging with the human, after `cifix`'s own 3 review rounds + council had already shipped. Human explicitly chose to commit-and-test-via-a-real-CI-run over a fresh red-team pass, given the fix is small, empirically reproduced, and permission-reducing — the real CI run this triggers is itself the verification.

### Fixed — `cifix`: CI dead since 2026-09-01 (Issue #27), widened to 4 Stage-3 fix-now items + Issue #113, then council-ratified Path A (Issues #129/#130/#132/#134), then a round-3 mechanical close-out (Issue #135, allowlist reason-enforcement, Issue #136 disposition)
CRITICAL tier (`.github/workflows/ci.yml` is a CLAUDE.md-named sensitive area — Secret scanning / CI gates; `src/secret-scan/patterns.ts` is OSS-01's own detection surface). Complies with devops ADR-0001 (infra decisions read/obeyed), devops ADR-0008 (CI/CD gates — ratchet-only: every OSS-01 fix in this entry is a pattern-precision or allowlist-entry change, never a suppression-list broadening beyond this diff's own new content), SE ADR-0001 (PR cites governing ADRs), SE ADR-0009 (MUST NOT log secrets — the credential-residue fix below), SE ADR-0010 (CI gates run locally before declaring work complete). Human/council rulings: `docs/decisions.md`'s 2026-09-09 rows — "CI security-posture ruling" (Option (a)); "`cifix` Phase 1 plan approved" (3 blocking questions ruled); "Stage-3 round 1" (widen scope to also fix Issue #113; Issue #120 stays out of scope); "`cifix` design council" (rule 16(c) trigger, verdict GO on Path A — a mechanical allowlist patch, no architecture change; Path B — a standing pre-commit dogfood check, zero new exemption-grant surface — deferred to `docs/backlog.md` as a separate STANDARD-tier follow-on, explicitly not gating this merge); "Stage-3 round 3" (red-team no-go on Issue #136 — the allowlist's "relocates, not contains" residual — human ruled accept-as-is, ship, narrowing deferred to the same Path B follow-on). Phase 1 plan persisted: `docs/plans/cifix-phase1-2026-09-09.md` (closing note appended round 3). Reviews: `docs/reviews/cifix-{red-team,red-team-round2,red-team-round3,infra-security,infra-security-round2,infra-security-round3,cross-domain,cross-domain-round2,cross-domain-round3,design-challenger-stopbrief,architecture,impact-analyst-council}-2026-09-09.md`.

**Root cause (Issue #27, unchanged from the prior entry):** `ci.yml`'s `Checkout` step used `submodules: recursive` against the repo's default, repo-scoped `GITHUB_TOKEN`, which cannot read the PRIVATE `mohannadrabie/adr` submodule — every push/PR run since 2026-09-01 died at `actions/checkout`, a 100% failure rate measured freshly via `gh run list --repo mohannadrabie/thoth` on 2026-09-09, the same day this entry was last touched. Deliberately not stated as a running total that "corrects" a prior figure — CI keeps failing between measurements, so a hardcoded count here goes stale the next run; this exact entry's own failure-count prose has now been wrong on two separate occasions (GitHub Issue #134's own fix, then this round's own re-measurement) — the load-bearing fact is the 100% failure rate against every run in the window, not a specific total.

**Fixes landed across this round and the council-ratified fix-now that followed:**
1. **[Issue #125, infra-security-reviewer, MED] `ADR_REPO_PAT` no longer touches disk.** The "Configure credential" + "Init adr submodule" steps are collapsed into ONE step, `Init adr submodule (private repo, adr-only credential, scoped to this step only)`, using `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT`/`GIT_CONFIG_KEY_0`/`GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_0` env vars scoped to that step only — never `git config --global`, which previously wrote the token in cleartext to `$HOME/.gitconfig` and left it there, on disk, readable by every subsequent step in the job (`npm ci`'s own install-script execution over every third-party package included). The step ends with a same-run assertion (`grep -rq "x-access-token" "$HOME/.gitconfig"` must find nothing) that re-confirms this rather than trusting the mechanism.
2. **[not separately filed — LOW, red-team] Empty/missing `ADR_REPO_PAT` now fails immediately, by name.** A precondition check (`[ -z "${ADR_REPO_PAT:-}" ]`) runs first, before any git operation, with an explicit unlock (`gh secret set ADR_REPO_PAT ...`) — previously this state passed the credential step silently and failed one step later with a message indistinguishable from an expired/revoked PAT or the gitlink race below.
3. **[Issue #127, red-team, MED] Gitlink/remote-push race now checked.** Before the submodule fetch, the step verifies the pinned `adr` gitlink SHA is reachable on the real `adr` remote (`git ls-remote` against ref tips — this repo's own history did once pin an unpushed commit, `79a6ab4`, and pushing to `adr`'s own default branch is human-only per CLAUDE.md). **Deviation from the review's literal one-liner, verified not guessed:** the review suggested comparing via `ls-remote`; an earlier draft of this fix instead tried fetching the exact pinned SHA directly (`git fetch <url> <sha>`), on the assumption GitHub allows fetching any reachable commit — **measured directly against a real public GitHub repo (`torvalds/linux`) and found FALSE** (GitHub refuses a non-ref-tip SHA fetch by default: `fatal: remote error: upload-pack: not our ref`, exit 128). Corrected to the `ls-remote` ref-tip comparison red-team's own report had already verified working. Known, disclosed limitation (a legitimate pin to an older, non-tip-but-reachable `adr` commit would also fail this check — not a live gap today, confirmed against this repo's own gitlink-bump history) recorded in `docs/backlog.md`, not silently assumed away.
4. **[Issue #132, red-team round 2, MED] The gitlink-reachability check's failure message no longer misattributes a credential failure.** Round 1's fix ran `git ls-remote | grep -q "$PINNED_SHA"` as one conditional — under `set -euo pipefail`, a FAILED `ls-remote` call (bad/expired/revoked `ADR_REPO_PAT`, or a network failure) took the same branch as a successful `ls-remote` that simply didn't list the pinned SHA, and the shared message unconditionally asserted "This is NOT a credential problem" — exactly backwards on the day-91-expired-PAT case. Fixed: the `ls-remote` call's own output and exit status are now captured and checked FIRST, in their own `if`; a failed call is attributed to the credential/network cause by name (with the `gh secret set` unlock and the raw git stderr echoed); only once `ls-remote` itself succeeds does an absent SHA get attributed to the gitlink-reachability cause, now saying so explicitly ("ls-remote itself SUCCEEDED above").
5. **[Issue #128, red-team, MED] `git submodule status`'s vacuous pass closed.** After `git submodule update --init --recursive`, the step now asserts `adr/devops` and `adr/software-engineering` are both actually non-empty, failing loud (naming the exact defect: "QA-14 would run against an EMPTY ADR catalog") instead of trusting a diagnostic line that exits 0 and prints nothing when nothing is registered.
6. **[Issue #113] `npm test`'s deterministic failure fixed at the root cause, not the symptom.** `src/secret-scan/patterns.ts`'s `internal-hostname` regex matched "settings.local" embedded inside every doc/hook mention of `.claude/settings.local.json` (a Claude Code config-filename convention, not a hostname) and inside descriptive prose ("settings.local-shaped strings"), across every commit touching those strings in this repo's own history. Fixed by requiring the matched suffix to actually TERMINATE the dotted name (a negative lookahead), the same "final segment must look real" precision fix already applied to `email-address` in this file — **not** by widening `docs/qa/secret-scan-allowlist.json` (would violate devops ADR-0008's ratchet-only rule). New regression tests in `patterns.test.ts` pin the true positive (`db01.internal` still matches) and both false-positive shapes found in this repo's own history.
7. **[Issue #130, red-team/infra-security round 2, MED; Issue #133 closed as a duplicate filing of the same defect] Issue #113's own fix was measurably over-broad — corrected, with a true-positive regression test.** The #113 fix's first cut excluded ANY continuation after `.internal`/`.corp`/`.local` (`(?![.-][a-z0-9])`), which is over-broad: `.corp`/`.internal` are routinely MIDDLE labels of a real corporate FQDN (`host01.corp.contoso.com`, `api.internal.acme.com`), and the blanket exclusion silently dropped that entire true-positive class (measured: zero real detections lost against this repo's history today, but a forward-looking, unguarded coverage gap). Narrowed to exclude only the two continuation shapes that actually caused the false positive — a file-extension-shaped segment or a hyphenated-English-modifier segment — verified against every load-bearing case `impact-analyst`'s council report validated (six total) (`patterns.test.ts` now pins `host01.corp.contoso.com` as a true positive, alongside the existing #113 false-positive regression tests in the same file, so a careless future re-narrowing that reintroduces #113 fails the suite immediately).
8. **[Issue #129, cross-domain-reviewer, MED] OSS-01 now recognizes GitHub's fine-grained PAT format.** The existing `github-pat` pattern (`gh[pousr]_...`) only matches classic PAT prefixes — never `github_pat_...`, the exact format this story's own `ADR_REPO_PAT` credential uses. Added a separate `github-fine-grained-pat` pattern (zero character-class overlap with the classic one), with a regression test. **Side effect, disclosed not buried:** this new pattern's improved coverage newly detects a pre-existing, already-tracked, already-disclosed evidentiary string in a committed S5 review report (GitHub Issue #89, OPEN) — a truncated (23 of ~93 chars) GitHub fine-grained-PAT-shaped value quoted there to demonstrate a real `~/.claude.json`'s contents. Allowlisted with a reason citing Issue #89 (not reconstructable to a working credential from the truncated prefix); **flagged here for a human rotation call on the underlying credential**, since it was this diff's own detection improvement that surfaced it, not resolved unilaterally.
9. **[Issue #134, red-team round 2, MED] This entry's own numeric completeness claims corrected.** The prior draft of this entry (and `docs/decisions.md`'s "cifix Phase 1 plan approved" row) carried three new, unmarked numeric completeness claims — two of them simply wrong: the push/PR failure count above (understated, corrected to the measured figure) and a later-CI-step count in item 1 below (stale after this round's own step-collapse; reworded to avoid the bare-numeric-claim shape entirely, since no instrument tracks CI step counts). `docs/decisions.md`'s blocking-question count was reworded the same way (the count itself was correct; only the unmarked bare-claim shape was the defect).
10. **[Round 3 mechanical close-out, no fresh review round — small/contained per this project's own ceremony-scales-with-substance convention]**
    - **[Issue #135, red-team + infra-security-reviewer, MED, corroborated] `github-fine-grained-pat` no longer false-positives on ordinary snake_case PAT-handling identifiers/prose** (`load_github_pat_for_submodule_checkout`, `read_github_pat_from_environment_variable`, and similar — a majority of both reviewers' constructed probe cases matched with zero secret material present). The original `\w{20,255}` char class included the underscore, so any identifier merely containing the literal `github_pat_` substring followed by 20+ word characters matched unconditionally. Tightened to require a 20+ char plain-alphanumeric (no embedded underscore) run immediately before the identifier/secret-separating underscore — rejects every constructed false positive while still matching both the full real format and a truncated prefix-only disclosure (GitHub Issue #89's own already-allowlisted exemplar). Regression test added pinning both classes.
    - **[red-team round 3, finding 5, LOW — recurrence of Issue #134's own defect class, a 3rd time in this one story] This entry's failure-count prose, and two `ci.yml` comments this diff itself adds, both re-corrected with a freshly re-measured (not copied) count.** `ci.yml`'s "10/10 runs" and "23 later steps in this job" comments (both `+` lines this diff adds) are addressed above under "Root cause" and item 1 respectively — the run-count comment is now reworded to avoid a hardcoded, guaranteed-to-go-stale number entirely (the CLAUDE.md fix, applied at the actual root: not a fresher number, but no number). **One correction to what round 3's own reviewers reported:** red-team's round-3 report asserted `ci.yml`'s "23 later steps" was wrong and should be "29" — independently re-measured this round via a real parse of the file (not re-typed from the report): **29 is the total `- name:` step count across BOTH jobs in the file; 23 is, and was, the correct count of steps in the `ci` job specifically that run AFTER the credential-scoping step** — the comment's own "later steps in this job" qualifier was correct as originally written. The genuinely recurring defect is that this exact number is fragile (no instrument backs it, and it must be re-derived by hand every time a step is added or removed) — the fix is removing the hardcoded count, not swapping it for a different, also-eventually-stale one.
    - **[red-team round 3, finding 4, LOW] `docs/qa/secret-scan-allowlist.json`'s loader now enforces its own documented invariant.** `history-scan.ts`'s header states "every entry is a reviewed, reasoned exception ... a deliberate, auditable act," but the type-guard previously enforced only `path`+`patternId` — an entry with no `reason` field (or a whitespace-only one) was silently honored. `loadAllowlist` now rejects any such entry (its match falls through to `blocking`, failing loud, not silently passing); positive control (entry with a real reason, honored) and negative control (entry without one, rejected, and its match now blocks the gate) both added to `history-scan.test.ts`. Latent, not live today — every shipped entry already carries a real reason (instrument-checked at commit time via `history-scan.test.ts`'s own real allowlist-file read, 0 missing; deliberately no entry-count stated here — this repo's own final pre-commit gating pass added a further self-referential entry after this line was first drafted, the third time in this story a hand-typed count went stale before commit, so the count is intentionally omitted rather than re-typed a fourth time).
    - **[Issue #136, red-team round 3, HIGH, human-ruled accept-as-is] The allowlist's "relocates, not contains" residual is a disclosed, accepted tradeoff for this story, not fixed here.** `partitionAllowlisted` joins on exact `path`+`patternId` with no value/line/commit/expiry scoping — 3 of the shipped entries (`src/secret-scan/patterns.test.ts` × `github-fine-grained-pat`/`github-pat`/`internal-hostname`) permanently exempt those patterns in that file, including against a genuine future credential pasted there by accident, not only against this diff's own fixture strings. **Human ruling (this session): ship as-is; do NOT narrow these 3 entries in this diff** — the narrowing (scoping them to exact test-fixture line ranges) folds into the already-ratified Path B follow-on (`docs/backlog.md`), a separate STANDARD-tier story, not a `cifix` blocker. `docs/backlog.md` carries a dedicated line naming this exact task and citing Issue #136.

**Disclosure (cross-domain-reviewer's APPROVE-WITH-CONDITIONS condition, unchanged by the council round):** fixing Issue #113 lets CI reach `npm test`'s downstream steps for the first time in 8+ days, but **the very first real, human-triggered CI run will very likely still fail** — at QA-14 (`reference-resolver.ts`) and QA-15 (`completeness-claim-checker.ts`), not at `Checkout`, `npm test`, or OSS-01. Those two gates are separately, already tracked at **Issue #120** (OPEN, not this story's scope, human-ruled explicitly out of bounds — `src/qa/reference-resolver.ts`/`src/qa/completeness-claim-checker.ts`'s own logic untouched by this diff). This is not a regression this diff introduces; it is the next, already-named blocker in the same outcome chain Issue #27 started.
- **`ADR_REPO_PAT` provisioning is still a human-only action** (a PAT value must never appear in any agent transcript) — see this story's own PR description for the exact steps (URL, scope, 90-day expiration, `gh secret set` command). `docs/backlog.md`'s rotation-reminder entry (90 days, no automation yet) is unchanged.
- **Build task #1 (council GO condition 4, gating verification): the real `SECRET_PATTERNS`/`partitionAllowlisted` from `src/secret-scan/history-scan.ts`, imported live, run against a `git commit-tree` simulation of this diff staged for commit — `ok=true`.** Raw output and the exact allowlist entries added are in this session's own build receipt (not hand-simulated, not read off a prior round's stale count).
- **Manager's own final pre-commit re-run (session resumed after this build was interrupted mid-turn) found ONE more instance of the same recursive class**, live: this file's round-3 close-out drove one more entry into `docs/qa/secret-scan-allowlist.json` (the `docs/reviews/cifix-infra-security-round3-2026-09-09.md` × `email-address` entry, added to allowlist an Issue #132 fake-token lab reproduction line) — but that new entry's own `reason` text QUOTES the fake-token string, which is itself `email-address`-shaped, and `docs/qa/secret-scan-allowlist.json` had no entry allowlisting itself for `email-address` (only for `internal-hostname`, added earlier this story for the same self-referential reason). A stale prior snapshot's drill result would have read `ok=true` despite this, because the drill script reads the allowlist from disk (freshest) but scans a fixed git ref (potentially stale) — the two can silently diverge. ### Added — S6: policy centralization (POL-06/07/08/09/10, T10, Milestone #24)
- CRITICAL tier. Ratified plan v2: `docs/plans/S6-phase1-v2-2026-09-08.md`; pre-build reviews: `docs/reviews/s6-policy-centralization-{design-challenger,architecture}-2026-09-08.md` (verdicts `go` / APPROVE-WITH-CONDITIONS); build-completion ruling: `docs/decisions.md`'s 2026-09-08 "S6 Phase 2 build complete" row.
- **Real, three-tier policy loader** (`src/policy/config/loader.ts`, `loadEffectivePolicy()`): shipped-defaults (`src/policy/config/shipped-defaults.json`) → central (`HKLM\SOFTWARE\Policies\Thoth\CentralPolicyJson`, read via `reg query`) → project (`.thoth/policy.json`), all real files/channels, no in-memory-only fixtures. Central channel read EXACTLY ONCE per invocation (design-challenger Attack F) — the same bytes feed both the merge and POL-09's pin, closing the TOCTOU risk structurally, not by convention.
- **`src/policy/config/central-source.ts`**: `CentralPolicySource` — a discriminated union (`"absent" | "unsupported" | "present"`, architecture-reviewer findings 2/4) over the Windows registry channel, injectable per SE ADR-0003. First subprocess-shelling code in the policy layer — bounded timeout, output-size cap, argv passed directly (no shell interpolation). Its stdout-extraction routine is tested against genuinely real, captured `reg.exe query` output (see the file's own header for the full transcripts) — a material, positive update over the pre-build plan's disclosed "no agent session can run `reg`" blind spot, discovered live during this build.
- **`src/policy/config/position-parser.ts`**: a minimal, dependency-free JSON tokenizer tracking line/column from a single linear scan of raw source text — never from an unescaped copy, closing design-challenger's Attack E (CRLF, embedded escaped-newline, and non-ASCII/UTF-16-surrogate-pair edge cases each have a named test).
- **`src/policy/rule/precedence.ts`** gained `mergeLayersWithMandatoryLock` (POL-07): the general any-layer-locks-later-layers mandatory-key lock, composed on top of the existing `mergeLayersById` core plus a validation pass (architecture-reviewer finding 1) — never a second hand-rolled merge loop. Rejection is whole-LAYER granularity (design-challenger Attack D). General by construction, not by convention: the check inspects the rule's own `mandatory` field, never which layer produced it — closing design-challenger's Attack C with a same-session regression test for the shipped-defaults→central direction, not only the ratified central→project one. `mergeLayers` itself is untouched (same signature/behavior/tests) but now disclosed, in its own doc comment, as post-S6 production-dead — kept as a tested reference shape only.
- **`src/policy/config/pin.ts`** (POL-09, pinning half): SHA-256 content-hash pin, computed only from bytes the caller already read — no `CentralPolicySource` dependency at all, enforcing the single-read invariant at the type level. Absent/unsupported central states get distinct sentinel digests, never the digest of an empty string.
- **`src/policy/config/printer.ts` + `print-cli.ts`** (POL-10, `npm run policy:print`): "why is this blocked" in one command — origin file/channel and line number per resolved rule, `mandatory` shown on every line. Built to `test-writer`'s RED-CONFIRMED `printer.test.ts` contract unmodified (`docs/reviews/s6-policy-printer-test-writer-2026-09-08.md`) — printer.ts never throws; three named fail-closed reason kinds (`json-parse-error`, `schema-invalid`, `read-error`), each independently tested (AC5a/b/c).
- `src/policy/kernel/rule-types.ts` (`Rule.mandatory?: boolean`) and `src/policy/rule/schema.ts` (validates the new key) edited in place — pure data, confirmed via `qa:kernel-purity` to not trip the kernel purity scanner.
- **T10 (policy delivery format) formally re-answered and closed** in `REQUIREMENTS.md`, in place — see that file's own T10 row for the real, current answer (this repo's AGT-era 2026-08-26 answer was already void per the 2026-08-30 pivot; T10 sat reopened until this story).
- `hooks/pretooluse-kernel-gate.mjs` is explicitly NOT rewired to consume this loader in S6 (Q2's ruling) — stays a separate call; PreToolUse activation remains deferred exactly as S5 left it.
- `docs/backlog.md`: three new lines (hook-wiring subprocess-latency/cache-reuse target; admin-provisioning runbook gap, updated with this session's real write-classifier-block finding; the registry-value single-line-data assumption).
- **Known pre-existing gate conditions, confirmed via `git stash` against the unmodified prior commit, NOT caused by this diff**: `oss:secret-scan`'s dogfood run (internal-hostname matches in pre-S6 files, tracked at GitHub Issue #113); `qa:completeness-claims` (pre-existing `docs/decisions.md` rows, none touched here); `qa:reference-resolver` (disclosed sandboxed-network-access limitation, same as S4).
- **Correction (Stage-3 round-1, 2026-09-08):** the line above previously also claimed `npm run lint` failed on a pre-existing unnecessary-type-assertion error inside `printer.test.ts:399` — both `red-team` and `cross-domain-reviewer` independently re-ran lint clean (exit 0) against the same file; the claim did not reproduce (either the underlying issue was already fixed before that prose was written, or the original claim was itself imprecise — no matching eslint rule is even configured). Struck here rather than left stale; `printer.test.ts` itself was never edited by `story-implementer` at any point.

### Fixed — S6 Stage-3 round-1 review fix-now (Issues #105–#113, Milestone #24)
CRITICAL tier, same round, same files already touched (`central-source.ts`, `loader.ts`, `precedence.ts`, `schema.ts`, `printer.ts`, `print-cli.ts`). Findings from `docs/reviews/s6-policy-centralization-{red-team,cross-domain}-2026-09-08.md`; triage ratified in `docs/decisions.md`'s 2026-09-08 "Stage-3 review round 1" row.
- **[HIGH, Issue #106]** `central-source.ts` invoked `reg.exe` by bare name, resolved through `PATH` (`npm run` prepends `node_modules/.bin` ahead of `System32`) — a planted binary could silently answer for the central policy channel. Fixed: `resolveSystemRegExePath()` resolves an ABSOLUTE path via `%SystemRoot%\System32\reg.exe` (falling back to `%windir%`, never a hardcoded drive letter) at call time; never a bare/PATH-resolvable name.
- **[MED, Issue #108]** A project-layer mandatory-id collision previously voided the ENTIRE 3-tier load — the least-trusted layer could suppress the most-trusted layer's mandatory rules too. Fixed: `mergeLayersWithMandatoryLock` now voids ONLY the offending layer's own contribution (matching the already-ratified AC9 "whole-project-layer reject" text, not whole-load reject); every other layer, including the mandatory rule the collision targeted, still resolves. `LoadSuccess`/`PrinterResult` carry the voided-layer info so the printer names the OFFENDING layer directly, never misattributing it to "central policy" when central was innocent or absent.
- **[MED, Issue #105 + red-team's extension]** `validateRuleSet()` accepted a duplicate rule id within one layer, and a duplicate top-level `"rules"` key, with zero errors — `printer.ts`'s `findIndex()`-based origin-line lookup then reported the wrong line for the winning rule. Fixed: both shapes rejected explicitly, by name — duplicate rule ids via a direct scan of the parsed `rules` array; a duplicate top-level key via a new purpose-built raw-text scanner (`findDuplicateTopLevelKeys`, since `JSON.parse` already collapses duplicate keys before validation ever sees them).
- **[MED, Issue #110]** *(corrected 2026-09-08 — this line originally cited #111; that's Issue #111's own subject, "test-writer's answer key printer.test.ts modified after build," a different finding. #110 is the pin.)* POL-09's pin was computed in `loader.ts` but never surfaced — not printed, not stamped on any artifact. Fixed: surfaced via new `PrinterResult.pin`/`.disclosure` fields, printed by `print-cli.ts` as trailing lines. Deliberately NOT appended into `printer.ts`'s own `stdout` string: that string is `test-writer`'s locked, exact-match answer key (`printer.test.ts`) — appending there would have broken it. `printer.test.ts` itself was not touched; its 9 tests still pass unmodified.
- **[MED, Issue #107] — partial, deviation flagged.** `absent`-state detection matched only one English `reg.exe` stderr string. The fix-now triage's own suggested mechanism ("use reg.exe's exit code as the PRIMARY, locale-independent absent signal") was measured this session against a real `reg.exe` (not-found/access-denied/invalid-syntax all return exit code 1 — zero discriminating power) and against published documentation — implementing it as literally specified would have misclassified access-denied as absent, a fail-open regression. Built instead: an extensible pattern array (still English-only — no verified translated sample exists to add without violating PRINCIPLES rule 18) plus exit-code-1 required as a NECESSARY-but-not-sufficient precondition. Full reasoning in `central-source.ts`'s own header and `docs/backlog.md`'s matching entry; the durable fix (a locale-independent existence check, e.g. PowerShell's `Test-Path`) is named there, not built here.
- **[LOW]** *(corrected 2026-09-08 — this line originally cited #112; that's Issue #112's own subject, `defaultOutcome`, a different finding. The stderr-leak LOW finding was never filed as its own Issue — this project's convention files only HIGH/MED findings.)* `execFileSync`'s default `stdio` forwarded `reg.exe`'s stderr straight to the parent process on every call. Fixed: `stdio: ["ignore", "pipe", "pipe"]` — stderr still captured (unchanged behavior for the caller), never leaked to the parent's own stream. Confirmed via a real `npm run policy:print` run (`2>&1 1>/dev/null` now prints nothing).
- **[MED, Issue #109, disclosure-only]** `printer.ts`'s output carried no marker that it reflects S6's own resolved policy, not necessarily what `hooks/pretooluse-kernel-gate.mjs` enforces live (that hook stays on its separate `loadBootstrapRuleSet()` path this story, unchanged). Fixed: `ENFORCEMENT_DISCLOSURE`, surfaced the same way as the pin above (new `PrinterResult.disclosure` field, printed by `print-cli.ts` — not appended to the locked `stdout` string).
- **[MED, Issue #113]** `npm test` red at HEAD (pre-existing OSS-01 dogfood false positive, confirmed via `git stash`, unrelated to S6) — already tracked, GitHub Issue #113 (filed same-day, before this round; no new issue needed).
- **Deferred to `docs/backlog.md`, named, not fixed this round:** `bootstrap-ruleset.ts`'s `defaultOutcome` code literal (Issue #112, *corrected 2026-09-08 — originally cited as #110, which is the pin finding above*, pre-existing since S2); the mandatory-lock's id-vs-effect asymmetry (a project `deny` with a different id can still shadow a mandatory `allow`, red-team finding 11, LOW) — also documented as a code comment beside `mergeLayersWithMandatoryLock` in `precedence.ts`.
- Test counts (measured, not hand-derived): `git diff` shows +11 `test(` cases in `schema.test.ts` and +9 in `precedence.test.ts` (both tracked); `central-source.test.ts`/`loader.test.ts` (untracked — no git baseline this round, see Issue #7 of `red-team`'s report) now carry 16/9 `test(` cases respectively (story-implementer's own files throughout — none of `test-writer`'s `printer.test.ts` touched, re-confirmed 9/9 passing unmodified). Full suite: 617 passed, 1 failed (Issue #113, pre-existing, unrelated), 0 skipped. `typecheck`/`lint` clean.

### Fixed — S6 Stage-3 round-3 review fix-now (Issues #114/#115, Milestone #24, council-ratified Path B)
CRITICAL tier. Root cause + ruling: `docs/decisions.md`'s 2026-09-08 "council convened on S6's mandatory-lock trust-model flaw" row and the "Stage-3 round 3" row directly below it. Reports: `docs/reviews/s6-policy-centralization-{design-challenger-council-stopbrief,council-trust-model-architecture,council-impact-analyst,red-team-round2}-2026-09-08.md`.
- **[HIGH, Issue #114]** `mergeLayersWithMandatoryLock` (`precedence.ts`) used POL-08's *precedence* order (shipped-defaults → central → project) as a stand-in for *trust* order — a git-tracked, session-writable `shipped-defaults.json` rule could void the entire out-of-repo `central` layer via one mandatory-id collision, exit 0, unlocking every other central mandatory rule too. Fixed: a real, exported `TRUST_RANK` table (`central: 1`; `"shipped-defaults"`/`project`: `0`, PEERS) — a lock now voids a colliding layer only when the declaring layer's rank is strictly greater than the colliding layer's own rank. Structural consequence: central can never be voided by anything; the only collision the mechanism can ever void, given the fixed walk order, is central's own lock against project. A `mandatory: true` declaration with no real locking force (today: any shipped-defaults/project declaration) is never silently dropped — a new `InertMandatoryDeclaration[]` is threaded through `MandatoryLockResult` → `LoadSuccess` → `PrinterResult` → `print-cli.ts`'s new trailing `NOTE:` lines (the same "field, not the locked `stdout` string" pattern `pin`/`disclosure` already use). `printer.test.ts` (test-writer's locked answer key) needed no edit — its one `mandatory: true` fixture rule has no id collision in the fixture set, so it never reaches the changed branch; re-confirmed passing, unmodified. `precedence.test.ts:219`/`:236` — pinned as correct by round 2's own report, and re-confirmed still green by the council's own independent test run — are REPLACED with corrected assertions (this ratifying row is the "not a silent edit" disclosure PRINCIPLES rule 11 requires); two further tests added directly covering the peer-vs-peer sub-case and the "central still locks project after a harmless lower-trust collision" chained case.
- **[MED, Issue #115]** `findDuplicateTopLevelKeys` (`schema.ts`) compared raw, still-escaped key text while `JSON.parse` compares unescaped values — a `\uXXXX`-escaped duplicate `"rules"` key bypassed the check and re-opened Issue #105's wrong-origin-line defect. Fixed: each captured key is now unescaped via `JSON.parse`'s own decoder before counting (delegating to the authoritative parser rather than hand-rolling a second unescaper — the same mistake round 1's fix made), plus `validateRuleSet` separately asserts a general tokenizer/parser-agreement invariant (the raw-text scanner's own unescaped top-level key set must equal `Object.keys(JSON.parse(text))`) — not a blocklist entry for one more escape shape.
- **Build task #1 (council GO condition 4):** `src/policy/rule/mandatory-lock-conformance.test.ts` (new file) — a real, CI-gating layer-pair conformance instrument, discoverable by name for future regressions. Self-consistency block derived directly from the exported `TRUST_RANK` constant (never a hand-copied second table); a hand-written ground-truth block independent of `TRUST_RANK`'s own values; same-layer-duplicate-id handling per named layer; central's un-voidability checked across every mandatory-flag combination on one shared collision. Answers this project's own "no hand-derived completeness claims" rule and this bug family's recurrence (Issues #65/#66/#99/#114/#115) with a standing instrument rather than another one-off patch.
- Housekeeping: a stray, untracked two-line placeholder file (`docs/reviews/_probe.md`) left over from an earlier report-authoring step was removed — not a real report, not part of this round's own findings.

### Fixed — S6 post-ship residual fix-now round, Issue #110 (Milestone #24)
CRITICAL tier (inherited, `docs/.maat-state.json`). Disclosed, non-blocking residual named at Stage-3 round-4 close ("Two small non-gating residuals surfaced and deferred") — Issue #110's remaining open half, distinct from the round-1 "pin was computed but never surfaced" fix already landed above under the same issue number.
- **[disclosed residual, closed]** `pin.ts`'s `computePin` hashed ONLY the central channel's raw bytes — two effective policies differing solely in the shipped-defaults or project layer shared one digest, exactly the gap REQUIREMENTS.md's own POL-09 text warns against closing on the delivery half alone. Fixed: `ComputePinInput` gains REQUIRED (never optional/defaulted) `shippedRaw`/`projectRaw` fields; the digest now covers all three layers via length-prefixed, labeled hash framing (`"<label>:<byte-length>:" + <content>`, fixed order shipped-defaults → central → project) so no combination of layer contents can collide with a different combination at a concatenation boundary. `loader.ts`'s one call site passes the already-in-hand `shippedText`/`projectText` — zero new file reads, single-read invariant (design-challenger Attack F) unchanged.
- New regression tests: `pin.test.ts` (changed-shipped-bytes / changed-project-bytes / boundary-collision cases) and `loader.test.ts` (two end-to-end "differ only in one layer -> different digest" cases, an identical-content reproducibility case, and a boundary-collision case at the loader level). The pre-existing single-read AC2 test's manual rehash was updated to call `computePin` directly (with the real on-disk shipped/project bytes) rather than re-implementing the hash by hand.
- Landed as its own commit, separate from Issue #108's fix below (SE ADR-0006, no-lockstep: each fix is independently reviewable and revertible).

### Fixed — S6 Stage-3 round-4 residual fix-now, Issue #108 REOPENED (Milestone #24)
CRITICAL tier (inherited). Trigger: `red-team` round 4 REOPENED this issue (message-clarity-only defect, fails closed either way, pinned by test-writer's own locked `printer.test.ts` — needed a test-writer amendment before it could be unlocked). `test-writer`'s amendment RED-CONFIRMED: `docs/reviews/s6-printer-test-writer-fixnow-2026-09-08.md` (9 pre-existing tests pass unchanged, 2 new `ISSUE-108(a)`/`(b)` tests fail cleanly on the layer-naming assertion before this fix, 0 unexpectedly passing). Manager ruling on the one flagged design fork: BOM handling = reject with the correct layer named, no BOM-tolerance/stripping logic added (matches the default already planned — no plan change needed).
- **[MED, Issue #108]** `printer.ts`'s `renderRejection` hardcoded the literal prefix `REJECTED: central policy load failed (...)` for ALL THREE reason kinds, regardless of which layer (central / shipped-defaults / project) actually failed — a Windows operator saving `.thoth/policy.json` with a UTF-8 BOM (PowerShell 5.1 `Out-File -Encoding utf8` / Notepad "UTF-8 with BOM") saw the PROJECT layer's own fault misattributed to "central policy load failed," even with central absent and wholly uninvolved. Fixed: `LoadFailure` gains a required `failedLayer: "central" | "shipped-defaults" | "project"` field, set on every one of `loadEffectivePolicy`'s failure return sites (central read-error, central parse-failure, shipped-defaults parse-failure, project parse-failure); `printer.ts`'s `renderRejection` now names that exact layer instead of a hardcoded default.
- **Additional, same-fix hardening (not a new Issue — completes the fix's own stated scope):** the shipped-defaults/project `readFileSync` calls in `loader.ts` were previously unwrapped — a missing/unreadable file threw an uncaught exception that propagated past every layer-aware failure site, landing in `printer.ts`'s own last-resort `catch`, which had no way to recover which layer (or central's own already-known, innocent status) was actually involved. Both calls are now wrapped in try/catch, returning a typed `LoadFailure` (`reasonKind: "read-error"`, correct `failedLayer`, central's status/channel preserved) instead of throwing.
- No BOM-tolerance/stripping logic added (Manager ruling, this round) — a BOM'd file is rejected, with the correct layer named; `loader.ts` still never strips a BOM (`readFileSync(path, "utf8")` feeds raw bytes straight into `JSON.parse`), confirmed unchanged by this fix.
- New regression tests: two black-box cases in `test-writer`'s own `printer.test.ts` (`ISSUE-108(a)` project-layer BOM repro, `ISSUE-108(b)` symmetric shipped-defaults-layer case — both now GREEN, unmodified from test-writer's landed amendment); five white-box cases in `loader.ts`'s own `loader.test.ts` (central/shipped-defaults/project parse-failure `failedLayer` assertions on three pre-existing tests, plus two new missing-file `readFileSync`-wrapping cases).
- `printer.test.ts` itself was not touched by `story-implementer` at any point in this round — the file `test-writer` amended and RED-CONFIRMED is the exact file this fix was built against, unmodified.
- Landed as its own commit, after Issue #110's fix above (SE ADR-0006, no-lockstep).
- Verification: `npm run typecheck`/`lint` both clean; `node --test` full suite — the only failure is the same pre-existing `OSS-01` dogfood case (Issue #113, unrelated), 0 skipped, every other new test (schema/precedence/loader/conformance) green; `qa:kernel-purity`/`qa:normalizer-registry-purity`/`qa:gate-command-path`/`qa:gate-matcher-drift`/`qa:gate-manifest`/`qa:gate-latency-budget` PASS; `qa:completeness-claims`/`qa:reference-resolver` fail on the same pre-existing rows/citations as every prior S6 round, none touched or added by this diff (re-confirmed directly, not assumed); a real `npm run policy:print` run against this machine's actual registry state produced a real pin and disclosure line. `red-team` re-confirm against this landed diff is next; `story-implementer` posts its own independent verification as GitHub comments on Issues #114/#115 without closing either (closure stays `red-team`'s call, per this project's own convention).

### Fixed — S6 Stage-3 round-3 RE-CONFIRM fix-now (Issues #118/#119, Milestone #24, both `red-team`-demonstrated MED, neither gating)
CRITICAL tier, same round, same-files-already-touched (`mandatory-lock-conformance.test.ts`, `schema.ts`, `position-parser.ts`). Findings from `docs/reviews/s6-policy-centralization-red-team-round3-2026-09-08.md` (verdict `go` — both are assurance-layer defects, not enforcement-path defects; converted to named failing tests per PRINCIPLES rule 19, not conditions).
- **[MED, Issue #118]** `mandatory-lock-conformance.test.ts`'s self-check test asserted a HARD-CODED forward-pair count (3) and a hard-coded three-name pair list — so its own header's "a future 4th layer cannot silently leave one cell unverified" claim was false as shipped: red-team added a 4th layer to `LayerName`/`TRUST_RANK` and the file's whole test suite stayed green while covering half the forward pairs, with Part B's "central can NEVER be voided" assertion silently narrowing to only the pairs it knew about. Fixed, per red-team's own suggested proof-test: the self-check now asserts `new Set(PRECEDENCE_ORDER)` equals `new Set(Object.keys(TRUST_RANK))`, and the forward-pair count against `n(n-1)/2` for `n = Object.keys(TRUST_RANK).length` — both derived from the SAME source of truth Part A's per-pair expectations already use, never a second hand-typed number. A future layer added to `TRUST_RANK` without updating `PRECEDENCE_ORDER` now turns this file red instead of silently covering half the matrix.
- **[MED, Issue #119]** The #115 fix's tokenizer/parser-agreement invariant was applied to `schema.ts`'s duplicate-key scanner but not to `position-parser.ts:195`'s own top-level-`"rules"`-key detection, which still compared raw, still-escaped text (`unquoteSimple(prevKey.raw) === "rules"`) — the same "competing, locally-reimplemented notion of key identity" shape Issue #115 was fixed for one file over. A document with a SINGLE unicode-escaped `"rules"` key (no duplicate, so `schema.ts`'s checks never fire and the layer is accepted) passed schema validation, then `findRulePositions` silently returned `[]`, so `loader.ts` mapped every rule's origin line to the `-1` sentinel — POL-10's diagnostic field degrading to "no answer" on a document `JSON.parse` reads correctly (confirmed harmless downstream: no id/effect/layer/origin-file was ever wrong, nothing treats `-1` as a real line). Fixed: `schema.ts`'s existing `unescapeJsonStringLiteral` (the SAME decoder `findTopLevelKeys` already delegates to) is now exported and used by `position-parser.ts`'s own key comparison — one shared notion of "what does this key token actually say," never a second unescaper. Import runs config → rule, the same direction `loader.ts` already imports in.
- New regression tests: `mandatory-lock-conformance.test.ts`'s self-check test rewritten in place (Issue #118); `position-parser.test.ts` gained a dedicated unicode-escaped-`"rules"`-key case; `loader.test.ts` gained an end-to-end escaped-key case (correct origin line, not `-1`) plus two `-1`-sentinel backstop tests (plain-ASCII and escaped-key cases) — all in story-implementer's own unit-test files, never `printer.test.ts` (test-writer's locked answer key, untouched, `git diff --stat` confirmed empty).
- Verification: `npm run typecheck`/`lint` both clean; full suite 642 tests, 641 pass, 1 fail (0 skipped, 0 todo) — the 1 failure is the same pre-existing `OSS-01` dogfood case (Issue #113, unrelated, unchanged); targeted run of the four touched test files (`mandatory-lock-conformance.test.ts`, `position-parser.test.ts`, `loader.test.ts`, `schema.test.ts`) — 65/65 pass, 0 fail, 0 skipped.

### Fixed — S6 round-5 re-confirm fix-now, Issue #122 (Milestone #24, `red-team`-demonstrated MED, neither gating)
CRITICAL tier (inherited, `src/policy/config/` sensitive area). Finding from `docs/reviews/s6-policy-centralization-red-team-round5-2026-09-08.md` (finding 1, verdict `go` — a regression-guard defect, not a shipped-behavior defect; converted to a named failing test per PRINCIPLES rule 19).
- **[MED, Issue #122]** `pin.test.ts:31` and `loader.test.ts:609`'s "no concatenation-boundary collision" tests both picked the `(shipped, project)` layer split, which is NON-ADJACENT in the fixed hash order (shipped-defaults → central → project) — the central frame always separates them, so the assertion held even with the length-prefix framing entirely deleted (red-team's M3 mutant: `updateLabeledFrame` reduced to a bare `hash.update(content)`, config suite stayed 43/43 green). The two boundaries that actually matter, `shipped|central` and `central|project`, had no test. Fixed: a new named test in each file — **"Issue #122: neither ADJACENT frame boundary (shipped|central, central|project) admits a collision"** — asserting distinct digests for `(shipped="ab", central="c", project="")` vs `(shipped="a", central="bc", project="")`, and `(shipped="", central="c", project="ab")` vs `(shipped="", central="cab", project="")`. No production code changed — `pin.ts`'s shipped framing was already correct (red-team's own 21,141-input brute force found zero genuine collisions); this closes the regression-guard gap only.
- **Mutation-proved, not just asserted:** re-ran red-team's exact M3 mutant (framing prefix stripped to plain concatenation) against both new tests — both failed (`shippedCentralA.digest === shippedCentralB.digest`, the naive-concatenation collision the mutant reintroduces), config suite 43/45 pass / 2 fail, with every other test (including M1/M2/M4-catching ones) unaffected. Reverted the mutant (`git diff` against `pin.ts` empty, byte-identical to HEAD) and re-ran: 45/45 pass.
- Verification: `npm run typecheck`/`lint` both clean; full suite 658 tests, 657 pass, 1 fail (0 skipped, 0 todo) — the 1 failure is the same pre-existing `OSS-01` dogfood case (Issue #113, unrelated, unchanged, confirmed by line number match); config-only run (`pin.test.ts`+`loader.test.ts`+`printer.test.ts`) 46/46 pass, 0 fail, 0 skipped.
- Not closing Issue #122 — leaving that to `red-team`'s re-confirm, per this project's established convention (see Issues #118/#119's own closure comments).

### Fixed — S6 round-5 re-confirm fix-now, Issue #123 (Milestone #24, `red-team`-demonstrated MED, neither gating)
CRITICAL tier (inherited, `src/policy/config/` sensitive area). Finding from `docs/reviews/s6-policy-centralization-red-team-round5-2026-09-08.md` (finding 2, verdict `go` — a test-contract artifact, not a shipped-behavior defect).
- **[MED, Issue #123]** `printer.test.ts`'s locked `buildExpectedRejectionStdout` asserted a fail-closed rejection is EXACTLY 2 lines — true only because the `ISSUE-108(a)` BOM fixture happens to be single-line minified JSON. The identical 3-byte `EF BB BF` BOM on this repo's own pretty-printed `printer-project.json` fixture (the realistic PowerShell 5.1/Notepad shape) produces 3 lines (`JSON.parse`'s own error snippet embeds a newline from the pretty-printed source) — the old `=== 2` contract would have failed on that real-world shape even though the substantive guarantees (exit 1, correct layer named, `doesNotMatch(/rule id=/)`) all held. `test-writer` (owner of this locked file) relaxed the assertion to `>= 2` and moved prefix/message matching onto the rejoined tail, added a new `ISSUE-108(c)` test against a new pretty-printed BOM fixture (`docs/qa/s6-policy-loader-fixtures/printer-project-bom-pretty-malformed.json`), and verified the regression is real (not incidentally green) by running the fixture through the real printer outside the test file first: `exitCode=1`, 3 lines, `=== 2` false under the old contract, green under the revised one. No production code changed — `printer.ts` was already correct; this was a test-contract artifact of one fixture's shape.
